Radio Waves

by Tedioustyping10

Summary

“There’s more to driving than just keeping the car on track,” Lando says. “Sometimes we know things you don't.”

“Like what?” Oscar asks, as if he can’t actually believe there might be something Lando knows that he doesn’t

“Like how the track feels or the tyres or if the suspension starts to feel off?” Lando lists off. “Or we can see what some of the other cars are doing that the cameras don’t pick up. We can help make decisions that you can’t see. Because we feel them. Before your little computer does.”

“If that’s true,” Oscar starts, drawing out the words in that stupid Australian accent of his. “Then why are you always asking Will for help on the radio?”

Lando’s actually speechless for a moment.

Fuck this guy, Lando thinks, glaring at Oscar.

----

Lando's the reigning world champion. Oscar's his new race engineer. They do not work together well. But they have one goal. Make Lando a back-to-back World Champion.

Cue teambuilding, radio fights, Lando and Daniel fighting for the title and Oscar and Lando learning to be a team.

Thank you so much for taking the time to read! I hope you all enjoy reading this version of Lando and Oscar.

Chapter 1: Winter Break Blues

Chapter Summary

Oscar gets a promotion. Lando gets a new race engineer.

Chapter Notes

See the end of the chapter for notes

It’s a promotion.

 

At least, that’s what they tell him. That Will got promoted. And Oscar is only the natural fit to take his place. 

 

It sounds nice. Sitting in his boss's office, looking at a promotion letter with a significant raise. Getting a hard clap on his back from Zak. A gentle, encouraging smile from Andrea next to him. 

 

But.

 

It feels like more of being passed off. Like more of a shifting than a promotion. Oscar doesn’t want to use the word punishment. But it’s not completely the wrong word. Feels like someone on his side of the garage wants him moved. 

 

He doesn’t have to look very hard to know who.

 

In the beginning, he and Daniel got along great. Their shared Australian kinship made them feel somewhat connected in a sea of Brits. Oscar was far too young and awkward, still is too young and awkward, but Daniel always made the move to break the ice. Joking with Oscar about how no one here used the word thongs in the right way, or bringing Timtams to their debriefs. And Oscar has felt a sort of kinship with him. Even bringing his nan’s lambingtons to hospitality while they were in Australia, stashing one away especially for Daniel. 

 

But as the season progressed, it seemed like their shared home country was where their commonality ended. Where Daniel was loud and showy, Oscar was quiet and calm. Where Daniel took risks, Oscar presented sound data. Where Daniel was a seasoned veteran on the track, Oscar was a new face on the pit wall. 

 

And when Daniel’s performance started slipping, Oscar was where he placed his blame. 

 

Suddenly, Oscar wasn’t his fellow Aussie, but his downfall. All of Oscar’s data and advice went out the window. Every time he spoke on the radio, he was met with silence or full, unbidden anger. In debriefs, they shared short, clipped conversations when they didn’t fall into full-blown arguments. 

 

All of it made worse by watching his teammate cross the line in Abu Dhabi as a newly made world champion. 

The sound of the team cheering as Lando took it all was deafening. 

 

It was a moment to be proud of. Something McLaren had worked hard for. And Oscar was proud. Of the team. Of even Lando. 

 

But he was envious. Of Will. Watching as he was clapped on the back by their bosses. As he started to cry tears of joy. As he congratulated Lando on the radio with unrestrained pride. As he watched Lando do donuts on the track, grinning even when he’d told him not to. 

 

As Lando thanked him, voice shaky and full of tears, so grateful and disbelieving. 

 

Meanwhile, Oscar had hoped that the sound of celebration wasn’t too loud as he’d clicked on Daniel’s radio. 

 

“Good job, mate,” Oscar said evenly, trying to keep his message short and direct. “That’s, uh, P5. And the Constructor’s for us as well.” 

 

There had been silence for a moment. Fireworks boomed in the air. So beautiful, and yet they made Oscar want to hunch in on himself. He kept his eyes forward on his screens. 

 

He heard Daniel’s radio turn on, static buzzing in his headphones. 

 

“And Lando?” Daniel asked, voice rough and heated. “He beat Max?” 

 

Oscar blanched a bit. “Yeah, he did.”

 

Static. Static. Static.

 

Oscar bit his lip. “He’s the champion.”

 

Daniel didn’t respond, and Oscar heard the stillness that meant his radio was off. From the onboard camera, Oscar could see his fists clenched against the wheel. 

 

He pressed the button for Daniel’s radio again. 

 

”Really good job this season though, mate,” Oscar tried, bringing in as much fake lightness and pride in his voice as he could muster. A monumental task at this moment. “We couldn't have done this without you. It’ll be you next year for sure.” 

 

A lie. Oscar knows Daniel is good. Know he probably does have what it takes to be a champion. But his temperament. The growing resentment towards the team, his teammate, and Oscar will never get him there. Oscar sighed to himself, fiddling with the cords of his headphones. 

 

He’d only been met with more silence. Oscar looked over to Zak and Andrea. They were beaming with joy as they hugged. It made Oscar feel a little ill. Normally, Oscar doesn’t feel so out of place. He knows he’s worked hard to be here and is good at his job, even as young as he is. But he wanted nothing more than to disappear in that moment. 

 

“Thanks, mate,” Daniel said finally, voice flat and simmering with contained anger. ”Where should I park?” 

 

Oscar took the chance to go off the topic at hand and walked Daniel through the ending procedure and led him to park beside a less disgruntled George Russel in parc ferme. 

 

They shook hands in the celebrations and the photos and gave tense smiles in the debrief, but Oscar knew Daniel was filled with rage. Furious that he’d left Redbull for this. That he’d been beaten by a younger teammate again. That talks of retirement were swirling the paddock. That Lando had been the one to do what Daniel wanted more than anything. 

 

Lando, who’d found Oscar in the crowd and smiled at him so brightly and sincerely. 

 

“Thank you, Oscar,” he’d said, warm and genuine, shaking Oscar’s hand. It was warm and sticky with champagne.“I know you’re not on my side, but we wouldn’t have the Constructor’s without you.”

 

”‘Course, mate,” Oscar said, a bit dumbfounded. A bit shocked that Lando was talking to him and that he was talking to the new world champion. His eyes were rimmed red by tears, and Oscar could see his mum hovering in the background, wiping away her own tears of joy. The sight was enough to move anyone. 

 

He offers Lando a smile that he hopes reads as sincere. “Congrats on the title, Lando. You deserve it.” 

 

Oscar wasn’t quite sure he believed it or that Lando believed him, but the way Lando’s eyes had scrunched up in delight and offered him a toothy grin made the words settle right in Oscar’s stomach. Even as he drank champagne straight from the bottle with the rest of the team and watched Lando’s side of the garage celebrate in delirious pride. Even as Daniel had slinked away after a few minutes of obligatory photos. Even as he wallowed in his hotel room later, feeling like Daniel’s disappointment was entirely his fault. 

 

“You’re smart as a whip,” Zak says, bringing Oscar back to the present. “You’ll have a lot to offer Lando.” 

 

Oscar’s not sure about that. Didn’t seem to help Daniel, who now refused to even look at Oscar in the hallways. 

 

And Lando. God, he wonders how he reacted to this news. Will has been with Lando since he started at McLaren. Had been by his side for 7 years now. 

 

“And Lando’s ok with this?” Oscar asks, raising his brow. Because he has to know. Has to know if he’s walking from one hostile environment to another. If Lando thinks Oscar is incompetent and inexperienced. If he shares Daniel’s opinion.

 

Andrea coughs and avoids his gaze. “Lando knows that this is a good opportunity for Will to gain more experience as a leader, and I need him with me at the top. He’s taken Lando to the title. Did what he needed to do. Now he has opportunities here for his own growth.”

 

”But,” Oscar starts. Unsure what to say. Unsure how to not sound like an idiot if he says well ok but I failed your prize horse, Danny Ric. 

 

“You are a valuable team member here, Oscar,” Zak reassures, that audacious American accent booming in the quiet of his own office. “What you bring to the table is unmatched. We think it’ll really help Lando this season.  I know you’re the best bet to replace Will and help Lando get another title under his belt.”

 

So, they’re not even backing Daniel then. Oscar isn’t surprised, but it still feels a bit odd to hear it straight from their CEO’s mouth.

 

“And Daniel?” He asks. 

 

“Daniel is bringing in an engineer from one of his old teams,” Andrea says, clearly a bit annoyed that he has to bring in outside talent. “But, he will be ok. Just focus this year on Lando and let’s work towards going back-to-back.” 

 

Oscar nods. It’s not like he has much choice. 

 

Seems like maybe Lando doesn’t have much of a choice either. He tries not to let his mind run wild, thinking of what that will mean for their partnership this season. 

 

Truthfully, Oscar’s always thought Lando was nice enough. But a bit annoying.

 

He’s sure some of this is colored by Danny’s opinion. It’s not like he kept quiet about it. Talking about Lando like his friend on Thursdays, but hating him by Sunday. 

 

It’s odd to say the least. 

 

But Oscar has also heard Lando in meetings and on the radio. For such a good driver, he seems a bit helpless at times. Always bothering Will for help on such an obvious thing and being moody and silent when things don’t go his way. 

 

Not to mention the way both his mechanics and Zak seem to coddle him. Even Andrea, who tries to stay subjective, clearly has some sort of fatherly soft spot for Lando. 

 

He isn’t sure if it’s because he’s been with them since the beginning or if it’s because he practically bleeds papaya. Always taking the blame on himself and never blaming the team, even when it’s clearly their fault. 

 

And the truth is, Oscar used to like Lando quite a lot as well. When Oscar was still driving, it felt like Lando was who he should aim to be. The kind of driver he should strive to be. One who excelled and was taken in by a good team at a young age. 

 

But once Oscar had left driving behind and gone to University, he’d felt a bit of envy towards Lando. Lando, who joined McLaren so young. Learning from Fernando Alonso, for God's sake, and clearly being Zak’s golden boy. Lando, who seemed to have it so easy, beloved by the paddock and media alike. Always wanting to be the center of attention. 

 

Even as Oscar had excelled and been poached in his own way by McLaren, it still felt like the work revolved around Lando Norris. That every part of the team’s success hinged on this…well, Oscar didn’t want to say boy, but he basically was. Almost acting like the team mascot. Like a little papaya Mickey Mouse. 

 

Still, Oscar knows it is a bit of a privilege to work for the reigning World Champion. To get to sit on the pit wall and know that he’s looking out for car #1. To have the task of helping their world champion try and win races. Try for another Constructor’s. Try to make Lando a two-time world champion. 

 

And to do it without the imminent cloud of rage that Daniel harbored over him, every time he called him into the pits, and it was the wrong timing. Or he tried to let him know where to brake, and it caused him to lock up. Or left him out too long and got overtaken by a Ferrari or Mercedes on newer tyres. 

 

Oscar takes a deep breath and resolves himself to at least give this new partnership a full, honest try. To bring his best work forward. To prove his worth here. If only to sate his own desire to prove that he is good at his job and is worthy of being a champion’s engineer. 

 

“Thank you,” Oscar says, forcing a smile onto his face for Zak and Andrea. “I appreciate the opportunity, and I’ll do my best to help Lando defend his title.” 

 

He shakes both their hands before quickly leaving the room, waiting for the haven of his own office. 

 

It can’t be that bad, he reasons in his head. Certainly, it can’t be worse than it was with Daniel. 

 

And he won’t fuck this up. Not like he did with Daniel. 

 

Can’t fuck this up. 

 

No.

 

Oscar’s going to make Lando Norris a two-time World Champion.

 

----

 

Lando doesn’t want to sound like… well. Like a brat. But he doesn’t want Will to leave. It’s not like Will’s his dad or anything, but sometimes it feels like they have some sort of brotherly bond. The sort of bond that is only made from 7 years of teamwork. Of being made Champions together. He sees Will more than his own brother anyway. 

 

Not to mention, even Lando can admit that he’s… a bit possessive. 

 

His therapist might trace it back to being one of four siblings. That he felt a nagging need to find something of his own in a house of kids all chasing something, all clamoring for their parents' love and affection. The need for some individual attention as the smallest of his siblings, right in the middle of the order. 

 

But Lando also doesn’t trust easily. Doesn’t feel like he has many people he can fully just be.. himself with. Just Lando. Not Lando Norris, McLaren driver. Or Lando Norris, World Champion. Someone who is allowed to have downs and bad moments without the entire world judging him. Someone who can be quiet and keep to themselves without expectation. 

 

Sure, he has people who he can have that with who aren’t Will. He still has his family, and Jon and Max. People who have truly seen him at his most beaten down. When he was feeling worthless. Feeling like everything he said or did was wrong. 

 

But, they couldn’t be in the car with him. They didn’t have a direct line into his ear once the visor went down. But Will was. And even when they snipped at each other or Lando disagreed with his notes or whatever, Lando still knew he could trust Will. That, at the end of the day, he cared for Lando. That he understood what Lando needed and that they were working towards the same thing. 

 

Lando’s not stupid. He knows people think Will babies him on the radio or some bullshit like that. That Lando can’t drive without being walked through the entire experience. That he needs his hand held every step of the way. But Lando didn’t need babying.

 

 Sometimes, he just needed someone in his ear who believed in him. In his driving. In his words. In his ability to make decisions behind the wheel. 

 

And Will did. 

 

And now Lando is the world champion. 

 

And McLaren has decided that instead of helping him to defend his title with a trusted engineer. They want to give him Oscar Piastri.

 

It’s not that Lando doesn’t like Oscar. 

 

He just doesn’t even think of him. 

 

His title fight had been with Max. Not Daniel. So it hadn’t mattered much what the other side of the garage was doing. Unless it was fucking up their chance to get the Constructor’s. Which had happened more than a few times. 

 

Though Lando wasn’t sure if it was the garage or the driver that had a part in that.  

 

Lando does like Daniel. Honestly. Thinks he’s funny and easy to be around most of the time. They don’t work together as well as he and Carlos did, but they make it work. Can have a good laugh or chat. 

 

He hopes they still can going into the new season. 

 

Even if Daniel is growing to resent the team. Resent him. 

 

When they’re off track, it still feels nice. 

 

But Lando’s aware that Daniel doesn’t like seeing Lando do better. Qaulify higher. Get podiums and wins. Grab the title. 

 

And Lando gets it. He wouldn’t want to see that either. Not when he’s been here longer. Been with McLaren as they struggled. But he thinks - hopes - he would be able to push past it. Be able to set aside his own disappointment and carry on. Still be able to laugh together. Still want to cheer for each other’s wins. 

 

He’s finding that not to be true for Daniel. He’s hardly seen him since the last race. Since he did his media and took team pictures and left. 

 

And apparently, made demands to Andrea. 

 

He’s not sure what’s the chicken or the egg. If Will’s promotion or Daniel’s demand for a new race engineer came first. 

 

But the consequences of either are here now. 

 

And Lando was given no choice but to accept Oscar as his new race engineer.

 

Oscar, who delivered radio messages in that dry, flat monotone. Who spat out data like it was nothing. Who looked at Lando like he was a child.

 

Which was crazy. Because Oscar was younger. Had been at McLaren for far less time than Lando. 

 

He didn’t know the whole story. But he knew that Oscar was some smart kid who’d been poached from university by Zak before he’d even finished. That’d he’d been hanging around the garage as an intern even when Lando was just getting used to being in F1. That he slotted into that pitwall seat like he owned it. 

 

Posh for sure. Definitely from a rich family, Lando concluded. 

 

Not in the way Lando was. Growing up in a good vibes, money is no issue, pursue your passions and artistic whims as you please type of way. 

 

No, it seems like Oscar came from a business, no-nonsense type of rich. Boarding school and Oxford-type shit. 

 

Smart, but smug. 

 

Polite, but arrogant.

 

Sure of himself and looking down at others. 

 

He’s sure Oscar likely thinks he’s a bit of a dumb athlete. A fool. A tool just there to put his data into practice.

 

 Undeserving of his title. 

 

“You’ve got to give him a chance, Bob,” Max says, looking at him. Lando huffs into his drink. Lando, who doesn’t even really like drinking outside of clubs, had reluctantly accepted Max’s offer of a beer. Anything to prolong their last night of freedom before Lando’s thrown back into the chaos of the season. 

 

He hardly tastes the beer as it slides down his throat. 

 

He’s been drinking a lot since winning. In clubs. At ceremonies. On yachts. Everyone and anyone wanting to treat the champion to celebratory champagne or doubles of premium liquor. He has a bottle of whiskey sitting in his kitchen from Zak. An expensive bottle of Italian wine from Andrea. 

 

He’d be more worried about his liver if it didn’t feel like such a headrush every time someone reminded him he was the Champion. That he’d done it. That all the stress and tears and anger had been worth it. 

 

Even if the glory is a bit short-lived. 

 

Jon already has him working and sweating each day. His diet plan is back in full force. Alcohol on the ban list until he podiums again. 

 

Still, he thinks he can stomach one last pint of shitty beer with his best friend before he’s thrown back into the season. 

 

Until he’s back in the car without Will. 

 

“It’s just that he’s so boring,” Lando bemoans, taking another sip of his drink. It’s cold, and he’s cold. He’d rather have a tea right now.  “Like I don’t think he’s ever laughed in his life if I'm being honest.” 

 

“You don’t even really know him,” Max points out. “Like, have you actually exchanged more than a few sentences with him?” 

 

“I know Daniel doesn’t like him,” Lando points out. 

 

Max rolls his eyes from where he’s sitting on the opposite end of his couch. 

 

“Daniel doesn’t like anything that isn’t Redbull,” he says. “Besides, I’ve heard some of his radios. It seems like Oscar’s had a bit of a rough go. Like he’s giving good info, and Daniel doesn’t listen at all.” 

 

“It’s his job, ain’t it?” Lando says, he sets down his drink on the coffee table and burrows further into the nest of blankets Max’s girlfriend has draped over the couch.  “Sometimes we disagree with our engineers. I don’t always agree with Will. But it’s give and take.”

 

“Sure, but that doesn’t mean you wanna be shouted at for two hours,” Max says, shrugging. His dog comes padding into the room, hopping up onto the couch and patting around until he settles at Lando’s feet. He revels in the much needed warmth and reaches down to stroke his fur. His chest feels a little open and hollow at the action. 

 

God, he wants a dog. 

 

“But, what if he’s not what I need?” he asks, focusing on where his fingers smooth out long, golden hair, instead of looking at Max. “What if I end up shouting on the radio? What if we don’t get on?”  

 

“Well, you’re not Verstappen, mate,” Max says, tapping at his own glass. “You don’t need Will like you did before. Right? You needed him to help you get the title, and you’ve got it.”

 

“We just worked together well,” Lando says. “He understands that sometimes I just need to know I’m doing well. That I have the capability to do what I need to do.” 

 

“Yeah, but you’re a different man now,” Max says simply. “You’ve proven you can be a champion. That you’re capable of great drives, mate. I’ve seen how much more confident you are. How much freer you look.” 

 

Well, Lando can’t argue that. It has felt like his shoulders are lighter than ever. That he’d managed to do what he needed to. But there was still something lingering in the back of his mind. 

 

“I just,” Lando starts, chewing on the words for a moment. “I can’t fight like Max again. It went against everything I know. Feels like it almost killed me. I don’t know if I can do that again.” 

 

“No one’s saying you have to,” Max offers. 

 

“I know,” Lando says. “It’s just… if I can do it again, then I want to do it my way.”

 

“You did do it your way,” Max emphasizes, and Lando can’t bear how he looks at him. All soft and concerned and everything Lando wants to avoid sometimes. Something that feels akin to pity. 

 

“I did, but I still tried to copy Max in the end,” Lando says. “Still had to try and pull dirty moves and get under his skin. Which is stupid because all it ended up doing was have me in my own head. I want to do it without any of the bullshit. Just clean, honest racing.”

 

Max smiles at him. He looks at him like Lando’s some long-lost relative come back from a long time away. It makes him feel odd. 

 

“Well, you’ve proven that you can do it once,” Max says. “Do it your way this time around. And maybe Oscar’s approach will help with that.” 

 

“I just don’t see how,” Lando sighs. “I’m not used to working with anyone else. With someone who doesn’t know me. And he’s so cold and emotionless.” 

 

“It’ll be different from Will,” Max agrees. “But defending a title will also be different. Different isn’t always a bad thing. Maybe you need a bit more logic to balance out your emotional side.”

 

Lando despises how that makes so much sense. 

 

“Hm, when you’d get so fucking smart?” Lando bites out, making Max chuckle. 

 

“When I had to make sure your stupid ass ate and didn’t have a mental breakdown last season,” Max says, rolling his eyes. “I’m like your fucking girlfriend sometimes.”  

 

“Piss off,” Lando says, not really meaning it. Wanting to tell Max a lot of things he can’t quite vocalize right now. Can’t convey his own gratitude. Can’t quite say how much he needed Max when he felt like he was losing himself. 

 

It’s too much for this moment. When he should be relaxing and trying to enjoy the company of his friend before it all ramps up again.

 

And he’s tired. He wants to curl up in his bed. Wants to sleep for a month straight to recover from the winter break travel and parties. Wants to drive his car with the same team he’s always had. Wants to fall back into what he knows. Not change everything. Not work with someone new. Someone so different from him. 

 

He pulls at his hoodie strings until the hood closes in around his face. 

 

This is going to be a long season.

 

----

 

Despite Lando’s resistance, they agree to meet for coffee and a chat on the last day at the factory before they head to Bahrain for testing. 

 

Well, Oscar had sent him a calendar invite to his work email, and Lando felt like he should at least try. After a long week of Max and Jon bothering him about getting used to working with Oscar, and giving him an honest try at getting to know him.

 

Even Will had sat him down in the sim and suggested he give Oscar a fair chance. That’d he still be around at races and was still cheering Lando on. But Lando needed to accept the change. 

 

He figures he owes it to them to at least try.  

 

If only they’d know how unbearably awkward it would be. 

 

Oscar insists on them chatting in his office. It’s smaller than Will’s, more exposed to the rest of the engineering team, but he has a door that closes and blocks out the noise. Gives them a smidgeon of privacy, for which Lando is grateful.

 

Even though they both spent most of the time traveling, Oscar has made an effort to decorate the space. Pictures of his family on the shelf behind his desk. A stuffed Koala next to them. A Lego model of Senna’s car. The desk is a bit cluttered, filled with papers and Post-it notes. Pen and highlighters scattered around. 

 

Messier than Lando thinks it would be. 

 

He tries not to be obvious about snooping as Oscar settles himself behind the desk and pushes a coffee cup towards him. 

 

Lando sits in the chair in front of the desk, feeling very much like a naughty student sent to the head teacher’s office. He slumps in his chair and takes the offered drink. The sound of his sipping is loud as he waits for Oscar to speak. 

 

Oscar, who shifts in his chair uncomfortably and pulls at the collar of his polo. He clears his throat and opens his mouth as if to speak before closing it again. He coughs and takes a sip of his coffee. 

 

Two introverts then, Lando thinks. 

 

He sighs and puts on his social, extroverted media persona. 

 

“So,” Lando starts. “Excited for the season?” 

 

It’s a stupid question. Cause what is Oscar going to say? No? 

 

But he’s looking at Lando so blankly, Lando isn’t even sure what to say. Lando smiles in an attempt to lighten the mood. 

 

People wanna say he’s charming? Might as well use it to break the ice. 

 

Oscar nods, and for some reason, Lando finds the action irritating. The type of irritation that scratches at his brain and settles deep into his bones. 

 

“Of course,” Oscar says, tone flat and dry as the desert. “It’s a big opportunity for me to be on your side of the garage. As the reigning champion.”

 

Even as bland as he says it, the words still send a thrill up Lando’s spine. Pumping him full of indescribable euphoria. He tries to temper the way the joy tugs at the corner of his lips, wanting to manifest as a huge grin. 

 

“I’m excited to work together this season,” Lando says, evening his tone out and batting away his smile. Hopeing his voice doesn’t betray him. That it doesn’t come with his own brand of sarcasm. That it at least sounds sincere. 

 

“Me too,” Oscar says quietly, sipping at his coffee. If he’s actually excited, he certainly doesn’t sound like it. Sounds more like working for Lando will be a special kind of torture. 

 

“Yeah, hopefully I’m a bit of an easier client than Danny is,” Lando jokes, laughing a bit. “I know it was a bit of a hard time between you two.”

 

Oscar looks at him. Somewhat of that same blank stare, but there’s a hint of something in his face. His brown eyes a little darker as he looks at Lando. His jaw tightens and twitches a bit. His flat, impassive face hardening just the slightest. 

 

Interesting, Lando thinks. 

 

“What do you mean?” Oscar asks, even if they both know exactly what he meant. 

 

“Ah, I just know you guys didn't like jive that well together,” Lando says with a shrug. Easy and light, but the words settle harshly in Oscar. 

 

“Did he say that?” he asks, raising his brow. 

 

Now, Lando feels awkward again. Even more than when they were sitting in silence, if that’s even possible. He knows there’s tension between them. That they didn’t work together well. But he’d never actually known if Oscar had felt the same sort of anger or irritation that Daniel had. 

 

Even when Danny was clearly pissed on the radio and taking low blows to the team’s strategy or the instructions Oscar gave, Oscar never really sounded that mad. He spoke in that same flat, dry tone he was speaking to Lando in now. Lando figured he was just the sort of guy who was able to brush off the words. The anger. That he was professional and controlled. That he probably looked down on drivers who couldn’t always control their emotions in the heat of the moment. 

 

But Lando had never seen Oscar’s face during the races. Obviously, how would he? 

 

But now, looking at Oscar. Looking at the minute signs of unspoken annoyance and anger clouding his expression and hanging around the tightness of his muscles. Well, Lando’s clearly hit a nerve. 

 

Not the best start to their professional relationship to piss off his new race engineer. 

 

“No, I mean, kinda,” Lando says, feeling like words are sort of tumbling around in his mouth. Brain searching desperately for a way to take it back.“Just you know, difference of opinion, I suppose.” 

 

Oscar scoffs and shakes his head. 

 

“Daniel doesn’t listen,” Oscar says, scoffing a bit. “There’s no difference of opinion when he didn’t even take into account what others have to say. Maybe if he listened to me, his name would be on that trophy instead of yours.”

 

Lando jolts back with annoyance. 

 

The fuck?

 

“Ay, what's that supposed to mean?” Lando asks, a bitter edge to his voice. Because he sure as hell hopes Oscar isn’t implying that Lando doesn't deserve that title. That he didn’t fight tooth and nail for it. That Lando only won it because of the team. 

 

That Oscar doesn’t feed into the media’s bullshit. Into Daniel’s bullshit. 

 

That Lando’s the golden boy. McLaren’s preferred champion. That they’d do anything to make sure he won and not Daniel. 

 

That Lando didn’t win that championship with his own hard work and dedication. 

 

“I just mean he had the potential,’ Oscar says, shrugging. Lando hates it. The way he says it is so smug. Like he knows better than Daniel. Than Lando. “But he doesn’t want to listen to the data. To the strategy. He thinks he knows better.”

 

That almost irritates Lando more. He has all the respect for the team and the strategists. But it's different when you’re the one in the car. Daniel wasn't always right on the radio. But he also wasn't always wrong. 

 

It seems Oscar didn’t think that was the case. 

 

No wonder Daniel hates this guy. 

 

“Well, you aren’t in the car,” Lando argues. It’s not like he disagrees completely with Oscar’s assessment, but to think he knows everything? More than a driver that’s been around. Cause of what? Spreadsheets and watching a little dot on the screen. It’s laughable. 

 

“I don't need to be in the car,” Oscar says, matter-of-factly. “I'm there to see everything you can't. You focus on driving right, and I focus on everything else. That’s what makes a good team.”

 

“There’s more to driving than just keeping the car on track,” Lando says. “Sometimes we know things you don't.”

 

“Like what?” Oscar asks, as if he can’t actually believe there might be something Lando knows that he doesn’t 

 

Prick, Lando thinks. 

 

“Like how the track feels or the tyres or if the suspension starts to feel off?” Lando lists off. “Or we can see what some of the other cars are doing that the cameras don’t pick up. We can help make decisions that you can’t see. Because we feel them. Before your little computer does.” 

 

“If that’s true,” Oscar starts, drawing out the words in that stupid Australian accent of his. “Then why are you always asking Will for help on the radio?”

 

Lando’s actually speechless for a moment. 

 

Fuck this guy, Lando thinks, glaring at Oscar. Twat. 

 

“I trust Will,” Lando says evenly, trying to rein in the anger boiling beneath the surface. The temper that Lando's worked hard to keep hidden. “He knows when I need some extra help, but he also listens when I have feedback. He takes what I say back to the team. He trusts that what I say actually means something.”

 

Oscar’s face twists up as he thinks. He lets out a deep breath, clearly trying to calm himself down before he speaks. “I’m not trying to fight with you, Lando. I’m just trying to work out what will be best for us. But this is my job. So when I speak on the radio, I need you to listen and to incorporate that into your driving. As long as you do that, then we’ll be fine.” 

 

Fine. 

 

Fine doesn’t win championships. 

 

“But you don't care what I have to say,” Lando says, raising his brow. 

 

“I didn’t say that.”

 

“No, but you think you’re better than us drivers cause you have an education and shit,” Lando says. 

 

Oscar doesn’t refute him completely. “I mean, this is what I went to school for, Lando. I studied for years to be an engineer, and I worked hard to get here. What I say matters. I study the data and the telemetry, and I use it to help you do the best you can do. I’m just saying sometimes drivers think they know better, but on the wall we have all the information we need.” 

 

“Ok,” Lando says slowly. “But this is my life. I’ve been racing for longer than you’ve been in school. I don’t need a degree because I’ve been there. In the garage with the mechanics. In the wind tunnel. In the fucking car. I think I’ve picked up a thing or two on how to go racing. About how the car works.”

 

“I know,” Oscar says, flatly. “But I know things you can’t see on the track. You have a race engineer for a reason.”

 

“And I'm the world champion for a reason.”

 

Lando knows he sounds petulant. Probably the child, Oscar thinks he is. But he hates feeling stupid. He didn’t finish school. He didn’t go to university. 

 

But Lando learned on the job. He learned from lockups and overtakes. From crashes and lost positions. He knows how these cars work. The team can design the cars on their computers or run them on the sim, but it’s Lando who can translate the way it feels under his body into actual, usable feedback. 

 

“I’m not trying to argue with you, Lando,” Oscar says, clearly trying to placate him. 

 

Lando’s not in the mood for this. To be talked down to. To have to be calmed down like he’s some hotheaded footballer. 

 

People want the confidence of a champion. 

 

A champion doesn't need this.

 

“Well, you’re doing a shit job of it already,” Lando says. He sets his cup down on the desk and stands up. His hands clench a bit at the side. “Guess I know what Daniel meant then.”

 

Oscar takes in a sharp breath. “Wait, Lando. I-.”

 

Fuck this. And fuck Oscar. 

 

“See you in Bahrain, Piastri.”

 

He leaves the room without a second glance, feeling nothing like a world champion and every bit like the temperamental rookie he was 7 years ago. 

Chapter End Notes

I am back because I guess I just can't stop writing Landoscar. And also, I am a sucker for writing an enemies to lovers!

Trying something a bit different with a bit of a longer work this time, and writing from both Lando and Oscar's perspectives. I hope you all enjoy and any and all feedback is so loved and appreciated!

Chapter 2: Bahrain On My Parade

Chapter Summary

It's preseason testing time and the first chance for Lando and Oscar to work together on track.

Chapter Notes

See the end of the chapter for notes

Oscar squints his eyes as sunlight streams into the van and shines directly in his face. 

 

It’s too bright. 

 

Too hot. 

 

He’s already sweating under his team polo and wishing he was elsewhere. 

 

His colleagues chat around him, sipping on their morning coffees and preparing for a long day of testing. His new counterpart is sitting behind him, chatting amicably with everyone else and offering tidbits of his last testing week.

 

The words drift in and out of Oscar’s consciousness as he watches them draw closer to the track. He doesn’t know much about Tom, except that he was clearly far older and more experienced than Oscar. 

 

Surely, he had to be if Daniel had worked with him prior and brought him over from another team. Considering that he clearly deemed Oscar too inexperienced to be kept on. To learn. If Oscar were more annoying, he’d ask Tom what his salary was. What his buyout was from the other team. What offer Daniel had forced Andrea to make. 

 

Surely, more than Oscar made, even with his so-called promotion. 

 

He tried not to feel a bit bitter about it. Like, he was being passed off and punished. Even if from the outside it looked like the opposite. 

 

Tom makes a joke that has some of the communications team laughing. 

 

They’d met a few times despite Oscar’s desire to keep the garages as separate as possible.  But Will had tasked Oscar with showing Tom how their data systems worked and going through the team-specific terms they used. It had been fine. Would be better if Tom hadn’t looked at Oscar like he was some high schooler trying to show a seasoned pro how to do his job.

 

And if Oscar hadn’t felt the same. 

 

Felt like every bit the failure Daniel made him out to be. 

 

And who Lando believed him to be. 

 

Lando, whom Oscar had yet to see since their last conversation. He’d come in on a late flight Wednesday night and spent most of Thursday doing media work with Daniel. Even in meetings, he was fleeting and avoided eye contact with Oscar, directing his words to Will and Andrea only. 

 

The most Oscar had seen were pictures of him on X. Seen Lando arrive in a stupid LN1 shirt and loose jeans. A flashy, diamond McLaren necklace hanging from his throat. Curls styled to look carefully tousled. A slit in his eyebrow. He looked like a douchebag. 

 

Is a douchebag, Oscar reminds himself, thinking of how quickly Lando had gotten defensive. Same as Daniel. Unwilling to listen. Always thinking he knows better. 

 

Still, Oscar feels a bit bad. He really hadn’t wanted to fight with Lando. He does want to help Lando win. Wants to prove he’s actually capable of doing his job. 

 

But that driver’s ego always wins out. Oscar has seen enough of it working his way through karting. Older kids winning races and thinking they’re the shit. Seen it from some of his few friends in F3, bragging about how teams were scouting them for junior programs. Seen it in his internship days, sitting back at the MTC and hearing notes from Carlos Sainz about how bad the car was. Heard the rumors about how he couldn’t wait to wear red. 

 

It was irritating to say the least. How a bunch of uneducated boys thought they knew better than the men and women who kept these cars running. Who were analyzing even the smallest parts and looking for ways to shave off kilos, all for a few hundredths of a second.  

 

And Oscar couldn’t say the team was always right. They didn’t always design the best car. Didn’t always make the best calls. But they were doing the best with what they had. With the data they got. 

 

More than what Lando had. 

 

Lando had feelings. 

 

Feelings didn’t build cars or championships. 

 

Oscar hugs his backpack tighter to his chest. 

 

Feelings lost drivers a win. Feelings caused drivers to make erratic, impulsive decisions. 

 

Having too many feelings almost made Lando a second-place finisher. 

 

Feelings had no place in racing or in Oscar’s job. And the fact that Lando couldn’t understand that. Couldn’t for a second learn towards logic and data. It makes Oscar weary. Makes him feel like he’s captaining a sinking ship. 

 

Uncertain that this will work between them. That he can get Lando to understand his point of view. That Lando won’t fight back on the radio the same way Daniel did for half a year. 

 

The van pulls up to the track entrance, and he shuffles out behind his coworkers. He slings his backpack around his shoulders, making to follow everyone when his eye catches on a shiny, black 750S pulling up beside them. 

 

It’s a gorgeous car and one Oscar’s hands itch to drive. Something to dream of until he makes a salary closer to Zak. Something he could only yearn to have parked in his building's garage instead of the used Toyota he brought when he graduated from university. 

 

The car of someone with money. Of someone who wants to be seen. 

 

He knows who it is before he even sees the LN444 plate on the back. 

 

As if it’s a beacon for attention, media circle the car as it parks, cameras flashing before he knows it. Oscar stops, watching as the car is parked crooked in its designated spot before both doors swing open. 

 

Lando and Daniel smile as they get out of the car, both in McLaren shirts and jeans. They laugh with each other, but Oscar’s too far away to hear them. 

 

He watches as they chat amicably. So different from the way they’d interacted at every meeting over the break. He watches as Lando slings his backpack over one shoulder like some preteen cool kid, the array of keychains swaying dangerously in a flash of neon green. He fumbles for his paddock pass before a bodyguard appears out of nowhere to hand it to him. 

 

“You coming, Oscar?” one of the mechanics, Harry maybe, asks. And Oscar snaps out of his reverie. He nods, pulling his pass out from the pocket of his team-issued slacks and heads for the gates, with his head down. 

 

He bumps into a photographer after one step. 

 

“Sorry, mate,” the guy gruffs out. “But I gotta get a shot of them.”

 

“Yeah, sorry,” he says automatically, moving out of the way. 

 

“Hey, he’s trying to get to work, bro,” A voice says behind them. “You’re in his way.” 

 

Lando saddles up to them. Oscar’s hit with a cloud of grapefruit and something woody, and undoubtedly expensive.  It swirls in his nose and sits warm and intoxicating in his brain. Suddenly, Oscar hopes the stupid chocolate Lynx, his sister bought him, isn’t too strong. 

 

This close, Oscar can see the two diagonal moles that frame his nose and the smattering of gray in his facial hair. His eyes search Oscar’s. Deep, aquatic eyes that assess him. Make him feel a bit flayed open. 

 

“Morning, Lando,” Oscar says automatically, ignoring how Daniel’s glaring at him over Lando’s shoulder. A team of bodyguards and assistants forms an almost semi-circle around them. Oscar’s sure the social team is getting antsy waiting for Lando to scan in and they can get shots of his entrance. 

 

“Hiya Osc,” Lando says low and teasing. A smug and irritating smirk on his lips. “And what are you doing out here like a little lost lamb? Tripping photographers and the like.”

 

Oscar stops himself from scoffing in Lando’s face. Certainly, not the image the team would want. Not if they have Lando and Daniel arriving together like the best of buds. 

 

“Nice car,” Oscar says. “Not flashy or obvious in any way at all. Certainly, no need to remind everyone who you are. Unless you’re worried about being a forgettable champion.” 

 

Lando laughs, smile wide and open. His eyes, however, fix Oscar with something cold and angry. 

 

“Fuck off,” he curses under his breath, soft and only for Oscar’s ears. He claps him on the arm and speaks a bit louder. “Better get inside. Wouldn’t want to be late for our first day, would we?” 

 

He winks at Oscar before slinking away. 

 

It sends a shiver up his spine. 

 

Of irritation. 

 

Prat, Oscar thinks as he trails behind the two drivers into the paddock. 

 

----

 

Oscar’s back is screaming from where he’s hunched over a table in hospitality, reading through run plans on his computer. 

 

He’d made most of his notes and final changes during the long flight from London to Sakhir, but it doesn’t sit as right with him as it did a few days ago. Too many short runs. Not enough race simulations. Not enough time for Oscar to truly see how this car performed long term. 

 

He knew it was going to be fast. Had to be. For this team. Beyond helping his driver, Oscar needed to help his team. Needed to be a back to back Constructer’s. Needed to work towards another trophy for the team that had given him a chance. A place in racing. 

 

He sighs, typing up another note to pass onto the team, before drafting up a new tyre plan. 

 

“Ah, there he is,” A voice sounds next to him. Oscar sits up, wincing as his back aches against the stretch, to see Will approaching with Lando’s trainer. “Oscar, do you have a minute?” 

 

Well, Will’s technically his boss now, so Oscar always has a minute for him. He nods and gestures at the empty seats at the table. He shuffles his notebooks and cold, half-drunk coffee out of the way.  

 

“I just wanted you to get acquainted with Jon, Lando’s performance coach,” Will says pleasantly. Jon extends his hand, and Oscar shakes it firmly. 

 

“Nice to meet you, mate,” Jon says, smiling like he genuinely means it. Oscar says it back even when he’s not sure he completely means it. He doesn’t not mean it, but he isn’t sure what use Lando’s trainer is to him. 

 

“Same, mate,” Oscar says politely. 

 

“It’s good for you to get to know each other a bit,” Will says. “Jon’s been with Lando since he was a kid. He has a lot of great insights into Lando’s performance that really helped me when we were starting out.”

 

“Yeah, when he was annoying little fucker,” Jon says, laughing. 

 

“Exactly,” Will says, laughing fondly. “Jon also has a good idea of the schedule, and it helps to know what Lando’s schedule is pre and post-race so you can accommodate around that. Fit in some time for briefing and giving over-run plans again.”

 

“Yeah,” Oscar nods. “I’d love to go over all that.” 

 

Not true, but it's good to know. Any snippet of any information that can make dealing with Lando less painful than it’s been so far. 

 

“Good,” Will says. “Gotta run and meet with Andrea, but let me know if you need anything from me.” 

 

He claps Jon on the shoulder and leaves quickly. It’s odd how Oscar and Will have sat together so closely for months and yet Oscar feels like he hardly knows him. Felt like he was somewhat of the enemy at times, which is stupid considering Daniel would have never caught up to Max or Lando. 

 

Yet, Oscar clearly missed the camaraderie amongst Lando’s side of the garage. Oscar feels well-connected to his coworkers. The mechanics, the design team, and all the other engineers on his side clearly work hard and want to do a good job. But did it matter if it was Daniel in the car or someone else? Yeah, they always want to win, but would it matter who sat in the car? 

 

It seems like on the other side, everything centered around Lando. Like he was leading the team, not the other way around. Maybe it was just the disconnect Oscar has with Daniel, and certainly his personal team, that made it all feel a bit impersonal. Last year, the Stig from Top Gear could have been in the car, and Oscar would not have cared as long as he did well. And listened.

 

But it seems like there’s a personal drive amongst this side of the garage to see Lando succeed personally. 

 

Oscar had seen bits of it as an intern, more of it once he was put on the wall. It's a different feeling being in it versus watching it from the outside. He's not sure if he likes it or not. 

 

“How’s it been with Lando then?” Jon asks, directing Oscar’s attention back. “I’m sure he’s been a right brat.”

 

“Oh, uh,” Oscar splutters. “No, I mean we…uh…we’ve been getting on.” 

 

Jon laughs. “No need to lie to me, mate. I’ve never heard Lando spend a whole run complaining about someone as he did with you. Frankly, I thought he might pass out from the lack of breath.” 

 

“Jesus,” Oscar says, scrubbing a hand down his face, feeling rightfully embarrassed. 

 

“It’s no worries,” Jon says, smiling bright and genuine. “I know how he is.”

 

“How he is?” 

 

“A little shit,” Jon says. 

 

“Right,” Oscar agrees. “We just have a difference of opinion, it seems.”

 

“Sure,” he says, flippantly. “Lando’s just adjusting to it all, I think. I know he’s been annoying, but I wouldn’t take it too personally. He’s been with Will for a long time, and he’s not good at explaining or asking for what he needs sometimes. Obviously, Will and I get it since we’ve been with him for a while, but you’ve never really worked with Lando before?” 

 

Oscar shakes his head. “Obviously, we have quite a different working style. But I think we have the same goal in mind.” 

 

“Well, that's good,” Jon says. “But honestly, all Lando really needs is a bit of reassurance sometimes. Especially after last year.” 

 

“But he won,” Oscar says. “Isn’t that reassurance enough?” 

 

“I mean, he’s certainly happier,” Jon says. “More confident. Less burdened. But I’m not sure what’ll happen once he’s really back in the car. Lando’s always been one to beat himself up at any chance. And I think last season really made him punish himself in ways he didn’t even know were possible.” 

Well, that doesn’t completely surprise Oscar. 

 

Oscar is aware of how the media perceives Lando. 

 

Weak. Complainer. Emotional. 

 

Oscar can certainly agree with the last one. But, he hadn’t heard much complaining from Lando on the radio. Sure, he asked for help or whatever, but complain? Oscar heard a lot more of that from his own headphones. 

 

“Do you think Lando is too emotional?” Oscar asks. Maybe it’s overstepping their boundaries as somewhat of coworkers. As part of Lando’s team. But Oscar is too curious to hear what someone who knows him so intimately might say.

 

Jon seems to consider his answer for a moment before speaking. 

 

“It’s not really the emotion, is it?” he says. “We all get a bit emotional. Even just watching the race, I can get angry or upset or whatever. But Lando is front and center. He doesn’t leave the emotion inside the helmet and lock it away for the media like some other drivers.”

 

Jon sighs as he continues. “It’s not the emotion that bothers people, really. It’s that Lando is open enough to express it. People are uncomfortable with vulnerability.” 

 

Oscar hadn’t really thought of it like that. 

 

He knows it's a common misconception amongst everyone he knows, even his own family, that he isn’t that emotional. That his liking for data and spreadsheets is just an extension of his calm, composed demeanor. His often blank facade. 

 

Truthfully, Oscar worries he may feel emotions more strongly than everyone else, but he’s suppressed them for far too long. That the fear and stress and anger actually sit quite strong and deep inside of him. That sometimes they ran so whole and consistently in his body that he’d had no choice but to push them down until he became numb to them. In turn, Oscar learned to lean towards things that didn’t require emotion. Things like engineering and statistics. Things that wouldn’t remind him of how it felt to feel so strongly. And his impassive face became a mask of passive, collected indifference. 

 

So unlike Lando, who couldn’t hide any emotion that crossed him.  He imagined Lando had never had much struggle in growing up. Didn’t know deep sadness or unending anger. Rather, Lando felt every emotion bright and fleeting, lighting up like a match. 

 

One that couldn’t extinguish until it was felt and expressed. 

 

Quickly lit and quickly extinguished. 

 

That once Lando had a little whine in a debrief or bemoaned his performance to a journalist when he shouldn’t, that he would be ok. Off to golf with his friends or a party in Ibiza. 

 

Oscar isn’t good at letting go like that. If he feels it, he sits with it. Let it sit inside his bones and grow heavy in his stomach. 

 

Better to avoid that completely. 

 

So Oscar gets what Jon means a bit. It is a bit uncomfortable, not just to feel it, but to watch someone unable to suppress their emotions like Lando. 

 

“So what should I do then?” Oscar asks. “What do you do when Lando gets emotional? Just tell him he’s doing a good job?” 

 

“Well, I’m sure he wouldn't mind that,” Jon says, chuckling. “But, really, he just wants to be acknowledged. To be heard.”

 

“But will he listen back?” Oscar asks. 

 

“Not always,” Jon says. “But Lando deals strongly in trust. If he trusts you, then he’ll listen. It’s a two-way street with him.”

 

“Well, I certainly don’t think he trusts me,” Oscar says. 

 

“It’ll take time,” Jon says. “Lando’s friendly and open about how he feels most of the time, but he takes some time to warm up to people on a real level. He doesn’t trust easily. Doesn’t let people in that fast.”

 

Jon hums. “It’s funny, everyone thinks they know exactly how he feels all the time. But honestly, most people haven’t got an actual clue how he feels or who he really is. It’s a privilege to be one of Lando’s trusted people.”

 

The thought stirs in Oscar’s head. He doesn’t want to disregard what Jon’s saying. It just feels strange. He feels like he does know who Lando is. 

 

But maybe Jon is right. 

 

Oscar only knows who Lando has presented to him. 

 

“I hope he can learn to trust me then,” Oscar says, and finds he might mean it. If only for the sake of a decent season. 

 

“I hope so, too,” Jon agrees. “Needs it if he wants to win again. And I hope you’ll put some trust back in him, Oscar. He might surprise you.” 

 

“I’m not quite sure him surprising me is a good thing,” Oscar admits. 

 

“Believe me, it can be,” Jon says, making to leave. “You two will figure it out, I’m sure. If not for each other, then for the sake of just wanting to win.”

 

Well, Oscar agrees on that common goal.

 

“Of course.”

 

“And if Lando acts like a snippy little bitch again, let me know. I can always add another set of burpees to the workout plan.” 

 

Oscar watches as Jon heads back towards Lando’s driver room and slumps a bit in his chair. 

 

It sounds so simple when Jon spells it out. But the truth is more complicated. 

 

Trust? 

 

Not so easily earned or given in either of their cases. 

 

But maybe Oscar can just give Lando a few words of acknowledgment after a good lap and see where it gets him. 

----

 

Lando’s never been more grateful to see the inside of his driver's room. Well, the floor of it, as Jon forces him onto his stomach on the table, so he can press into all the knots in Lando’s back. It’s painful. 

 

Just not nearly as painful as his drive in had been. 

 

It’s not like he and Daniel had some sort of falling out. 

 

It’s just that Lando did his job. 

 

Accomplished his biggest dream. 

 

Lost a friend along the way. 

 

He wouldn’t say they were nearly as close as Lando was with George or Alex or Carlos. But Lando has been to his house for Christ's sake. Seen him in various states of intoxication. Met his family and his girlfriend and his closest friends. 

 

Shared the same with him.

 

And yet, the entire drive to the track had been in silence. Two men who could talk about nothing for hours suddenly run dry of topics. 

 

It wasn’t until they’d gotten to the track that Daniel had sighed and slipped on a fake smile along with his team cap.  

 

“We'd better have a laugh or Zak will lose his goddamn mind if we don’t look like the perfect papaya family.”

 

“Right,” Lando said, feeling stiff and awkward as he parked the car. One of his worst parking jobs to date. “I can do that.” 

 

And Lando did laugh. He’s not even sure what Daniel had actually said. All he knew was how to put his PR training in and just laugh and smile like they were the same as they always were. He cursed whoever thought it was a good idea that they arrive together. 

 

And of course, Oscar had been standing there, practically glaring at them both as they arrived. 

 

The icing on Lando’s bullshit cake. 

 

Maybe, if Lando weren’t currently surrounded by a horde of photographers and fans, he might entertain the idea of being a bit nicer. A little more apologetic after their last meeting. Jon had certainly given him a stern talking to, followed by another one from Will. 

 

Now, Lando figures it’s been a bit too late to apologize. Not to mention, it doesn’t seem like he was ever going to get one from Oscar. 

 

But he could be polite. 

 

He isn’t some fucking rookie. He’s a goddamn professional. He can maintain a working relationship. 

 

Even the sight of that smug, unimpressed face kinda pisses him off like no other. 

 

“He seems like he honestly wants to help,” Jon says, pressing his fingers deeply into Lando’s hamstring. 

 

“I’m just not used to him,” Lando says. He grits his teeth after one particularly hard dig into his muscles. “And he’s so… I don’t know. He just seems like he thinks he knows better than me.” 

 

Jon laughs. “Well, I can’t argue with that. But it never hurts to have another perspective.” 

 

“That’s what everyone keeps saying,” Lando says. “Yet, I don’t know if that’s true.”

 

“Well, we’ll just have to see, won’t we?” Jon asks, moving onto Lando’s other leg. “He seems like a nice guy. A bit awkward and invested in his work. But you’re the same in your own way.” 

 

“What?” Lando squeaks, trying to turn to look at Jon, who firmly presses his head back down. “I’m not awkward.” 

 

“You are,” he says, not looking for an argument. “You say weird shit all the time, mate. And you crack jokes when you don’t want to be uncomfortable. And you can pretend all you want, but I know you’re thinking about being in the car 24/7.“

 

“Thought this was massage therapy,” Lando says. “I didn't come here looking to get diagnosed.”

 

“I’m just saying maybe you both aren’t as different as you think,” Jon says. “For all you know, once you get into the car, you may work really well together.” 

 

Lando scoffs, burning a hole into the carpet with his gaze. 

 

Unsure if that’s possible. They’ve had a handful of conversations, and each one has left Lando humming with irritation. Wanting to rip that smug look off Oscar’s face. Wanting to scream at him. 

 

Wanting to feel less like a dumb athlete in Oscar’s presence. 

 

“We’ll see,” Lando says, biting his lip to keep his thoughts inside. 

 

“Good. But enough about that,” Jon says, finishing Lando’s legs and stepping back from the table. “I’m dying to know about that drive-in with Riccardo.”

 

“Ugh,” Lando says, turning onto his back. “I thought about ramming into a stop sign just to have something to talk about. You won’t believe how fucking awkward it was.” 

 

Jon laughs, and Lando melts back into the massage table. Grateful to get his mind off getting into the car for a few minutes. 

 

----

 

He tops the timing page on the first day. It feels good. To have the car underneath him again. Ears full of a rumbling engine and radio static. It’s a good first session. Feels like a great start to the season.

 

Oscar even comes onto the radio and lets Lando know the lap is good. 

 

Certainly not a rave review of his performance, but better than nothing. 

 

Unfortunately, it’s the last good day of testing for Lando. 

 

The longer he runs, the worse the car feels. 

 

Not that the car is bad, per se. Lando can tell the car is good. Great, even. Fast. And clearly miles ahead of the other teams. 

 

But it doesn’t feel good to him. It feels different. Adverse to him. To his driving style. 

 

He feels like he’s fighting the car the longer he carries on. No matter if the tyres are being changed or Oscar’s feeding different info in his ear. 

 

It doesn’t seem to be an issue for the other car, though. 

 

He can’t see the screen that well, but he can see Daniel’s times enough. 

 

High. Higher than Lando.

 

Who has been running a race simulation for the better part of the last day. 

 

“I want to go again,” he says, irritation thumming under the surface. “I want another qualifying sim. I can go to the top of the timesheet.”

 

“We already did that,” Oscar says over the radio, like he’s speaking to a child. “It’s more important that we see how well the car does in long stints. We want to test the fuel ranges.”

 

Stupid, Lando thinks. Surely, they have this information already. Surely, Oscar can run the calculations from the first 30 laps Lando did.

 

He bristles under his helmet.

 

All Jon’s hopes for good harmony between them is quickly dashed over the long hours of testing. It seems like from the moment something doesn’t run smoothly, the same difference of opinion bleeds into their conversations on the radio. 

 

When Lando went fast but inconsistent, Oscar still had a suggestion of where he could have pushed harder. 

 

When Lando asked for some info on the tyres, Oscar gave him too much information before telling Lando to pull back and that he's doing too much. 

 

When Lando practiced his start, Oscar was already in his ear by the first corner, telling him that he didn’t react fast enough and that he should try this and that. 

 

They were speaking to each other on the radio, but they might as well be talking to themselves. 

 

And Lando has tried. Took some of Oscar’s advice, acknowledged where Oscar might have been right. But, is met by total silence, if not a new data point, Oscar needs him to try out. 

 

 It was as Lando feared, his words often went unheard by Oscar. 

 

“I can go faster,” Lando insists. “Where am I on the timings?” 

 

“P11,” Oscar says. “But we already know we’re fast. This isn’t about that.” 

 

“Champion and I’m P11,” Lando scoffs. “What a fucking joke.” 

 

“How do the tyres feel?” Oscar asks, ignoring him completely. “Pace looks good.” 

 

“I can go longer,” Lando says automatically. “Bit of understeer in some of the corners, though.”

 

“I don’t see that,” Oscar says. “Try changing the gears when you do turn 3 on the next lap.” 

 

“Yeah, but I’m having trouble hitting the apex right,” Lando says. “I feel like the car is not going where I want. I’m missing the line when I come out of the braking.” 

 

Oscar scans all of his screens. “I don’t see anything wrong from my end. We can take a look when you come in next.” 

 

“It’s not something you’ll see on the screens,” Lando huffs. “Just doesn’t feel right.” 

 

“It doesn’t seem like the other car is having any issues,” Oscar says. He says it as blandly and neutrally as he does everything else, but it feels like a direct hit to Lando. Like he’s purposely trying to find a way to slash at Lando through all the belts and fireproof fabric. “Just you.”

 

“Piss off, Piastri,” Lando says. “I’m telling you this is how I feel. And I feel like I’m fighting the car.” 

 

The broadcast is gonna have a field day with this one. He can already see the clips of this radio all over Twitter.

 

“It’s a new car, Lando,” Oscar says, evenly. “You just have to get used to it. This is not last year’s car.” 

 

“I obviously know that,” he grits out. “I’m saying that something’s different. Been saying it for the last three days, if you actually cared to listen.” 

 

He can hear Oscar let out a heavy sigh over the radio. 

 

“Nothing is different from when we ran the sim,” Oscar says. “Or between either cars.”

 

Lando stays silent on the radio for a long time. 

 

“We can discuss it after,” Oscar suggests. 

 

“Can we?” Lando pushes. “Are you gonna listen? Or am I just gonna sit there while you tell me that nothing’s wrong?” 

 

“I didn’t say that nothing was wrong, but we can check it after,” Oscar says. “Focus on adjusting and putting the laps in.” 

 

There’s an uptick in his voice. Maybe he’s nervous. It’s not like these conversations are private. 

 

Good, Lando thinks. Let the whole fucking paddock hear how you treat the reigning champion. 

 

“Yeah, just shut up and drive, right?” Lando says, unable to stop himself. He can practically see the exasperated looks Will and Jon must have.  “Like some fucking monkey.” 

 

Oscar sighs in agitation. Lando wonders if Zak and Andrea are listening. If they care. 

 

“I’m not gonna get in a fight with you over the radio about testing, Lando,” Oscar says, finally. “We’ll discuss after.” 

 

“Isn’t this like everything for you nerds?” Lando says. “We’re testing the car we’re gonna debut in Australia. If the world champion isn’t comfortable, then maybe that’s something to look into?” 

 

Lando hits a nerve. 

 

“Or maybe the world champion doesn’t think he can win again unless the car is perfect for him?” Oscar snipes, like he can’t help himself. “A world champion should be able to drive whatever car he’s given.” 

 

And Oscar’s hit one back. 

 

There’s silence for a moment, the words actually making him speechless for a second. Lando takes another corner and almost hits the gravel. He rights the car and pushes onward. 

 

A hot flare of anger licks up his spine and settles sharp and strong on his tongue. 

 

“God, you are such a little pric-,” Lando starts before he’s cut off. 

 

“We can discuss whatever these issues you’re having outside the car, Lando,” Andrea butts in, firm, but still far too gentle for Oscar’s liking. “You know your feedback is very valuable to us. We’ll talk in the debrief, ok?”

 

Lando stares ahead. He can see Lawson and Charles ahead of him. Watches as they drive. Follows their lines as he forces himself to take steadying breaths. Or at least as steadying as he can get against the g-force. 

 

He bats away at the annoyance still clouding his mind and sitting heavy in his chest. 

 

He grips tighter on the wheel. Feels the curves of it beneath his fingers. Let’s the vibration of the engine settle in him like a second blood flow. 

 

‘Yeah. Copy.”

 

“Ok, finish these laps for the race sim,” Andrea says, clicking off his mic. “Please.” 

 

“Yes,” Lando says, flushing even beneath the balaclava. He hopes Oscar feels as stupid and chastised as he does. 

 

Actually, he hopes he feels more.



----

 

Oscar’s never felt more like a child being scolded. Even as a kid, he doesn’t think his parents have ever looked as disappointed in him as Andrea does now. 

 

He slumps in his seat inside Andrea’s hospitality office. Lando sits next to him, looking equally embarrassed. His tan skin hides it well, but Oscar can see a faint flush still coloring his cheeks and ears. He’s still in his suit, even if it's rolled down to his delicate waist. The sweat’s made his curls a little rumpled, but he still smells faintly of cologne and oil. 

 

It tickles all of Oscar’s senses. 

 

If he weren’t so anxious and frustrated, he might have think of being annoyed at how Lando can still look so good after spending four hours in the car. 

 

“You two need to sort this out,” Andrea stresses. The sun has long since set, but he’s sipping on a cappuccino like it’s eight in the morning. “Lando, you are the world champion.”

 

“Then why doesn’t he listen to what I say?” Lando argues, waving his hand at Oscar. 

 

Andrea holds up a finger. “Let me speak, please.” 

 

Lando sighs, but nods. 

 

“You are the champion, and you are an integral part of this team,” Andread continues. “And Oscar, you are the youngest engineer on the team. We brought you onto the wall so early for a reason. You’re both very smart and valuable assets of this team. So why can we not work together?”

 

They both sit in silence, waiting for the other to speak. Andrea looks at them both, brow raised. 

 

“Lando?” he asks, calling on him like a father to the eldest son. “What is the problem?” 

 

“He doesn’t want to listen to me,” Lando says, like a whiny child, pointedly ignoring Oscar’s answering scoff. “I’m not just saying shit to say shit. The car feels off to me.”

 

“Only to you,” Oscar says. “I’m trying, Lando, but if I tell you all the checks show that the car is working right, then what do you want me to do?” 

 

“Oh, I don’t know,” Lando says, high and mocking. “Maybe, like help me figure out what’s wrong or even just acknowledge that I feel like something’s off.” 

 

“I didn’t know I was your mummy and had to make sure you feel good about everything,” Oscar says, all but sticking his tongue out at the other man. It’s so childish to say, but he might as well stoop to his level.

 

“Well, sorry I’m a human with emotions and thoughts and not a fucking spreadsheet, so you don’t know how to interact with me,” Lando says, rolling his eyes. “Surely even your AI girlfriend codes some emotions for you. It can’t be that foreign a concept for you.” 

 

Oscar blinks at him, unbelieving. “I don’t have an AI girlfriend.” 

 

“Please, like you aren’t jacking it to ChatGPT in your spare time, you robot,” Lando scoffs. “Or do you prefer Gemini cause it’s a sponsor and you’re such a team player?” 

 

Oscar never resorts to violence, but he has the strangest, strongest urge to wrap his hands around Lando’s indecently thick throat. 

 

“You don’t have to act like such a little brat just because the data is too hard for you,” Oscar sneers. “Maybe if you spent less time fucking Instagram models and more time actually learning how the car runs best, you’d be at the top of the times, instead of Daniel.” 

 

Lando’s mouth drops open. 

 

“Maybe if you actually knew how to do your job, you’d still be on the other side of the pitwall,” Lando says. 

 

Oscar bellows out a hollow, befuddled laugh.

 

“So you agree that working with you is a punishment, then?” Oscar says, coolly. 

 

“Oh fuck you,” Lando says, crossing his arms over his chest. “Fucking honor more like.” 

 

“Please,” Oscar says. “You’re so fucking full of yourself, Norris.” 

 

“You wish you-,” Lando starts. 

 

“Ok, enough,” Andrea interrupts firmly, massaging his temples. “This can’t carry on into Australia.” 

 

Well, that’s something all three of them can agree on. 

 

“I’m not going to get rid of either of you,” Andrea says, like that’s even an option. Well, there is an option, Oscar knows. 

 

Get rid of him to appease the champion. The lowest on the totem pole in this group. The fragility of his position is all dependent on Lando’s will. 

 

He swallows harshly. 

 

“I’m sorry,” Oscar says, immediately. “I know we shouldn’t be fighting like kids.” 

 

Lando makes an annoyed noise next to him. Oscar looks at him and raises his brow. 

 

Luckily, Lando gets the message. 

 

“Sorry,” he says. “I’m just trying to go into the season at my best. I want to defend my title.” 

 

“Of course, we all want that as well, Lando,” Andrea says. “But Oscar is right that there is nothing wrong with the car right now. It’s a driving style issue. Let’s take it a few more races, and we can get a full idea of what we can do to make it better for you.” 

 

Lando looks unhappy, but nods. His lips forming into a grim, firm line. 

 

“And Oscar will do his best to help you communicate your feedback to the rest of the engineering team, yes?” 

 

“Yes.”

 

“Good,” Andrea sighs. “But what are we to do about you two?” 

 

“I,” Oscar starts and swallows. “I will try harder. To listen and acknowledge Lando’s feedback.” 

 

“That’s good,” Andrea says. “I fear it won’t be enough, though. You two need to find a way to get along and communicate effectively before we get into the thick of the season.” 

 

“Can’t we just limit radio communication or something?” Lando suggests, making Oscar roll his eyes again. “What?” 

 

“How would that even work?” Oscar says.

 

“I mean, it seems to work for Fernando,” Lando shrugs. “Just communicate what’s really important.”

 

“It’s all important,” Oscar says, incredulous. As if he’s talking to Lando just to talk.  

 

“Ok, I don’t think he’s our best example of a team player,” Andrea says, like the idea of Fernando haunts him. “You two just need to get better acquainted. It’s normal to have some growing pains when we make big changes.”

 

Both men make slight noises of acknowledgment. 

 

Andrea claps his hands and looks at both of them, a fervent look of excitement lighting his eyes. “I want you to do some teambuilding. Just the two of you. Find something you can both agree on and try it out. I’m not saying you have to have a long talk, but if you can just tolerate an activity together for an hour, then maybe we can make some headway.” 

 

“Teambuilding?” Lando says, raising his brows. “That’s so…corporate.” 

 

“There’s a reason it works, though,” Andrea says, smiling. “Yes, I think this will be nice.” 

 

Lando throws his head back and scrunches his face in annoyance. 

 

“So what are we supposed to do?” Lando asks. “Like a trust fall? Or go to Top Golf or something?” 

 

“Sure,” Andrea says, laughing in delight. “I’ll have you both figure it out. Just do it soon. I want to see a good improvement before we get to Albert Park.” 

 

Oscar sighs. It’s not like he has much choice. Too young and ingrained in McLaren to be hedging offers with other teams. Too deep a desire to keep helping them win. Even if he is not working for the winner of his choice. 

 

“Yeah, fine,” he agrees, anything to get out of this room and back in Andrea’s good graces. “We can do the teambuilding thing.”

 

Lando looks at him. “Seriously?” 

 

“Have another suggestion?” Oscar asks, shrugging. Lando looks at him for a moment as he thinks. Oscar can practically see the gears in his head, probably wondering how much he can leverage his position. How much fuss he can make until Will comes back to being his engineer. How much he cares about Will to not make him do that. 

 

Lando sighs after a moment, looking properly deflated. He looks tired, too, Oscar thinks. Like the long days of testing have finally caught up to him. 

 

“Fine,” Lando says. “We can try it out. But no promises. And we still need to work on the communications stuff.” 

 

“Agreed,” Oscar says as Andrea nods. 

 

“And if the car still doesn’t feel right after Australia, will you at least consider my feedback?” Lando says, looking at Oscar. 

 

Oscar bites his lip. Not like he has much choice. 

 

He nods. “Ok, I can do that.” 

 

Lando sits back in small satisfaction. “Alright then.” 

 

“Great,” Andrea says, smiling, but clearly done with their shit. “I can’t wait to hear what you two come up with.” 

 

Right. Oscar scours his brain for something they can do. It’s not like he can bring an F1 driver to an arcade or axe-throwing place. Not like, he’s going to invite Lando to his house. Or expect Lando to take him around Monaco to see where all the other tax evaders live. 

 

Luckily, it seems Lando has an idea first. 

 

“Have you ever played padel, Oscar?”

 

Chapter End Notes

Thank you so much for taking the time to read! All feedback and love have been greatly appreciated! I haven't really written Landoscar being this snippy to each other, but I'm finding it quite fun!

Also I do watch the races and I feel like I know a good amount, but I am no engineer, and I do research, but if things are wrong, please know I'm trying my best. I have a much better handle on the rules than the engineering part of F1. And I'm using writing this as an excuse to rewatch moments from last season. I miss watching Lando and Oscar fighting for wins lol

Chapter 3: Melbourne Melodrama

Chapter Summary

Lil bit of padel, a lot of Australia

Chapter Notes

See the end of the chapter for notes

Oscar’s never played padel. Which doesn’t really surprise Lando. He hadn’t thought of Oscar Piastri as someone who cares much for playing sports that don’t involve a motor. 

 

But Lando loves padel. Maybe, not so much the actual game. Rather, he loves the company it provides. Loves that it must be a four-person game. That it gives him a chance to catch Max or George or Alex in a moment that isn’t strictly focused on work.

 

Frankly, Lando thinks everyone is using padel as an excuse to see each other without being bombarded by media or team obligations. He knows they all play at not being friends, but the truth is, they are. They all spend more time together than they do with their families or their girlfriends. In fact, Lando finds it a bit silly to pretend he isn’t friends with at least some of the drivers. He’s been to Max’s baby shower. He went to one of Carlos’ cousins’ weddings. 

 

He’s helped a hungover George wash vomit out of his clothes, for God's sake. 

 

They’re friends. And he can openly admit he enjoys their company, even if they all want to act nonchalant. 

 

He’s sure everyone and their mother knows he’s never been good at turning off affection or friendship. Not fully. 

 

But Lando also knows it might be a bit much for someone like Oscar. Someone quiet and a bit shy. 

 

Lando understands that. He’s worked the extroverted, sunny personality into a perfect, media mold. But he’s quiet, too. He likes listening. He likes time alone to just feel like himself again.

 

It’s just that he doesn’t have the job for it. Even in the car, he’s always on the radio. 

 

So Oscar might not believe it, but Lando understands he might feel a bit shy playing padel with other drivers or strangers. 

 

Not to mention, he doesn’t even know how to play. God forbid, he makes Oscar Piastri do something he doesn’t know in front of strangers. 

 

Though he won’t be surprised if Oscar shows up and confesses to having done hours of research on the topic. Or watched videos and videos of tutorials. Or asked his AI to write him out the rules.  

 

Lando laughs to himself at the very thought. 

 

At least he can tell Andrea he tried, even if this doesn’t work out. Even if they end up bickering on the radio. Again. 

 

Even if Lando has to ask for a different engineer.

 

He’d prefer not. Really would never want to fuck over someone’s career like that. Even someone as insufferable and inflexible as Oscar. 

 

But at the end of the day, he has to do what he needs to win. That’s sports, isn’t it? 

 

Lando sighs loudly. He really needs to make this work, or else he’ll be wracked with guilt. 

 

He bounces the ball against the wall with his hands. A little reflex practice doesn’t hurt as he waits for Oscar to arrive. He’s rented out a court for the evening, the closest padel place he could find between his rental in London and Woking.  

 

Maybe it’s overkill to rent the whole place out, but he’d rather not be seen. Or have his photographs spread all over the internet. He’d stressed to Oscar over email that this would just be a lesson, but he can’t say it won’t turn into another spat. And he’d rather not look like a total dick on the internet. Again.

 

If this stupid teambuilding even works in the first place.

 

Lando does feel a bit ashamed. More than ashamed at how quickly his temper had flared. In front of Andrea, of all people. But something about Oscar just pissed him off. 

 

The smug way he spoke and assumed he knew better. How he treated Lando like an insolent child. How dismissive he was. 

 

It made him feel stupid. 

 

In a way, he hadn't felt since primary school, when he’d try to read out loud, and the letters in the words got all jumbled up.  

 

Maybe that was why Oscar made his blood boil so much. Taking him back to a time when everyone thought he was slow and dumb. Where the only place he could prove his worth was how fast he could drive on the track. 

 

He catches the ball as it rebounds off the wall and clutches it tight in his grip. 

 

He wishes the words didn’t get so mixed up coming from his brain to his mouth. That the anger didn’t light up his body and make his blood boil. That he could hold back all the vicious words and insults that flowed so easily. 

 

Wishes that he could express that he’s not an engineer, but he understands. That he knows how these cars work. That he’s been in the garage helping put it together. That he knows Oscar has the data. 

 

But Lando has the feeling.
