He certainly doesn’t like Oscar all that much still. 

 

But he is a part of Lando’s team. 

 

So, Lando will take care of him. Like he does with all of his people. No different than Will or Jarv or Max. For the most part. 

 

Different, only in that he wants him. Or his body at least. And the way he makes Lando feel so hazy and light from just a few words. The way that dream follows Lando like a ghost. 

 

“This is quite nice,” Oscar says, as they sit down. He looks around. 

 

“Surely, you’ve had some fine dining before, Piastri,” Lando says, sipping at his sprite. The little bit of sugar, Jon would allow. “Boarding schools. Karting. Oxford. You’re a rich kid.”

 

Oscar laughs, whole and honest. “Sure, you could say that. I bet our dads could go toe to toe on whose richer.” 

 

“My dad’s not Stroll rich, but he does decently,” Lando says. “Well, more than decent, but we’re not allowed to talk like that.” 

 

“No?” Oscar asks with a laugh. “The humble Norris’.”

 

“Hey, I’m humble,” Lando says, leaning back into his seat and fixing Oscar with a look. 

 

“You are,” Oscar agrees, smiling a bit. “Too humble. You’re a good driver, Lando. You’re allowed to have some hubris.”

 

The words make Lando shiver. 

 

“I don’t even know what that means,” he says, making Oscar roll his eyes. “But how do you do that?” 

 

“Do what?” Oscar says. 

 

“Just say that kinda stuff so naturally,” Lando says. “Like, I know I asked for some reassurance. But you’re like, um, unaturally good at it. I don’t know. It makes me feel… It just sounds better when it comes from you.” 

 

Oscar coughs a little and has the audacity to blush. “I’m just being honest.” 

 

Lando sighs. God, that’s so much worse. 

 

He wants to give Oscar one of his cars. 

 

He kinda wants to sit at Oscar’s feet like a puppy dog. 

 

Kinda wants to crawl under this table and show Oscar how good he can really be. 

 

He looks down at his menu and develops a strong interest in cacio e pepe. 

 

“There aren’t that many points between you and Daniel, you know?” Oscar says, fiddling with his napkin. “Far less than you had to overcome with Max last season. We’ll get you that second title.” 

 

Lando looks up at him. Oscar’s not smiling, but he’s looking at Lando with such large, earnest eyes. Like he’s really trying his best to give Lando the reassurance he needs. 

 

It makes Lando sweat a little. “I know.” 

 

Oscar smiles a bit, clearly proud of himself. 

 

Lando’s mouth waters. 

 

“So,” he says instead. “What did you think of golfing with Carlos?” 

 

He doesn’t really want to hear Oscar talk about golf or gush about Carlos, but he needs to get his mind off the fact that he craves his engineer’s approval like a kid waddling after their teacher. 

 

He lets Oscar prattle on about golf for a bit before he asks a few questions to get Oscar talking about road cars until they’ve long finished their meals and the restaurant is starting to empty out a bit. 

 

Lando’s only thought about the definition of Oscar’s pecs under his polo or the veins in his forearms a few times, when the waiter comes around to drop off the check. 

 

Lando reaches for it automatically when Oscar makes a noise. 

 

“What?” he asks, reaching for his wallet. 

 

“We can split it,” Oscar insists. “Or I can just take it. You paid for the round today.” 

 

“Oscar, it’s fine,” Lando says, laying his heavy, black card down. He scrunches his brows together, confused. “Don’t worry about it.” 

 

“But that’s not fair.” 

 

Lando’s actually a bit stunned for a moment. He thinks his mouth may be dropped open like a fish. He’s not sure he’s actually had anyone offer to pay the bill for him in years. 

 

Max may have tried when Lando first started at McLaren, but there had come a point where his emotional support weighed more than any financial contribution. 

 

It was obvious he’d pay for his family. The ones who’d been there for him no matter what could get a free meal or two. 

 

And his team, whether he had any true power or not, all seemed to regard Lando as the boss. And the boss always paid. Unless he was actually with Zak or Andrea. 

 

Not to mention, regardless of gender, it was pretty much assumed Lando would pay for a date. And all the other things that came along with dating. He’s pretty sure the sales girl at the Monte Carlo Louis Vuittion saw him as often as the grocery store cashier did when he’d been with his ex. 

 

In fact, the only people who didn’t expect to see Lando’s card were other drivers. Alex, George, and he naturally found themselves covering things with no need for receipts or paybacks. And Max got paid quite enough from Redbull that he didn’t feel bad hopping on a private jet or spending a day on the yacht with little more than a thank you in return. Lord knows they all made enough money. 

 

It didn’t bother Lando to be expected to pay for things. He’d just accepted it as another part of being a driver. But now he was feeling a little miffed that it had simply become a truth of life, even if it was nothing to his bank account. The money was nothing. But there was something to be said about missing even the act of someone pretending they were on the same level financially. 

 

It made him feel so insanely normal that for a moment, it left him breathless. 

 

It also made him want to buy Oscar anything he’d ever desired in his life. 

 

“I can handle one dinner, Oscar,” Lando says. “You’ve been working hard. Let me treat you.” 

 

“I don’t know,” Oscar says, watching as Lando hands the check off to their waiter. “I’ll get our next dinner or something.” 

 

“Please,” Lando says, like it isn’t obvious that Lando makes way more money than him. “I mean it. I want to treat you. You deserve it.” 

 

It’s dark in the restaurant, lit by soft bulbs and candles, but even Lando can see the blush making its way back across Oscar’s face, spreading to the tips of his ears down his neck.

 

“Ok,” Oscar says, front teeth on full display in a soft, shy bunny smile. Lando’s chest feels heavy all of a sudden. “Thank you, Lando.” 

 

“Yeah, of course,” Lando says, pointedly not looking at Oscar and trying to make sure he doesn’t have a fucking heart attack in this restaurant. 

 

----

 

Oscar finds Jon sipping on tea in hospitality. He’s not really doing anything, but sitting and scrolling on his phone, but Oscar hesitates a bit, not wanting to intrude. 

 

Luckily, Jon makes the decision for him. 

 

“Can I help you, Oscar?” Jon says, looking at him with a brow raised. 

 

“Uh, yeah,” Oscar says, moving to take a seat at the table. “I just wondered how Lando takes his coffee.” 

 

“You want to know how Lando drinks his coffee?” Jon asks. Oscar nods. “Can I ask why?” 

 

“I just thought it’d be nice to get him one,” Oscar says, attempting with every bone in his body to sound casual and nonchalant. Evidently, it’s not working based on the way Jon’s looking at him. “Also, he bought me dinner a couple of weeks ago, and I thought it might be nice to do something for him.” 

 

Jon whistles. “Well, this is quite the change of tune from a month ago.” 

 

Oscar fights off the flush threatening to overtake his face. “Right. So the coffee?” 

 

“Well, if you ask him, Lando would say he doesn’t drink coffee,” Jon says with a laugh. “And he shouldn’t with all the energy drinks he consumes.” 

 

“Oh,” Oscar says, feeling a little defeated. 

 

It’s stupid. The coffee here is free. And in no way equivalent to a round of golf at the type of place Lando and Carlos golf, or the expensive meal he’d treated Oscar to. But Oscar just wanted to do something nice.

 

He’d been sincere in his offer to split the bill. He’s not an idiot. He makes good money. Great money even. But it’s nothing compared to the $50 million or so Lando makes. Not to mention, he doesn’t even pay his taxes. 

 

But Oscar’s polite. And frankly, he isn’t used to being treated to much of anything. 

 

The decisive manner in which Lando had pulled out his card and didn’t even expect Oscar to put on a pantomime of paying made him feel strange. 

 

Oscar was the older brother. He paid for his sisters. Oscar was part of the team. He split the bill or bought the next round when he went to restaurants or pubs with the rest of the engineering team. 

 

Oscar had the higher-paying job. He paid for dates and trips and gifts for his ex. 

 

It was just expected of him. There was no one who’d bothered to treat Oscar to anything since he was a child. 

 

But Lando doing it so casually. So naturally. So insistent. 

 

It made Oscar’s blood run hot. 

 

Made his stomach flit with something and his brain go static. 

 

It was a feeling he hadn’t felt much before. And he wasn’t quite sure he liked it. 

 

Or perhaps, he’d liked it too much. 

 

Either way, he decided he would pay Lando back in some sort of way. Clean slate. 

 

“Off the record,” Jon says, conspiratorially. “Lando likes iced vanilla lattes.” 

 

“Oh!” Oscar says. “Right. Ok. I can do that.” 

 

Oscar stands, watching as a small queue forms at the coffee bar. 

 

“Thank you, Jon.” 

 

“No worries,” Jon says, eyeing Oscar up like he might be a creature from another planet. “As a favor to me, though, get it with skimmed milk.” 

 

“Yes, right,” Oscar says, giving Jon a nod and dashing away. His face burns. 



----

 

Lando doesn’t want to call Canada a clusterfuck. But, it’s a clusterfuck. 

 

Miami had been alright at best. 

 

But to be lining up seventh on the grid while Daniel is on the second row makes him vibrate with the same frustration he’s been feeling all season. 

 

There’s a fucking Aston Martin in front of him. 

 

“Ok, Lando radio check,” Oscar buzzes in his ear. 

 

He sighs and presses the button. “Yeah, cool.” 

 

He takes a deep, long breath, exhaling into his helmet. He doesn’t want to be annoyed with Oscar already. It’s not really his fault. Even he can admit that. 

 

Oscar had warned him not to push as hard. To let the car do some of the work. To not try and bring it to the very edge. Have spent hours trying to give Lando new things to try and ease his discomfort with the car. 

 

But Lando’s eyes had endlessly drifted between the track ahead of him and the delta that never seemed to stop increasing. 

 

It’s a miracle he didn’t punch the screen of his wheel. 

 

Half a second off of Daniel.  

 

In the same car. 

 

Lando grinds his teeth. 

 

“Lando,” Oscar says. “Remember that we have the pace here. Get a good start, and we can play for a podium.”

 

“Yeah, I got it,” he says, feeling every bit like he doesn't. 

 

Lando’s stomach sinks as he watches George start the formation lap. Cold track. Low grip. None of Lando’s natural advantage here. All for Mercedes to win. Even Daniel might have some trouble, but at least he was starting at the front. 

 

It feels so typical of his season so far. Losing the comfort with the car. Losing the championship lead. Losing the head-to-head in qualifying. 

 

Starting from the back and fighting to the front. He’s sick of it. Sick of having to push and push to reach that sacred last step of the podium. 

 

He should be fighting for the win. 

 

Better, he should be flying miles ahead of the rest of the pack, thinking only about counting down laps and what he’ll write on the champagne bottle. 

 

He follows Fernando closely, weaving. Anything to put some heat in these tyres. His mirrors are filled with Charles’ red. 

 

Lando takes another deep breath as they round the last corner and settle back towards the grid boxes. He pulls smoothly up as close to the front of it as he can get. 

 

Lando eyes the grid tape on the wall. At least, Oscar listened to him on that. 

 

“Ok, last cars are coming into position,” Oscar says, voice calm and smooth. Lando thinks of the coffee he brought him this morning. Sweet and creamy. Unexpected. A good way to start his morning. 

 

Lando pulls his focus towards the lights ahead. 

 

Maybe he can give Oscar a good way to end the day. 

 

“Lando, you’ve got this. Go on and show me how good you are.” 

 

Right. 

 

Lando’s got a mission then. 

 

----

 

The entire pit wall is sitting in terse, stilted silence.

 

 Andrea watches with a foreboding sort of frown on his face. 

 

Oscar sits in unease as Lando and Daniel’s cars drift closer and closer. The times come down with each uneven flow of his breathing. They’re so close. Lando’s so close. 

 

He wants to get on the radio. Tell Lando to wait. Just be patient and wait for the opportunity. 

 

That he’s fighting for fourth place. The points matter, but so does finishing. That he has enough laps to make the move on Daniel and still chase after Antonelli. 

 

He had told Lando all those things, but the short, clipped responses told him everything. 

 

Lando was driving with frayed nerves. He was driving anxious.  

 

His voice shook every time he responded, sounding close to exhausted and worried. Something akin to a panic attack. 

 

Oscar swallows thickly. A sense of doom hangs over both his shoulders and lingers in the pit lane. 

 

He clicks on the radio. 

 

“You can do this, Lando,” Oscar says, trying to keep his own voice calm and steady. “Just wait for the right place to overtake. You know how we go racing. No contact.” 

 

“I know!” Lando all, but shouts in return. “I know that. I need more. More info on how to get around him.” 

 

Oscar looks over at Tom, who’s speaking rapidly into his own mic now. 

 

“You know the corners you’re faster out of,” Oscar says, hoping Tom isn’t listening. That he doesn’t remember where Oscar told Lando he was good or where he needed to improve. “If Daniel doesn’t make a mistake trying to catch Antonelli, you know where you can catch him.” 

 

Lando doesn’t say anything. Oscar watches them drift closer and closer. Sees Lando line up his overtake again and again before backing out or being blocked by Daniel. 

 

“We’re losing time racing each other,” Andrea says, speaking only to the pitwall. “If we get out of Antonelli’s DRS, we will lose out on the fight. Make sure they do not come in contact.” 

 

Oscar and Tom both nod, even as their eyes keep track of the screens. 

 

Lando lines up again. There’s space on Daniel’s right side as they come onto the straight. He can make the pass. He can catch up to Antonelli. 

 

His stomach twists in nerves. His mouth runs dry even as his palms start to sweat. Oscar wipes them on his trousers and hunches over his screen. 

 

“Keep it clean,” Oscar reminds him. “Don’t make a stupid move, Lando.” 

 

Oscar exhales. 

 

Lando goes left. 

 

He hits Daniel first before his tyre slams into the wall. Oscar watches as he nurses his car to the side of the road. Watches as Daniel drives off, holding onto his P4 by the skin of his teeth. 

 

“Are you alright, Lando?” Oscar asks automatically. He can see the bob of Lando’s helmet as he looks onto the track and back to his own steering wheel. 

 

“I’m sorry,” Lando says immediately, voice small and ashamed. “Sorry. That was my fault. Stupid from me.” 

 

Oscar feels Andrea watching the reply with simmering anger. 

 

“Are you alright though?” Oscar tries again. 

 

He hears a long, heavy exhale from Lando. “Yeah, I’m fine. I’m sorry.” 

 

The defeat so strong and evident in his voice makes Oscar want to rip his headphones off and take a hammer to this car himself. 

 

Instead, he takes a breath. “Yeah, safety car is out then. You can jump out when you’re ready.”

----

 

Oscar waits for Lando to come back from media in the chairs by the driver's rooms. 

 

Lando sees him, already downtrodden face setting into a stony expression. Oscar stands and opens his mouth to speak when Daniel walks in.  All the smiles and laughs from his interviews gone. His face is set in a hard, angry line. 

 

Lando intercepts him before he can slink off to his room. 

 

“Hey, man,” Lando tries, sincere and apologetic. “I want to say sorry again.” 

 

Daniel laughs, low and humorless. 

 

“You’re sorry?” he asks. “You almost ruined my race, mate.” 

 

“I know,” Lando says. “I just. I thought there was space.” 

 

“You thought there was space?” Daniel says, mulling the words over in his mouth. “Well, you thought wrong. We have one fucking rule here: don’t hit each other.” 

 

Lando swallows, nervous. “Ok, I know. I won’t do it again. I promise.” 

 

“Please,” Daniel sneers, face twisted in anger. The expression on his normally upbeat face might surprise anyone else. But Oscar, who’s been on the receiving end more than a few times, knows it well. “You think because you’re the fucking champion, we’re all gonna move out of your way.”

 

“No, of course not,” Lando protests. 

 

“Then learn how to drive, baby champion,” Daniel says. “Or I’ll have Andrea get involved.” 

 

Daniel says nothing else as he stalks off with his trainer. 

 

Lando stands there for a moment. He’s not crying, but his eyes are wet. And he looks like a lost lamb. 

 

“Lando,” Oscar says, getting his attention. He turns to him, and his face hardens. 

 

“No, Oscar,” Lando says, pushing past him into his driver's room. Oscar follows swiftly, letting the door swing shut behind them. “I don’t want to talk right now or debrief or whatever. Can I just have a minute alone before you get on my case?” 

 

“What?” Oscar asks, shaking his head. “I just wanted to make sure you were alright. I know you didn’t mean to crash into Daniel. It was just a mistake.” 

 

Lando scoffs. “Yeah, one mistake of many. This whole season has been a series of mistakes.” 

 

“Lando,” Oscar tries. “It’s ok.”

 

“No!’ Lando says, loud in the small space of his room. “You don’t get it. You could never understand the pressure I’m under. Everyone thinks it's so easy to be the champion. That it proves to everyone that I’m good, but it's the opposite. Everyone thinks I’m shit. That I won on team orders or because I had a good car. No one ever thinks it might be because of me. That I can do this.”

 

Lando paces the room, and Oscar watches as he visibly works himself into a small frenzy. The anxious, panicked energy from the car is still lingering. Oscar sighs. 

 

Of course, it was a stupid mistake. But it's not like other experienced drivers, champions even, haven’t done similar things. 

 

“Fuck what those people say,” Oscar says earnestly. 

 

“Why?” Lando says, wiping at his eyes. “You’re one of them. You think the car should do the work for me. That I'm the one making this all difficult. That I’m the reason we aren’t winning on this side of the garage. That’s why we haven’t won since Melbourne.” 

 

“That’s not true.”

 

“Isn’t it?” Lando says. “Tell me you don’t think the team favored me, and that’s why I won last year. In fact, I know it’s true because you wanted them to make me the number one driver. Will said so.”

 

“That’s not exactly what I said,” Oscar argues. “I know you won because of you, Lando. And I think you should be rewarded for it.” 

 

“No, you think I should be coddled for it,” Lando says. “God, you probably still fucking hate me, don’t you. Do you sit on that pit wall and just get so fucking happy when I fuck up qualifying or lose places at the start? Did you get a hard-on watching me fuck up my own race today? Thinking you know better than me.” 

 

Oscar shakes his head. “No, Lando. I- I want you to win. We’ve talked about this.” 

 

“Do you still want Daniel to win?” Lando asks, voice suddenly so high and scared. “Do you wish you were still on that side of the garage?” 

 

Oscar looks at him, truly looks at him. He’s almost shaking with frustration and anxiety. He’s looking at Oscar like one work could break him. 

 

“Lando, I only care about your side,” Oscar promises. “Our side of the garage. I only want to see you win.” 

 

“Fuck you,” Lando spits. “No, you don’t.” 

 

“Yes, I do,” Oscar insists, starting to feel a similar frustration. At Lando. At himself for being unable to convince him. For being unable to use his brain to help the man before him.

 

“You’re fucking lying,” Lando says. 

 

“I’m not!” 

 

“Get fucked.” 

 

He can’t stand this. Seeing Lando so frayed and alone. So unsure of Oscar. Of this fragile truce, they’ve built

 

This fragile relationship. 

 

Their olive branch is starting to crumble again. 

 

“Lando, please. We can talk about it.”  

 

“No, I- I can’t with this anymore,” Lando says. He stops and looks at Oscar. His eyes wide and pleading. Like he’s so distraught with himself, but still searching for something from Oscar. 

 

“I’m promising you that I want to be here,” Oscar tries, low and steady. “To watch you win.” 

 

“You don’t.”

 

“I do,” Oscar insists. “If you’d just listen to me.” 

 

Oscar can’t do this. Can’t go back to before. Can’t watch Lando crumble under the weight of his own expectations. Can’t go back to feeling useless and young and out of place here. 

 

He can’t go back to who they were in Melbourne. Not when he knows Lando better now. When he sees him. 

 

Lando shakes his head. Weeks of frustration and impatience getting the better of him. Clouding his vision and his senses. “No, you listen to me, Oscar. Go fuc-,’ 

 

Oscar moves forward and cradles Lando’s head in his hands. He promptly cuts off whatever Lando was going to say by pressing their lips together. 

 

All of the tension eases out of Lando’s body in an instant. 

 

A soft, relieved sigh brushes against Oscar’s mouth. 

 

Shit. He hadn’t meant to do this. To kiss Lando like this. 

 

But it feels so right, for lack of a better word. Like, the only way to get him to just shut up. To understand what Oscar's saying. To feel his sincerity, his desire to put Lando at the top. 

 

And Oscar is lost in it. In this man. 

 

He cradles the sharp cut of Lando’s jaw and slides their lips together in slow, soft movements. He feels Lando’s hands, huge fucking hands, come up to grip at Oscar’s waist, fingers tugging at the fabric of his team polo. 

 

Lando smells like sweat and fuel, and so fucking good, it clouds Oscar’s mind.  His lips part slightly, and his facial hair scrapes against Oscar’s chin in a delicious burn. He backs Lando up until his back hits the wall.

 

Lando goes so easily, it makes Oscar’s blood run hot. 

 

He could go crazy. He wants to tilt Lando’s head back so he can press his mouth along the smooth skin of Lando’s neck. He wants to bite the sharp jut of his collarbone. Wants to come back up and lick into Lando’s mouth so he can mesmerize the taste of him. 

 

It strikes Oscar with sudden, horrid clarity that he wants Lando. Badly. His body sings with desire. With the need to see and feel this man in every way. 

 

Desire nestles in Oscar's bones and fills every space left in his lungs until all he knows is wanting. Wanting Lando. Wanting his pleasure. His pain. 

 

Wanting him to feel a similar desire for Oscar and to just submit to it. 

 

He sucks on Lando’s bottom lip as his hand comes up to thread into his sweat-slick curls. Lando pants into his mouth. Oscar’s out of his mind. His fingers latch onto soft curls and pull.

 

Lando gasps before a pained sound tears out of his throat. 

 

The noise reverberates against Oscar’s mouth and hits him like a bucket of cold water. 

 

Shit, Oscar thinks. He reels back, letting his hands fall immediately from Lando’s body. Letting his gaze take in the red, swollen pout of his mouth. 

 

The dark, unreadable expression that’s taken over Lando’s face as he catches his breath. 

 

“I shouldn’t have done that,” Oscar says, immediately. He takes another step back from Lando, inching closer to the door. 

 

Oscar’s fucked. He just kissed his coworker. His driver. His probably straight driver, who is undoubtedly not interested in him. Based on their conversation, he probably still can’t stand Oscar. 

 

And Oscar’s weak. Let his stupid base desires bleed into his work. Let them force Oscar into acting before thinking. 

 

Stupid, Oscar thinks. I’ve fucked everything.

 

“What?” Lando asks, his brow scrunching up in confusion. “What just happened here?” 

 

Fuck, Oscar has to get out of here. Sweat begins to form at his temples and on his palms. He swallows thickly. 

 

“I’m so sorry,” he rushes out. “That was so unprofessional of me.”

 

Lando squints his eyes at him. 

 

“No, Oscar, it’s al-.” 

 

“It’s fine, Lando,” Oscar says, voice coming out high and weak. “I crossed a boundary. Let’s forget this happened. OK?” 

 

He wipes his sweaty hands on his pants and heads towards the door.

 

“Oscar, wait,” Lando tries. “Did I do something wrong?” 

 

He can't deal with that right now.

 

Oscar rips the door open. 

 

“I’ll see you at the factory, Lando.”

 

And he’s gone into the rush and mess of the paddock.

Chapter 6: Magic in Monte Carlo

Chapter Summary

Both boys deal with the Canada fallout, Oscar has a good idea, and it's time for lights out in Monaco

Chapter Notes

See the end of the chapter for notes

 

His head is spinning. 

 

Lando looks out the window of the jet, watching as the clouds grow thicker and obscure his view of Montreal. 

 

Thank god. 

 

He sighs in his seat, making Max look at him from across the aisle. 

 

“You alright, mate?” 

 

No. 

 

Lando’s not alright. He’s stewing in a terrible mix of anxiety and confusion. 

 

“Yeah, I’m fine,” he says instead. “Thanks for letting me catch a ride.”

 

Max waves away the words. “Don’t worry about it. Guessing you didn’t want to be with Daniel after all that?” 

 

Lando shakes his head. That’s one reason. The last thing he wanted to do was sit in silence on that McLaren jet, with Daniel and Andrea and Zak. He’d gotten more than a stern talking to during the debrief, and the hoard of disappointed looks was enough to make him want to run back to Monaco and never leave his apartment ever again. Bad enough to fuck up his own race, but to disappoint the team? It made his stomach twist. 

 

Not to mention the threat of repercussions hanging over his head. Whatever that means. 

 

Worst of all had been the way Oscar refused to look at him. Like seeing Lando so weak and distraught disgusted him. Like watching Lando make such an easy mistake proved him right. Like Lando was once again the problem. 

 

Not the car or Oscar. Just Lando. 

 

“Yeah, just needed a break from the team and everything,” he says, picking at a thread on his sweatshirt. “Wanted to lick my wounds alone.” 

 

Max nods like he understands. 

 

He doesn’t. At least Lando doesn’t think he would. Not the weight of disappointing his team or crashing into his teammate like that on such an obviously bad move. 

 

 He would never understand how all of it pales in comparison to the disappointment of his engineer. 

 

He highly doubts Max and GP have had these kinds of struggles. They bicker on the radio, but nothing compared to the all out fights he and Oscar have gotten into. 

 

Certainly, Max and GP hadn’t ended a fight like he and Oscad had. 

 

Lando looks back out the window.

 

Oscar kissed him. 

 

Just grabbed his fucking face and smashed their mouths together. 

 

And it was fucking perfect. 

 

The way he’d just taken control. It set Lando alight. How Oscar has held him and guided the kiss. How he’d pushed Lando up against the wall. How he’d pulled on Lando’s hair. 

 

Like taking control came so naturally to him. Like handing it over came so naturally to Lando. 

 

It makes him more than a little breathless just thinking about it. 

 

Until his stupid mouth had opened and ruined the whole thing. 

 

It’s not his fault, really. It’s just that the kiss had felt so good. Lando had wanted Oscar to pull harder. To tug his head back and just take what he wanted. To devour his mouth. To cage Lando in against the wall. He was helpless to do much but moan at the feeling of Oscar giving him everything he craved. 

 

Still, it seemed like the sound of Lando had reminded him who exactly he was kissing. And it spooked him. 

 

He’d said something about boundaries. 

 

That much Lando had gleaned in the haze that kiss had put him in. Something about crossing professional lines. Which is stupid. Lando crossed a professional line almost every time they spoke. And Formula 1 has never been known for being the most professional of industries. 

 

But maybe that had really been a facade. An excuse. Something to make Lando feel less like shit. Like Oscar had just kissed him out of some weird frustration and need to shut him up. 

 

Or worse, some strange form of pity. Like he could see Lando spiraling and doubting everything about himself, and kissed him just to make him feel better. To stop his endless spiral of self-loathing. 

 

Lando bites his lip. He shouldn’t care. He’s kissed plenty of people for the wrong reason, himself. But Lando and Oscar were not strangers in a club or friends of a friend. Oscar was his engineer, and he needed him if he wanted to win. 

 

Even pushing aside his overwhelming attraction, Lando knew getting too close might ruin them. Might push them two steps backwards and break the tentative bond they’d been building. And Lando knows he’s emotional sometimes. He takes things personally. That he could let this come between them. That he could let himself get petty and upset about it. Let it get in the way of a win. 

 

He sighs. 

 

He could overthink this in a million ways. 

 

Could dissect every word and action of Oscar’s. 

 

He pushes the thought back from his mind. 

 

He doesn’t want to fuck this up. No matter Oscar’s intentions or Lando’s feelings, he has to brush that aside if he wants to win. 

 

He has to stop overthinking. 

 

Hasn’t that been the problem all along? The reason he spent precious laps worrying about making the right move on Daniel. Thinking of every scenario and possible issue, only for him to make the worst choice of them all. 

 

The same with Oscar. Worrying so much about him and what he thinks, he’d acted like a child. Let his emotions get the best of him. 

 

Let him be petty and annoying until it made Oscar snap. 

 

That can’t happen again. 

 

The overthinking. The worrying. Add it to the endless list of things he needs to work on this year. 

 

 His therapist is gonna have a field day with this task. 

 

He takes a deep, slow breath. Ok, he’ll work on those then. 

 

He just needs some space from Oscar first. 

 

----

 

Oscar’s grateful to be hidden in business class in his own pod. Will is slumbering in the pod next to him, and Tom is a few seats over, watching some romcom. He thinks he might lose his mind if he were squished between his team in economy for seven hours. 

 

He just needs some space from everyone. 

 

He shifts in his seat, shivering under the thin blanket provided for him. 

 

He’s stupid. He’s so fucking stupid. How could he kiss Lando like that? 

 

Even if it was… god, it was perfect. The way Lando just folded under his touch. How he sighed so perfectly against Oscar’s mouth. How he touched him. How he tasted like energy drinks and chocolate and something very Lando. 

 

But the lack of self-control. 

 

God, Oscar wants to hit himself for being so foolish. 

 

He doesn’t even know if Lando likes him. Hell, he’s not even sure if he likes Lando. 

 

Sure, they’re working better together. Getting a better understanding of each other. Spending more time together. 

 

But, would Oscar call him anything more than a coworker? Could he see them being friends? 

 

He feels like he knows more about Lando than ever. Thinks he can read his emotions over the radio. Can tell when he’s upset or anxious or excited. Think he’s cracked the case on giving Lando the right amount of positive affirmation. 

 

Actually, since Melbourne, praising Lando and his performance had started to come as easily as breathing. It wasn’t like it was a foolproof method. Oscar wasn’t about to throw out everything he knew. He still needed to provide data and strategy, but he could see where Lando was excelling. Could tell where Lando was a good driver. An excellent driver. And telling him so gave him a decent burst of performance.

 

It was just the comfort with the car, they couldn’t figure out. An upgrade to the front wing was coming. Something Lando had spent the better part of a month pushing for. But was it enough? 

 

The confidence in the car still needed to be built. The confidence they had in each other still needed to be nursed. 

 

And Oscar had fucked it up. 

 

Because Lando’s so… so hmm. 

 

Because Oscar thinks Lando is so beautiful. 

 

Lately, it feels like Oscar’s been actually looking at Lando. Not just a driver. Not just someone Oscar works with. Not just Lando Norris. Just Lando.

 

And fuck, Oscar wants him. Craves his body and his mouth and every gorgeous noise he can coax from those pink, pouty lips. 

 

It’s so unprofessional. Will, who probably sees Lando like one of his kids, probably isn’t looking at Lando like this. No, he definitely isn't. No other race engineer on the grid would try that with their driver. Or want to. 

 

It’s just Oscar who’s fucked up. 

 

Just Oscar, who wants what he can’t have. 

 

Oscar slinks lower into his seat, adjusts his headphones over his head, and turns up the house music he has playing. It pulses in his skull. 

 

He feels so out of his depth. This is a problem he can’t solve with data or logical decisions. Even when his back was fucked, and his future was taken away from him, there were options. Choices to be made. 

 

But this. This is a mindfield of pure emotions. The championship. The car. Lando. 

 

It’s all becoming a jumbled mess. The job and his feelings for Lando all mesh together in a complicated disaster. He wants Lando to win as much as he wants him. 

 

But those can’t go hand in hand. He can’t want Lando and do his job well. Can’t force his own feelings on his driver. Make Lando uncomfortable during a season he already feels so rattled and astray. 

 

He’s got to find a solution for one of these sooner or later. He chews on his bottom lip. 

 

What a fucking mess. 

 

He scrunches his eyes closed and waits for exhaustion to overtake him. Anything to get his mind off Lando for just a few moments. 

 

----

 

Imola is forgettable. Better than Canada, a decent P2, and Lando finishes a decent length away from Daniel, but far from his best or most memorable performance. 

 

What is memorable is the way Lando seems to spend the whole weekend shying away from Oscar. 

 

He curses himself and his actions. Lando avoids his gaze at every turn and speaks to him only when he has to during debrief or on the radio. 

 

He dodges Oscar when they happen to walk into the garage at the same time. 

 

He takes his lunch up to his driver’s room when Oscar sits down for his own. 

 

He doesn’t even argue when Oscar gives instructions over the radio. Just takes them silently and drives. 

 

There’s a part of Oscar that wants to fight. That wants to corner Lando in his room and beg for his forgiveness. Or just anything to put them back to where they were, as fragile as it was. He knows Andrea is watching them like a hawk. Noticing how stiff and awkward they are, not fighting, but not talking either. 

 

“And that’s P2, mate,” Oscar says as Lando crosses the line. “Great job.” 

 

There’s silence for a long time. “Yeah, good job. Thanks to the team. Good work today. Upgrade is better.” 

 

And that was it. No banter or bickering or soft insults to Oscar. That’s how it had been from FP1 to now. No more speaking than necessary.  

 

It actually makes Oscar miss it. Miss the sharpness of Lando’s tongue. The heat of his voice over the radio. All that passion and feeling directed right at Oscar. 

 

Now it was just bland and emotionless. Everything Oscar thought he wanted during testing. But it’s nothing he wants at all, sitting under the Italian sun. 

 

Monaco has to be different. 

 

Oscar arrives in the principality with the rest of the team, the hot Mediterranean air making him sweat as soon as they cross the border from Nice and settle into their hotel. 

 

His room is nice. More than. A view of the ocean and the marina, bits of the track. The early June sun shining into his room. He looks out over the coast. Tastes the salt of the ocean in the air. 

 

He wonders what it’s like to live here like most of the drivers. To wake up every day to gorgeous views and a city filled with luxury at every turn. To race on the same streets that you drive on every day. He thinks about Lando, who is probably still at his own flat, enjoying the early afternoon. 

 

He didn’t use to give much thought to Lando outside of race weekends. After they left the track, Lando became data on a screen, onboards to watch, times to improve. But now, he wonders what Lando’s up to. What he does in his free time. What he looks like waking up on a lazy morning. If his curls are as unkempt as they are post-race, tussled and tangled. 

 

If he’s thinking of where Oscar is right now. 

 

He sighs and looks for his phone in his mess of a backpack. Lando’s not that far from Daniel still. 13 points. That was nothing. But he needed to get them back on track. Back to working together, or they might lose the close grip they had on the gap. 

 

Maybe it’s inappropriate to badger his boss for their driver’s number, but Will provides it almost instantly with a hoard of thumbs up emojis once Oscar lets him know he just wants to speak to him about more team building. 

 

Oscar

Hi, it’s Oscar. 

It’s a long 30 minutes before Oscar gets a response. Oscar spends it pacing the length of his hotel room.

 

Lando Norris - Driver

piastri?

wut do u want Oscar

 

Oscar bristles a little thinking of how many other Oscars Lando might know. Enough that he’s not saving their numbers in his phone. 

 

Oscar

Yes.

I was thinking we could talk? Before the race?

Andrea and Will think it’s a good idea to keep working on the teambuilding. 

 

It’s only a hint of a lie. Technically, no one ever said anything to him. But Oscar knows that’s the mood on the pitwall. That they’re still not getting along. That the fire of their arguments had turned to icy silence. Plus, Lando actually listens quite well to their bosses. 

 

Lando Norris - Driver

idk 

i dont really wont 2

were fine

 

Oscar tries to bite back the sting he feels. That Lando was so blatantly admitting he doesn't want to see Oscar. All because of his fuck up. 

Oscar

Please, Lando. 

I know what happened was messed up. 

But we need to work together

 

There’s nothing for a good ten minutes. 

 

Regardless of Canada, I still want to help you win.

I’ll do anything. 

 

Oscar’s not sure if that will work. If pleading desperately will do anything. 

 

Lando Norris - Driver

fine

I got some time on thurs morn before media 

U can com over w coffee or something

 

Oscar

Yes! That works for me!

 

Lando sends him his address, and Oscar tries not to breathe a sigh of relief. It lasts only for a moment before Oscar realizes he should be prepared to bring something good to the table to get their relationship back to where it’s supposed to be. 

 

----

 

Lando’s apartment is nice. More than nice. It’s in a building manned by a doorman who seems to be expecting Oscar when he rolls up at 9:15. 

 

The inside is tastefully decorated, and so Lando. It’s a mix of dark woods and fabrics and everything else Lando has seemingly had an interest in. There’s a wall of his old helmets near the windows that overlooks Monte Carlo and the marina below that. There’s a large gaming setup with multiple monitors and headphones. A simulator shoved in the corner. A personalized Monster fridge that seems to be stocked exclusively with Red Bulls. 

 

Despite the collection of items, it’s surprisingly well-kept and tidy. An opposite to Oscar, who for all his form and structure, has never really learned not to let his room be a bit of a mess of clothes. 

 

Oscar wonders if it’s so clean because of Lando or if he just doesn’t spend that much time here. 

 

Either way, Lando lets him in and welcomes him into his equally tidy kitchen, where a plate of pastries is sitting on the kitchen table. 

 

“Help yourself,” Lando says, casually. “The woman at the bakery gave me like a whole box as a good luck charm.” 

 

“Thanks,” Oscar says, sliding the iced latte he brought for Lando across the table to him. He sets his own flat white down and eyes the assortment before picking up a chocolate chip muffin. “Your flat’s really nice.” 

 

“Thanks, mate,” Lando says, sipping sullenly at his coffee. “So, what did you want to talk about? Some new strategy you’ve cooked up? You’re not gonna make me go on another run, are you?” 

 

Oh, so they’re playing this game. Pretending as if nothing happened. Oscar sips at his drinks, weighing his options. 

 

Isn’t pretending what he wanted? For Lando to forget. To act like Oscar hadn’t found him in emotional distress and just fucking kissed him like he was entitled to that. Like he hadn’t crossed a line. 

 

Oscar sighs. It’s what he wanted, but it feels wrong going into this weekend. 7 races since Lando’s last win. And this was Monaco. Boring as it may be, it was the goddamn crown jewel of wins for the teams and the drivers. 

 

But, to win? Here it would be all about Saturday. Here, Lando would need to go back to that spark that made him so good at qualifying. 

 

He’d had an idea on the way over. It hadn’t been far from the hotel to Lando’s building, and he’d had a decent 20 minutes to think. To try and come up with something. It might not repair whatever Oscar has fractured between them, but he could at least prove that he was trying his best to help Lando win. To help him get that second coveted title. To prove that he was a more than worthy champion. 

 

Not that he had to prove it to himself or Oscar, but he knew Lando still felt the need to prove it to the world. 

 

So, Oscar would help.

 

“I did have an idea,” Oscar says, cautiously. “Something to help you this weekend.” 

 

“Ok?” 

 

“It’s a little unconventional,” Oscar says. 

 

Lando sighs. “Have at it, Piastri.”

 

Oscar takes a deep breath.

 

“What are your thoughts on turning off your time delta on the dash during qualifying?” Oscar asks, watching as Lando’s face scrunches up. 

 

“How would that help?” Lando says. “The problem is me. And I doubt you think less data would help me.” 

 

“That’s exactly what I’m saying, though,” Oscar says. “You don’t need the data. It’s making you panic. When you see the time going up, you’re overcompensating in the next corner and the next and then the whole lap gets ruined.”

 

“You think I’m overthinking the laps because of the delta?” Lando asks. He contemplates this for a moment. “I could see that. But you think not having it wouldn’t make me worse? Wouldn’t I just worry until I get to see the time at the end?” 

 

“Well, I can’t say for sure,” Oscar says, he offers Lando a small smile. “As you would say, I’m not in the car. But I think it could help you just focus on what’s ahead. If you’re not stressing about the time, then you can actually focus on taking the corners, and when you make a mistake, you’ll be better focused on how to fix it next lap. It’ll be less about the actual time and more about the action.” 

 

Lando is silent for a moment. He sips at his coffee and looks at Oscar. A sort of searching look like he’s trying to understand something. Trying to read Oscar. Like, he still thinks there is some part of him that wants to fuck Lando over. 

 

“And you would be ok with that?” Lando asks, slowly. “With me having less data? Isn’t that like everything you’re against?” 

 

Oscar chuckles. “A bit. But if we want to win, we have to trust each other, right? You need the feeling while you drive. So trust me with your strategy on Sunday, and I’ll trust you to set us up in the right place on Saturday.” 

 

Lando narrows his eyes at Oscar. Tilts his head and takes him in. After a moment, he lets out a small, dry laugh. 

 

“Hm, you’re very mature, Mr. Piastri,” Lando says, grinning a bit. “Well, most of the time.” 

 

“Uh, um, yeah,” Oscar says, clearing his throat and willing away the heat starting to bloom on his cheeks. 

 

“Ok, I’m down to give it a try, mate,” Lando says. He exhales and leans back in his seat. Oscar averts his eyes as his shirt stretches up a bit, showing a sliver of tan, smooth skin. “Can’t be any worse than I’ve already been feeling.” 

 

“Oh, ok,” Oscar says, surprised Lando’s taken to the idea so quickly. “Great!”

 

Lando eyes him for a moment, drawing his gaze up and down Oscar’s body. He leans forward, resetting his elbows on the table. Words seem to sit at the tip of his tongue, but he pinches his mouth as if to keep them in. 

 

“Was there anything else you wanted to talk about?” he asks after a moment, looking up at Oscar through those dark, long lashes. 

 

Oh. No. Oscar needs to leave. Before he does something stupid like embarrass himself again. Or lunge across this table and kiss Lando again.

 

“No, uh, nope,” Oscar stutters out. 

 

“Really?” Lando asks, sounding a bit disappointed. “Alright. Guess I’ll see you at the track then.”

 

He looks away from Oscar, eyes taking interest in the view outside his window as his mouth sets into a firm, thin line. 

 

Oscar’s never felt more dismissed. 

 

----

 

It’s weird not having the delta on. Lando can’t stop himself from staring at the blank corner of his screen for the first few laps. Weird to have Oscar in his ear, telling him the sectors, he took the fastest instead. To wait until he crosses the line to see his final time. 

 

But it’s also freeing. 

 

Not knowing. By Q2, Lando doesn’t even look for the times. He just focuses. Take this corner like this. Brake here. Accelerate on this straight. 

 

Monaco has always been as tricky as she is beautiful. But without the delta, Lando feels like he can fully commit to each turn. Each braking point. Each kerb he needs to hit. Drives the hairpin easy and quick. 

 

“Ok, Lando,” Oscar says as the clock starts for Q3. “I think we’ll be able to go three runs on what we have. Daniel will go first, and then you can follow.” 

 

“Copy,” Lando says. 

 

When he beats Daniel’s first time by .067, he feels more himself than he has in a long time.

 

“Really good, Lando,” Oscar tells him. “We’re at 1:10:4. Come back in, and then we’ll go again in a bit. Provisional Pole for now.” 

 

On the second run, Lando feels invigorated. Like he knows these turns. Like he has nothing left to do but, focus on them and how to take them the best he can. 

 

“That’s 1:10:1, ahead of Daniel by .015,” Oscar says. “Still provisional pole. You can go again.” 

 

“Copy,” Lando says. 

 

“You’re doing really, really well, Lando,” Oscar says, soft and a bit stunned. Like, he can’t believe this is working. 

 

And his words settle so wonderfully in Lando’s brain. So perfect. Exactly what he needs. 

 

By the last run, he doesn’t have to think at all. No worrying about the time. No wondering if his lap is good. 

 

He just knows it is. Knows he is. 

 

He’s the last to cross the line, everything hinging on this last lap. 

 

“Holy shit,” Oscar breathes out once Lando does. “That’s pole, mate.” 

 

Fuck. It feels so good. Like a weight has been lifted off his shoulders. Like he’s finally where he should be. 

 

“Daniel P3, Leclerc P2, you’re ahead by .109,” Oscar says, voice cracking a bit, though Lando can’t be sure if it’s him or the radio. “New track record too. 1:09:9. First driver to do it under 70 seconds.” 

 

God, he feels good. Great, even.

 

Lando flips up his visor, letting the wind hit his stinging eyes. It’s crazy. He shouldn’t be crying over this. It’s just qualifying. Nothing he hasn’t done before. 

 

 But it’s Monaco qualifying. And he’s on pole. 

 

“Yeah, thank you all,” Lando says, trying to contain the break in his voice. It’d be more embarrassing, really, to be so emotional about this if it didn’t feel like everything he needed. If it didn’t feel like he could breathe for the first time in months. “Thank you, Oscar. We did it.” 

 

Oscar laughs over the radio, something low and delighted. “All you, mate. You'd better go on and win us a race tomorrow, too.” 

 

Lando pulls into parc ferme, pulling up behind the 1 board. He prays his tears are getting dried by the balaclava. He slips his visor down just in case. His mum will definitely start crying if she sees his eyes even the slightest wet. 

 

“Yeah, anything for you, Osc.” 

 

----

 

It’s a good win on Sunday. 

 

Not necessarily the most exciting, except for Lando’s lockup on the start that almost gave Leclerc the lead. But it’s good. Well, earned. And clearly something Lando needed. 

 

Something the team needed just based on how loudly cheers erupt around him as soon as Lando crosses the finish line. 

 

Kinda feels like something Oscar needed as well. 

 

“Ok, that’s P1 in Monaco,” Oscar says, over the radio. 

 

“MONAAAACOO BABY YEAH BABY.” 

 

Lando’s resounding answer is loud in his ears. He bites back a laugh, more than a little endeared by how excited Lando is. 

 

“Alright, good job,” Oscar says, trying and failing to sound as dry as possible. “Don’t let it get to your head.”

 

“Thank you, thank you, thank you,” Lando chants over the radio. “Seriously, thank you to the team. This is a dream. You’re all the best. Thank you. I love you all.” 

 

Oscar does end up laughing as he walks Lando through the ending procedure. Though he isn’t sure if Lando hears any of it by the way he continues to prattle on and on with gratitude. He watches on the screens as Lando parks his car in parc ferme. Watches as he climbs out of the car and stands atop, victorious. 

 

A feeling of pride reverberates in his chest. Not for himself or for the team. But for Lando. For his glorious, resplendent winner. 

 

He tugs off his headphones and slumps in his seat a bit, feeling a little like he was the one who was driving. 

 

It’s not often he lets himself wonder about what-ifs. What if he hadn't crashed? If he hadn’t fucked his back? If he made it to F1. If he were the lone Alpine crossing the line in P13. 

 

Or if he could be Daniel. Gracing the podium and driving alongside Lando. 

 

Or if he was Lando. Crossing that line in P1 and knowing for today, you were the winner. The sole victor. The king of the track for tonight. 

 

Part of him doesn’t think it would feel anywhere as sweet as it does right now, watching Lando shake his fist in the air and celebrate this win. 

 

He’s lost in the image of it.

 

“You’re getting along better.” 

 

Oscar looks over to see Will looking at him with a crooked smile. 

 

“Ah, yeah,” Oscar says, rubbing the back of his neck. “Guess the teambuilding is starting to work.” 

 

“Looks like it’s just works from my end,” he says with a laugh. “Mega lap from Lando yesterday and drive from him today.”

 

“Yeah, he’s been on it this weekend,” Oscar says, fighting back a smile. More than on it. Positively electric. 

 

“Heard the delta was your idea,” Will says, casually.  

 

“Yeah, but I mean, Lando still had to execute it,” Oscar says. Will looks at him with a sort of inquiring look. Nothing malicious, but rather like Oscar’s a funny joke, he hasn’t quite fully wrapped his head around. 

 

“Think it takes a good team to make these kinds of weekends happen,” Will says, lightly. “Guess we were right about pairing you two up. Good job today, Oscar.” 

 

Will claps Oscar on the shoulder as he leaves, following the rest of the pitwall to watch the podium. 

 

The words rattle around in Oscar’s brain. 

 

It kinda pains him how long they’ve spent arguing. Fighting and not seeing the other’s opinion. 

Cause when they’re together. When they’re in sync. He feels a bit like they’re unstoppable. 

 

Oscar watches as Lando receives hugs from his mechanics. From his parents and his friends. 

 

Watches as Lando finally gets weighed and takes off the helmet. Tugs off his balaclava. 

 

He smiles to himself. He was right. 

 

Lando does look really pretty when he wins. 

 

----

 

Fucking boat hospitality, Lando thinks as he stumbles back onto this massive yacht acting as their hospitality for the weekend. He’s not drunk. But it’s been a long time since he’s won. Almost three months now. 

 

A long time since he’s been able to smash his bottle on the ground and hear the national anthem and just close his eyes and soak it all in. 

 

So, he drank what was left in his bottle happily. The Moet, dry and crisp, as is slid down his throat. So cold and bubbly it almost burned. 

 

He’s euphoric. 

 

He won Monaco. 

 

His cheeks hurt from smiling so much. 

 

He drags his body back to his cabin. Daniel’s not come back yet. So there’s only a few people milling about on the top deck, but Lando is alone down here. He unzips his suit and tugs it down to his waist. 

 

“Fuck,” he says into the empty room, smiling to himself. He collapses on the clean, crisp bed. “I won Monaco.” 

 

“Yeah, you did.” 

 

Lando jolts up, his heart beating quickly, but calming once he sees Oscar in the doorway. He’s smiling too. And the sight of it is so nice. Those adorable bunny teeth peeking out. Lando wants to lick them. 

 

“Hi,” Lando says, a little breathless. 

 

“Hi,” Oscar says. “Congrats.” 

 

“Thank you,” Lando says. He heaves himself off the bed and makes to stand. “Ah, come in.” 

 

Oscar does, letting the door of the cabin swing shut behind him. 

 

Lando’s suddenly reminded of the last time they were alone in his driver's room like this. 

 

The vibe is completely different than Montreal. A sunny, luxury yacht miles away from the cold, stilted room he’d had in Canada. And he’s different. 

 

No longer encased in an endless loop of anxiety. Of worry. Of anger. At Daniel and the team. At himself. At Oscar. 

 

No. Today, Oscar’s the reason he’s got to step on the top step. The reason he’s just that much closer to Daniel in points. That he could prove he’s the reigning champion for a reason. 

 

And Oscar’s keeping a careful distance between them. 

 

If Lando were any less happy and relieved, he might feel more upset about it. 

 

Cause what else is there to say. Oscar made it clear. Kissing Lando was a mistake. And Lando had tried to find some space between them. Tried to put up that professional line that Oscar wanted. Even as hard as it was. Especially knowing how well they worked when they actually talked. 

 

It’s just the way he’s looking at Lando now, all soft and proud, makes Lando’s heart pound a little. 

 

“You were right,” Lando says. “About the delta. I felt like I was flying by the last lap.” 

 

“Wow, Lando Norris admits I was right about something,” Oscar says with a laugh. “Sure, you didn’t drink too much of that champagne up there?” 

 

Lando rolls his eyes. “Piss off. I mean it.” 

 

“I know,” Oscar says. “I’m glad it helped. But you were the one driving, so. That wouldn’t work with everyone.” 

 

“I guess,” Lando shrugs. “How’d you know it would work with me?” 

 

Oscar laughs as he shifts from foot to foot. “I didn’t.” 

 

“Oh wow,” Lando says. “Working with me has finally cooked your fucking brain. Maybe Max was right.” 

 

“Verstappen?” 

 

“Nah, my best mate,” Lando says. ”He’s surprised you haven’t quit on me yet. Thinks I’m an absolute loon at the best of times.” 

 

Oscar hums, looking around the cabin. “I don’t give up easily.” 

 

“I see that,” Lando says. He plays with the bracelets on his wrist. 

 

“I didn’t know if it would work,” Oscar admits. “But you’ve always been good at quali before. So I figured something was tripping you up. But maybe there’s something to be said about knowing less and feeling more.” 

 

“Wow, who are you?” Lando says. “Is this one of those things where your body got taken over by an alien?”

 

Oscar scoffs. “Well, not everyone’s as good at reading data and implementing it as I am. But a professional needs to learn to manage other working styles.”

 

“Jesus, never mind,” Lando says, throwing his hands. “You are still Oscar.”

 

“Plus, my strategy did stop us from getting undercut today,” he says. “So, I guess we are alright at working together.” 

 

“Better than alright, if you’re gonna help me get wins like this,” Lando says. “You’re right, I guess. The less I know, the better.” 

 

“I don’t think that’s exactly what I said,” Oscar replies, tone so dry and flat, it makes Lando smile. “I believe I just said it might help you not overthink.” 

 

“No, no,” Lando says. “I got it. No thinking. Just feeling. Now we’re on my vibe, mate.” 

 

Oscar rolls his eyes. “Sure, if that makes you feel better. We’re on your ‘vibe’ for Saturdays. And mine on Sundays.” 

 

Lando nods. “Exactly. You may be young, Oscar, but you’re not dumb.” 

 

Oscar shakes his head lightly, laughing to himself. “Sure.” 

 

Lando looks at him. Takes in the man before him. There’s late afternoon sun pouring into the room, the shadows of the ocean, creating a glistening effect on his skin. He’s sweat a little, making his fluffy hair, a little flat. And he’s obviously wrung the neck of his shirt out. His lips are bitten and chapped beyond belief. 

 

But Lando still thinks he’s quite handsome. 

 

His fingers itch to run through Oscar’s hair. Or to count all the freckles and spots on his face. To throw his arms around Oscar’s broad shoulders and just fall into him. 

 

“You forgot something, though,” he says. 

 

“What?” Oscar asks. Lando takes a breath before he draws closer. Oscar backs up automatically, but there’s little space in the room, meaning his back is up against the door in an instant. Lando tries not to feel a bit of glee at having Oscar trapped so easily. 

 

It had been a certain kind of torture having Oscar sitting in his home this week. Something about them sharing coffee and pastries. Oscar sitting so nicely at his kitchen table. For as stiff and awkward as Oscar has been, Lando had quite liked the image of him in his home. 

 

Had half a mind to do something stupid. Like plop himself right in Oscar’s lap. Or kiss him breathless. Or lead him right into Lando’s bedroom and not let him leave until Sunday. 

 

It isn’t an urge Lando has with many people. His home is a bit of a sacred space. A place to escape all the noise. Somewhere where he leaves the mask at the door. Where’s he’s just Lando. Even his ex had only been over a few times. 

 

Yet. Lando wanted to tie Oscar to his bed and never let him go. 

 

Wanted to wake up with him. Wanted to feed him all the chocolate chip muffins he wanted. Wanted to see how well Oscar might slot into his life outside of the track. 

 

The pure want of it all had scared Lando in the moment. 

 

Something he’d pushed to the back of his mind with the noise and crowd of a race weekend. But looking at Oscar here, so close and real, made it all bubble again. It was confusing to say the least. It’s not like Oscar even really likes him. 

 

But it feels like it sometimes. Like Oscar might feel even an ounce of what Lando does. Like, he doesn’t even realize how much more he sees Lando than anyone else has. 

 

Like they’re teetering on the edge of something more than just lust. 

 

Or at least Lando is. 

 

“You forgot to tell me how good I was,” he says, slow and sweet. Needy.  

 

Oscar huffs out a laugh. “I said good job.” 

 

“Yeah,” Lando breathes out. “I did a good job. But what about me? Was I good?” 

 

The for you goes unsaid. 

 

Oscar looks at him. He’s not that much taller than Lando, but with Lando in his boots and Oscar in a tall pair of sneakers, he’s able to look down at him. It makes Lando feel a bit small, towered over by a few cms and his large shoulders. He finds he kinda likes it. A lot. 

 

Lando draws himself up, bringing their faces close. Oscar takes a sharp inhale, but doesn’t move his face away. Practically encouraging Lando to try his best. 

 

“Yes,” Oscar says after a moment, low and dark. Accent thicker than Lando’s ever heard it. “You were so good for me, Lando.”

 

The words make him absolutely giddy. Higher than the champagne or the rush of the crowd once he’d gotten out of the car. The feeling only second to this win. 

 

His mouth waters a bit, and his heart pounds. It feels like blood rushes to his head. A bad current in the marina would send him toppling to the ground, given how dizzy he is from a few words. 

 

He leans closer, neck stretching for the last bit of distance. Until their lips are only a breath’s width apart. He watches as Oscar’s own gaze becomes a bit hazy. As his eyes flit down to Lando’s lips. 

 

His hand comes up and cradles Lando’s face without a second thought. The other one squeezing the bulk of Lando’s bicep through his fireproofs. His thumb sweeps across Lando’s cheek, still flushed red from the sun and the exertion. 

 

His eyes start to half close. 

 

Lando’s entire body tingles with want. 

 

“Are you gonna kiss me again?” Lando asks softly. The words come out light and devastating, brushing against Oscar’s own mouth and caressing his skin. 

 

He watches as Oscar’s eyes flit up to meet his own, expecting ones. 

 

He licks his lips, drawing Oscar’s attention back to them. The champagne on Lando’s breath curling sweet and intoxicating between them.  

 

“I- I,” Oscar stutters quietly. His thumb stops its movement across Lando’s skin. 

 

Lando can’t help as his mouth opens and forms a silent plea. 

 

Please.

 

He lets his eyes start to drift shut. Wanting. Waiting. 

 

There’s a loud thud above them. The sound of something falling, followed by a round of laughter as the team makes their way onto the boat. The sound of footsteps on the deck sounds loudly above them. 

 

The shock of it makes Oscar fall back. His hands immediately dropping from Lando. 

 

He mourns the warmth right away. 

 

“This,” Oscar starts, shaking his head. “This isn’t what you think it is.” 

 

“What?” Lando asks. He suddenly feels cold. Exposed. He stumbles back until his knees hit the bed. 

 

“We’re not doing this,” Oscar says. And Lando’s body tenses. His heart gets caught in his throat. 

 

So, he was right. That kiss was nothing more than a remedy to Lando’s incoming panic attack. Something born of pity and annoyance. 

 

As much as he thought it might be, the confirmation still stings. 

 

Lando’s been rejected a lot. More than one might think for a spoiled kid turned professional athlete. Rejected by older kids in karting for being so small and fragile, needing his brother to come and to act as his bodyguard. Rejected by mechanics and engineers (Oscar included) who thought they knew better, that his lack of schooling and slow nature of piecing his words together made him a fool. Rejected by people who he thought loved him for him, but only wanted a piece of his fame. 

 

He’s gotten good at taking it on the chin. Not letting it bother him. But the way his body can’t help but want Oscar feels beyond logic and control. Beyond something he can brush off.

 

Distance certainly hadn’t helped. 

 

Would Oscar’s pure rejection? 

 

It feels like it only makes Lando yearn for him more. For more of his attention and fleeting affection. For Oscar’s body to be close to his. 

 

For Oscar to want him back in any small amount. 

 

He swallows harshly. 

 

“Oi, where’s our race winner?” Someone calls from above. Oscar’s eyes go up to the ceiling before he looks back at Lando. 

 

“I should go,” Oscar says. 

 

Lando looks at his feet. “Yeah, ok. I gotta get ready for the dinner, so.”

 

“Yeah,” Oscar says, blandly. His face settles into a blank, unreadable expression. It makes Lando want to physically recoil. “I’ll see you at the factory then.” 

 

And isn’t that their favorite line?  Lando sighs, but nods. “Yeah, cool.” 

 

Oscar nods and opens the cabin door. The sound of the team celebrating filters in, loud and intrusive. A shock after the quiet of the two of them just shared. 

 

Lando looks at Oscar’s back. So tense and stiff, all covered in garish papaya. He hates the sight of it for the first time since he’s known Oscar. 

 

“Hey,” he calls softly. Oscar pauses and looks back at Lando over his shoulder. “Thank you for seeing me this weekend. For listening.”

 

Oscar’s eyes soften just a bit. He offers Lando a tight, closed-lipped smile. 

 

“Congrats again, Lando,” he says before he leaves, the door swinging shut behind him and closing with a soft click. 

 

—- 

 

They go out for drinks to celebrate. Oscar is squished in between a few of his mechanics at some bar overlooking the coast. It’s gorgeous. Breathtaking, really, if his mind weren't already somewhere else. He looks out over the barrage of apartment buildings that line the coast. Some with lights on. Some with dark windows. 

 

He’s sure Lando’s off at some club. Probably dancing with some model or celebrity. He’s probably off his fucking head, filled up with champagne and the thrill of winning fucking Monaco. 

 

Probably laughing it up with his mates about how pathetic Oscar is. How he’d kissed him and honestly thought Lando would forget it.

 

Oscar had hoped Lando would forget. Forget that Oscar made a fool of himself. That he’d let his body act on instinct instead of listening to his brain. 

 

He wants to beat himself up for being so stupid. And then he wants to forget the whole thing even happened. It seems like Lando won’t let him. 

 

Lando, who had looked so beautiful looking up at Oscar. Whose skin had felt so soft and smooth under Oscar’s touch. Whose mouth begged to be kissed. 

 

And yet, he’d made a joke of the whole thing. Oscar cringes, remembering how Lando asked if he was going to kiss him. It was said so soft and sweet, but Oscar can’t help but think Lando was just playing with him. Riling him up like he always does. Tugging at all of Oscar’s strings. It would hurt more if Oscar didn’t think he deserved it. 

 

For being so unprofessional. For thinking a world champion has any time or affection for his engineer. For wanting Lando so much when he shouldn’t. Lando, who’s likely drunk in some VIP section, with Oscar far from his mind. 

 

Oscar wonders if he’ll go back to a hotel with some singer who’s made her way here for the race. Or some pretty model who’s got a night off. Or some friend of a WAG who’s looking for paddock passes and a pretty boyfriend. 

 

Or will Lando wander back to his own place? Sleep in his own warm sheets and wake up at home with another trophy to add to his shelves. A smile on his face as he wakes up a winner. 

 

However briefly, Oscar imagines himself there. He’d only gotten a few glances from the short time he was there, seeing only the living room and the kitchen. But he can see a life where he’s nestled in sheets that smell like both of them and bathed in soft light, waiting for the sound of the door unlocking. Welcoming Lando home with soft smiles and even softer embraces. 

 

Sitting in the kitchen and sharing breakfast. Brushing their teeth together in the bathroom. Lounging on the balcony and watching the sun set over the ocean, feeling warm and homey and together. 

 

It’s so stupid. Oscar swallows his old-fashioned down. The taste of it is bitter and burns his throat harshly. He needs to get out of here, suffocated by people and his own thoughts. He makes his excuses and accepts a few rounds of pats on the back. 

 

He walks back to the hotel, taking in the salty air of the ocean and the sound of people partying in the harbor. 

 

He wonders what it might be like to wake up in soft coastal sunlight, holding a winner in his arms. It makes his chest ache. It feels wrong to imagine all this when it’s not fair to either of them. When it’s completely out of the realm of possibility. 

 

When he returns to his room, he goes through the motions of washing up and slipping into the stiff, cold sheets of his hotel bed. His head practically on the mattress from how thin the pillow is. 

 

He imagines more expensive pillows and a less empty bed. His mind and his hand drift as the ideas swirl. Warm, sun-kissed skin. Sweaty, messy curls. Oceanic eyes rolling back in pleasure. Bitten lips offering whiny, perfect pants and moans. Athletic hips and thighs moving and rolling like they can’t stop, even if they wanted to. 

 

Puppy dog eyes looking up at him and wishing for the next command. Aching to be good for Oscar. To trust his hands to guide him perfectly. To take his words like gospel until he felt so soft and free, he had no choice but to give Oscar everything. 

 

It’s a perfect picture in Oscar’s mind. Easier to swallow desire than domesticity. 

 

He sees it all so perfectly in his mind. The phantom of his scent making Oscar’s mind hazy. The taste of him sits on his tongue. So real and vivid, but so far and out of touch. His hand speeds up. His heart pounds. His mouth runs dry. 

 

Lando’s name falls from his mouth like a prayer. 

 

Like a plea. 

 

Like if he says it enough, it’ll manifest his fantasy into reality. 

 

He dirties his hand in a few hurried, desperate strokes. 

 

He collapses onto his back shortly after, feeling more unsatisfied than ever. A craving for his driver scratching his brain. A chasm of want opening in his chest. Catching his breath from nothing but a figment of his imagination. 

 

The sound of an engine tearing through the streets echoes in the quiet of his room. 

 

Oscar’s sick of thinking about cars and engines and drivers.
