Chapter 7: Silver(stone) Lining

Chapter Summary

Lando goes on a good run of races, there's karting, and summer break starts

Chapter Notes

See the end of the chapter for notes

Monaco makes him a new man. 

 

At least in terms of the car. 

 

A certain confidence seems to fill Lando as they head into the last run of races before the summer break. 

 

He takes his P2 in Barcelona on the chin, congratulating Daniel and looking significantly less downtrodden in the post-race interviews than he had in the first run of races. 

 

Austria, however, is a masterclass. Lando secures pole beautifully. Over half a second between him and Leclerc. 

 

And his win. 

 

God, it makes Oscar a little lightheaded just thinking about it. A perfect win. 

 

Even if Oscar spent the first half of the race with his stomach in knots, watching Lando and Daniel drive side by side. 

 

“Lando, make sure we don’t have any contact,” Oscar says, relaying Andrea’s strict orders over the radio. “Clean race and we can win this.” 

 

“Yeah, got it,” he says, sounding so casual and calm, Oscar almost double-checks that he actually clicked onto Lando’s radio and not someone else's. 

 

But then Oscar watches them go wheel to wheel. Watches as Daniel overtakes Lando for a few seconds. Well, he watches as Lando lets him pass, so he can get into his DRS and take the inside line. He leads again on the next corner.

 

There’s something irrevocably wrong with Oscar that he finds that so fucking hot. 

 

It makes his blood pound. Makes sweat start to form at his temples. Makes his mouth water watching as Lando makes such clean, decisive moves, not letting Daniel bother him. 

 

Fucking hell, he’s really down bad if he’s even starting to get turned on by Lando’s fucking wheel-to-wheel. 

 

“Ok, really good job,” Oscar says, watching as Daniel locks up. A terrible sense of satisfaction brewing in Oscar’s stomach, seeing smoke on the tyres and a flatspot forming. “You’ve got this, Lando. Build a bit of pace between you and the other car now.” 

 

“Yeah, copy,” Lando responds. 

 

And away, Lando goes. 70 laps of stress for Oscar. But Lando is gorgeous behind the wheel. 

 

Oscar never paid that much attention to Lando last season, unless his race affected Daniel. A mistake. An absolute lapse of judgment for not paying attention before.

 

Now, watching him drive so well. Take a win so brilliantly. 

 

It feels like watching a completely different person from the spring. Like a homecoming. Like the king come to get his throne back. Like a champion fighting for his title. 

 

 And Oscar gets to be a part of it. Gets to guide Lando and work the strategy. Gets to watch as Lando disregards some of it and drives like he was born to. 

 

Oscar feels privileged to be on this side of the garage. Feels like it might be where he was meant to be in the first place. 

 

If only their relationship off the track was working as well as it was on. 

 

Since Monaco, Oscar finds himself caught between wanting to avoid Lando and wanting to drift endlessly in his orbit. 

 

Like, the feeling of being so close to him. To openly allow himself to want him had unlocked something in Oscar. A new sort of stress took him over. He still wanted to keep their relationship professional. At least, while they were working so well together. With the championship lead still so close, it felt like Oscar needed to double down. Be the professional he was hired to be. Be the race engineer Lando needed. Dispel any unnecessary tension between them. Ward Lando off from pulling any more of his threads.

 

Or he might unravel completely. 

 

Because he just wants him so strong and fully. 

 

It’s not even really fully comprehensible to Oscar how he went from barely tolerating Lando to seeking any bit of him he could. Maybe it was the easy way he smiled at Oscar after a good session. Or the way, he’d started to actually listen to what Oscar was saying. More and more, Lando would let Oscar’s words hang in the air after he finished talking as he digested them, took them in without the tone, and let them settle in his brain. 

 

Maybe it was the way Lando had started sending him more of his notes after sessions, looking at the data and pointing out where he could do better. Not in a self-deprecating way, but in an analytical way. More of a job than a beating. The way Oscar might look at the data. 

 

Or maybe it was just the way that watching Lando drive so well felt like falling in love. 

 

Not love, not really, but some bright, soft fondness that settled in his chest when he knew Lando was satisfied in the car. How he wanted to make sure that every time Lando hopped out, there was still light in his eyes. That he was having fun. Remembering why he’d worked so hard to be here. 

 

Sometimes the feeling was so suffocating that Oscar wasn’t sure if he’d make it to the end of the season. If he could handle being this close to Lando. Being the voice in his ear. The angel and the devil on his shoulder. The one Lando relied on. 

 

Be all of that and not have him in the way he wanted. It felt like being at a zoo sometimes. Watching something so wild and beautiful, but being trapped by glass. So close and yet never allowed to touch. Like he was made to watch as a force of nature lived so strongly without him. 

 

Still, the championship. Both of them. They could be enough, Oscar reasoned. Better to have his driver trust him. Rely on him. Better to make a champion than entangle them in a mess of Oscar’s feelings. 

 

Lando didn’t need that. Didn’t want that. 

 

He wants to be a two-time world champion. He wants a second Constructor's. He wants to win.  

 

And when Lando stands atop his car, the champion for a second time, Oscar will let himself be satisfied with that and slink away at the break. Let himself look for open doors while he nurses his wanton heart. 

 

----

 

Lando isn’t sure what fucking geniuses in the marketing department cooked this idea up. 

 

But he waits in a race suit, swinging his helmet around as a crew sets up cameras around him. 

 

It’s nothing against karting. Honestly, Lando loves to go karting. It makes him feel like a kid again. Like that little boy who was just chasing a dream and a hope. 

 

It’s just not something he does with Oscar Piastri.  

 

“Couldn’t we have gotten Alex or something?” Lando asks, as one of the marketing girls affixes a mic to his suit. “Or Carlos?” 

 

“It’s best we keep it to current McLaren personnel only,” she says. “Plus, people online are loving you and Oscar together.”

 

“Really?” Lando asks, raising a brow. He finds that a bit hard to believe. Max hasn’t said anything to him about it. Nor his sisters, who loved to torture him with whatever online jokes and memes Lando had gotten himself tangled in. 

 

Though his manager had spent an inordinate amount of time sizing up Oscar in Austria. So much so, he’d had to tell Mark to cool it before he made Oscar more uncomfortable than he probably was. 

 

They hadn’t talked about Monaco or Canada before that. Hadn’t talked about how Oscar had given Lando a taste of what he’d really wanted and then just walked away. 

 

Instead, Lando has swallowed everything he wanted to say and just focused on the car. On driving better than he had before. On himself. Not Oscar or Daniel or Max. Just him and doing the best he could do. 

 

His therapist was proud of him for getting some of his spark back. For working through some of the anxiety that had plagued him for most of the season. She ended their last session with a satisfied smile and a reminder for Lando to keep up with his journal.

 

 He’d neglected to tell her about Oscar. About the kiss. About the new tangled mess of feelings that was occupying his mind, off track. But he figured that was a conversation for the postseason. Something best left for when he could breathe over the break. 

 

He looks around the karting track. At the mill of people. Does everything have some sort of opinion on him and Oscar? Can they all see what’s going on behind the scenes? Or is it just some internet fodder? 

 

“Duh, fans love you guys,” the marketing girl says, like Lando has any idea what's popular with online Formula 1 fans anymore. Finds it hard to believe anyone online likes what he’s doing. “They’re calling you guys Landoscar! It’s cute. Everyone likes seeing you both start getting along better.”

 

She looks like she wants to say something more, but bites her lip. 

 

“People care about that?” he asks. 

 

“It’s like an enemies-to-lovers thing,” she says, a bit embarrassed. “Not that you and Oscar are lovers or anything! But it’s a good story. For your title campaign too. And I mean… two young, cute guys. You’re bound to be shipped together.” 

 

Ugh. Lando fights not to roll his eyes at this girl just doing her job. Instead, he says nothing and lets her mic him up. 

 

He’s not quite sure that they’re really getting along. They do over the radio. And in debriefs. 

 

But beyond that. Well. 

 

Oscar makes his head fucking spin. The way he looks at Lando, like he has something to say, but would rather take a step back and keep it inside. It’s infuriating. It makes Lando want to open his mouth and make a mess of himself. 

 

He’s not sure if it’s just because Lando hasn’t met someone who’s really gotten him in a long time, but he can’t stop the way he still feels so drawn to Oscar. Even after all the bullshit. Lando still craves him. 

 

And apparently, it must be written all over his face if people on the internet are fucking shipping them together. From what? A few radio messages and footage of them in the garage. 

 

He can’t even imagine the field day Twitter is having with his constant need for reassurance. Surely, he sounds like a pathetic puppy over the radio, whining and begging for Oscar to tell him he’s good. 

 

Though he hasn’t had to ask in a long time. Oscar just offered praise and affirmation like it was so easy for him, like it was second nature for him to work Lando into a tizzy from a few words. 

 

No wonder Oscar pities him. He must be so fucking obvious. 

 

He thinks of George and Alex waggling their eyebrows at him during the last race as Oscar had come to find Lando before the anthem. They’d both looked between them and snickered like school children. Lando had written it off at the time, usual theatrics from the pair, focusing on what Oscar had to say. 

 

God, did everyone in the paddock know Lando had a pathetic little crush on his race engineer? 

 

“Hey.”

 

Lando looks up. His breath catches.

 

In order not to lose his head, Lando had sworn off looking up videos of Oscar in F3. Seems like it was the right decision as Oscar walks towards him, clad in a black racesuit, a generic McLaren helmet in his hands. 

 

He looks good. Good enough, that Lando’s a bit thankful that he sits on the pitwall in the same basic blank pants and polo as the rest of the team. 

 

He can’t even imagine if Oscar made it up the ranks. If he was on the grid. Maybe, if he were in Daniel’s place, standing next to Lando in the same papaya suit and tight black fireproofs underneath. He thinks he would have lost his mind. The thought of the broad, delicious back in tight Nomex. 

 

If the internet thought he was down bad for his engineer, they’d hate to see how pathetic Lando could get if Oscar was his teammate. 

 

“Hi,” Lando greets him, biting back his thoughts. “How’d they scam you into this?” 

 

The marketing girl fixes his mic and huffs away, clearly a little offended. Oscar watches her go with an amused smile before looking back at Lando. 

 

“There are certain drawbacks to being in a high position,” Oscar says, shrugging. “Doing some marketing is part of it. I'll say this is a bit better than just doing a boring interview.” 

 

Well, Lando can agree with that. As awkward as this might be, being paid to kart instead of some bullshit sponsorship video was better. He looks at the two karts acquired for them. 

 

Oscar stretches out his body, drawing Lando’s eyes to the long, muscled line of his shoulders. 

 

“And your back will be ok?” Lando asks, concerned. The last thing he ever wants is for Oscar to risk any sort of injury. Lando’s back is as fucked as it can get in F1, but he has access to special mattresses and Jon’s massages. But, Oscar. Even with his good salary, he can’t get a new back if it’s permanently fucked. 

 

Oscar lets out a quiet, amused chuckle. 

 

“Yeah, I’ll be ok,” Oscar says with a soft smile. “My Toyota goes faster.” 

 

“Alright, well, don’t push it,” Lando says, still a bit worried about him. “Can’t have my engineer throwing his back out like a grandad.” 

 

“You think you’ll push me that hard?” Oscar says, a sort of challenging glint in his eyes. “I was a karting champ in Australia.” 

 

“Ok? I was one too,” Lando scoffs. Oscar chuckles, something low and smug. “Fuck all my stats then.” 

 

“Language Lando!” Someone calls to them, but Lando’s only looking at Oscar.

 

Suddenly, Lando wishes it were always like this. This easy. This back and forth that comes so naturally. That Oscar could speak to him softly and blush so beautifully all the time. 

 

Lando swallows down the knot in his throat. 

 

“Ok, world champ, show how you get to where you are then,” Oscar says, tugging his helmet on. 

 

Never one to back down from a challenge, Lando tries his best. 

 

There’s something about being back in a kart that always makes Lando feel a bit nostalgic. For the little kid who just wanted to race. To be the best. 

 

By all titles, Lando is the current best. 

 

But that little boy would never be satisfied. He’d be racing against bigger kids, fumbling along behind his dad and brother to the track, dreaming up a thousand what-ifs. Hungry for the next step. Believing he could be a multiple-time world champion. Like Seb or Lewis. 

 

Like Lando is hoping to be right now. 

 

So he doesn’t phone it. And he certainly doesn’t intend to let Oscar show him up. 

 

It’s just that Oscar’s fucking good. Actually, insane, even in a kart.

 

Lando wins their little race by the skin of his teeth, trading the lead with Oscar corner after corner for twenty laps. 

 

Twenty devastating laps for Lando.

 

If Oscar was hot in a racing suit. He’s even hotter overtaking Lando. 

 

On the 10th lap, he nearly pushes Lando into the grass and Lando has to fight the moan threatening to tear out of his throat. 

 

On the last lap, he passes Lando on the first corner, making Lando fight for the win until the very end. It’s annoying as it is arousing. The competitive nature making Lando hope and pray there isn’t a tent in his suit when he gets out of the kart. 

 

“Fucking hell,” Lando says, ripping off his helmet once they're done and ignoring the marketing team's noise of distress. Oscar gets out of his own kart and takes off his helmet. 

 

His hair is all messed up, sticking up in odd directions, but there’s a pleased, smug smile tugging at his lips. 

 

“You sure you’re at your full potential today, Lando?” Oscar asks. 

 

“Why the hell didn’t you say you were actually good?” Lando asks, trying to flame down the irritation and desire that seeing Oscar like this invokes in him. 

 

Oscar all proud and sure and cocky. All full control of himself and his abilities. 

 

Lando hates it. He fucking loves it. 

 

“Mate, I did," Oscar says with a laugh. “You know how the feeders go. Gotta be good at karting first to get into the other categories. Or does that feel like a long time ago for you? You’re not exactly young anymore.” 

 

“The fuck?” Lando scoffs. “I’m like barely a year older than you.” 

 

“Almost two, actually,” Oscar corrects. “I think I saw a gray in your goatee the other day.”

 

Lando rolls his eyes, hand coming up to the scratch at the hairs on his chin. He’s definitely going to shave before media day. 

 

“Then respect your elders,” Lando says. 

 

“Sure,” Oscar says. “You were quite good out there.” 

 

A shiver racks through Lando that he hopes doesn’t come across on camera. 

 

“Thank you,” Lando says, satisfied. 

 

“Hm, quite good for an old man,” Oscar says, eyes sparkling in amusement. 

 

“You’re so fucking fired,” Lando says, making Oscar throw his head back in laughter. “Someone call Andrea.” 

 

Luckily, for Lando, their crew seems to enjoy the banter and deem it enough for them to end the shoot. 

 

A PA guides both of them inside, leading them to a holding room while they sort out Lando’s transport and schedule.  

 

Lando and Oscar huddle in the room, they’d laid out as a green room, shedding their suits to their waists and pulling on sweatshirts. His is some expensive, Kith thing Max gave him for his birthday. But Oscar’s tugging on a team sweatshirt. He eyes the LN1 on his chest. Ignoring how it makes his heart pound a little. 

 

“So did you start in karts as a fucking baby then?” Lando asks, once they’re alone again. 

 

Oscar shakes his head with a laugh. “RC racing, actually. My dad got me into it, and then I was pretty good, so we thought we might try actual racing.” 

 

“RC cars?” Lando says. “Like against other kids?” 

 

“No,” Oscar says with a laugh. “A bunch of adults, actually. Well, there were some kids, but they weren’t as good as me.” 

 

Lando loves the way confidence looks in him. Away from the pit wall. He wonders what confidence looked like on Oscar behind the wheel of a single seater. 

 

Wonders what could have been if not for Oscar’s crash. 

 

“Do you miss it? Racing?” Lando asks after a moment, the curiosity biting him. More than vaguely aware he might be coming off as insensitive. Oscar shrugs, unbothered.  

 

“No,” he says, honestly. “I mean, I did. Used to miss it a lot. But it’s been a long time since then. And I’ve got a new life now. Plus, I still get to be a part of racing.” 

 

“Sorry,” Lando says. “I just can’t imagine it.” 

 

Oscar shrugs.

 

“I’m sure if I made it to F1 or had a contract or something, then I’d be worse off,” Oscar says. “But I made my amends with it. That’s sport, isn’t it? Sometimes you make it, sometimes you don’t. I mean, even if I got to F2, there’s no guarantee I’d make it to the next step.” 

 

“If you drive like you kart, then I have no doubt about it,” Lando says, honestly. 

 

“Thanks,” Oscar says, smiling. “It’s alright though. Really. I mean, I think my dad’s more upset than I am about the whole thing.” 

 

“Why?” 

 

“Just... he invested a lot in my career,” Oscar says. “And then I get injured and have to quit? It’s not like it's ideal. I mean, I’m basically the reason my parents are divorced.” 

 

“What?” Lando asks. “There’s no way that’s true.” 

 

“I guess not technically,” Oscar says, scratching at his face. “But my dad knew I wanted to do this and that I had the potential. So, he enrolled me in boarding school and brought me to England, and then he went back to Australia once he knew I wasn’t gonna like crash out or anything. Didn’t tell anyone else that he was gonna leave me here. And mum. She obviously didn’t like that.” 

 

Lando looks at him. Oscar says the words so casually. So nonchalant. But Lando takes a moment to soak them in. To fully hear the words outside of the tone. And they’re sad. Regretful. 

 

“That’s not your fault, though,” Lando says. “You were just a kid. Sounds like your dad’s kinda a dick. I’d divorce him too if I were your mum.” 

 

Oscar lets out a soft laugh. “Yeah, I guess so.” 

 

Lando leans forward and looks at Oscar. “You know it’s not your fault, right?” 

 

“I suppose in the abstract,” Oscar says. “Or like when I take a step back. But sometimes it still feels like if I hadn’t wanted to be a driver, then maybe they’d still be together. Or if I’d made it to F1, then at least it would be worth it.” 

 

Oscar shrugs again like it doesn’t bother him, but Lando can see the stiff line in his shoulders. 

 

“Your family’s sacrifices were still worth it,” Lando insists. “Even if you didn’t make it past F3, you still had to try, right? I can tell you were good, Oscar. Talent worth taking chances for.”

 

Oscar hums and offers Lando a small, grateful smile. “I suppose so.” 

 

“I know so,” Lando insists. “Plus, now you’re with me. Which is a pretty good consolation prize, I think.”

 

He means it as a way to lighten the mood, but Oscar fixes him with a deep, sincere look, making sure to maintain eye contact. 

 

“I like being here,” Oscar says. “I’m ok to have closed that chapter of my life, Lando. I’d much rather be a part of your story.” 

 

It’s sweet. Something that makes Lando fidget with feeling. Even as another part of him ponders the meaning. How can Oscar say something so carelessly kind like that? Something so devastating when he’d acted like Lando was a bother a few weeks ago. 

 

When he kissed him like it was nothing. 

 

Was it nothing?

 

The way Oscar’s looking at him makes him feel like he’s the main character in Oscar’s current chapter. Makes him feel like there’s something he’s been missing. 

 

Like an itch has covered his body until he figures it out. 

 

“Oscar,” Lando starts tentatively. Curiosity clawing at his chest. “When you, um, in Canada? Was that out of like pity or something?” 

 

Lando’s aware his cheeks are heating up, but he has to know. Has to know what Oscar meant when he kissed him. If it meant anything at all. 

 

“Pity,” Oscar says, testing the word in his mouth. “Do you think that?” 

 

He doesn’t sound angry or mocking. Rather, Oscar sounds like he’s almost in a bit of disbelief. Like Lando just said something crazy. 

 

“I don’t know,” Lando says, running a hand through his hair, settling it on the back of his neck. “I know I was kinda spiraling. And I’m sure I was being a bit of a dick. So, I thought, maybe, you just wanted to shut me up or something. Or like it's just your own weird way of like trying to calm me down.” 

 

Oscar looks at him. He bites his lip for a moment. An answering blush starting to color his face and the tips of his ears. 

 

He exhales loudly. 

 

“It wasn’t pity,” Oscar says, finally. “I’ve never pitied you, Lando. Maybe it was my own way of trying to bring you out of the spiral you were in.” 

 

“Oh,” Lando says. He sighs and folds into himself a bit. Ok, that makes sense. It’s not exactly what he wants to hear, but it's better than feeling like a charity case. 

 

“But, I also did it because I wanted to,” Oscar says, making Lando’s head snap up. 

 

“I-,” Lando starts, words falling away from his brain for a moment. “ Really?”

 

Oscar shrugs, lips forming into a tight line with the corners upticked. “I don’t think either of us is really the type to do something we don’t want.” 

 

And it’s true. Isn’t that what got Lando and Oscar into most messes? That they were both inflexible. Both so used to their own way of doing things. Both lone icebergs with an ocean between them. 

 

Secretly, Lando felt like he was starting to thaw a bit. And maybe, Oscar was too. 

 

“No,” Lando says. “But you make me want a lot of things I’m not used to, Oscar.” 

 

Lando’s never been religious, but he imagines this is what going to confession is like. Flayed out and vulnerable. Waiting in silence for judgment. For penance. 

 

It sends a shock of fear through him, turning his blood cold and his stomach fluttering. 

 

His words hang in the air between them. 

 

Oscar looks at him, wide-eyed and clearly a bit taken aback. Lando wants to run from this room. Wants to get into a car and go back to his hotel and curl up on himself. Wants to make a joke out of it. Wants to do anything to go back to five minutes. 

 

But Oscar’s face pulls back into a soft, gentle smile. The one Lando’s starting to realize might just be for him. 

 

“Yeah, I feel the same,” Oscar says. And the words aren’t that crazy. Not a love confession or anything like that. 

 

It’s just that they fill Lando up with a sort of lightness he hasn’t felt in weeks. 

 

“Oscar,” he says, fondly. 

 

Oscar licks his lips and looks at Lando. “Lando, do you-,” 

 

“Hey, guys.” 

 

They both turn to see a McLaren employee walking into the room. They both greet him lamely. 

 

“Lando’s car is here,” he says. “If you’re good to go?” 

 

Lando’s not. He’s still half-dressed, and he doesn’t even know where his backpack is. But what’s there really to say? Now that they’re not alone. 

 

Oscar’s looking at him like he has so much he wants to say. And Lando’s desperate to know what he was going to ask. Wants to steal away another five minutes alone for them. But, he can’t. 

 

Has a stupid schedule and a life and a race weekend to get ready for. That Oscar needs to get ready for. 

 

“I should let you go,” he says, like it’s the last thing he wants to do. “I’ll see you at the track on Thursday, Lando.” 

 

For once, those words don’t feel like a dismissal. 

 

If Lando’s delusional enough, they might even sound like a promise. 

 

----

 

Silverstone is a lot. 

 

The hype of it had given Oscar all sorts of anxiety leading up to the weekend. A home race for the team and for Lando is bad enough. But for Lando to have his own grandstand? 

 

The pressure of it feels like a lot to Oscar. He can’t even imagine what it feels like to be Lando. 

 

Though he greets Oscar with an easy, relaxed smile when Oscar shows up early on Thursday to see Lando’s setup. 

 

“Osc!” he shouts with a bright smile, that cute little gap on full display. “Come look at my Landostand!”

 

It’s odd. It feels like something has switched with them. 

 

Not feels like. It has. It’s not like Lando confessed his love or anything, but there was clearly something between them. Something they’d both hinted at. It makes Oscar feel a little lighter. Less like a fool. 

 

More like an endless pool of curiosity has settled inside him. He wishes they hadn’t been interrupted. That he’d been able to ask Lando what it was that Oscar was making him want. 

 

Was it the same thing Oscar wanted? 

 

It felt like it was. But Oscar also wondered if that was just hope clouding his mind. 

 

Hope or reality, Oscar decides maybe distance wasn’t the answer. He still wants to help Lando win. Still wants to do his job. Still has some unrestrained desire to just be close to him. 

 

So he shows up to Lando’s grandstand. It’s warm, even for England, but Lando’s in thick black jeans paired with a shockingly neon jersey and hat. Oscar thinks he might be able to see him all the way back in London. 

 

Oscar smiles at Lando as he approaches. There’s a man next to Lando who seems to assess him as he gets closer. 

 

“Ooo, casual,” Lando remarks as Oscar comes to stand before them. Oscar looks down at his plain white shorts and maroon T-shirt. Nothing too exciting, but the cleanest clothes at the top of his suitcase. His team kit is sitting in his backpack in hospitality. 

 

“I’m changing later,” Oscar explains. “I wanted to pop by before I meet with Will.” 

 

“Nah,” Lando says. Grinning. He looks at Oscar, a long once over. “I like it. Color looks good on you.” 

 

Surely, a matching flush is spreading on his cheeks. “Well, I can’t pull off neon as well as you.” 

 

“Oh, Oscar,” Lando says, looking up at him through those dark, gorgeous lashes. “You flatter me. Though I think you could pull it off. Not sure there’s a lot you can’t.” 

 

Oscar’s not really even sure what he can say to that. 

 

Luckily, he’s intercepted by Lando’s companion. “Jesus Christ, that’s enough, Bob. I’m about to be sick.” 

 

The man shakes his head like he’s seen enough. 

 

“Fuck you,” Lando hisses under his breath at the man, before fixing his face back into a smile at Oscar. “Oscar, this is Max. My best mate. I guess.” 

 

“He guesses,” Max says, rolling his eyes. He extends his hand to Oscar, who takes it firmly. “It’s nice to finally meet you, mate.” 

 

“You too,” Oscar says. He’s not sure Max means it. He can guess he doesn’t care much for listening to Oscar berate his best friend over the radio. 

 

“Ok, yes, it’s all very nice to get acquainted,” Lando says, clapping his hands. “Now go, Max. I need to show Oscar my grandstand.” 

 

“Sure, sure,” Max scoffs. He gives Oscar an appraising look, setting his lips into a firm line. “I’ll catch you later, Bob.” 

 

They both watch as Max retreats, heading towards a group that Oscare recognizes as Lando’s manager and some of his family. Once he is a fair distance away, Lando turns back to Oscar. A sort of frenzied, eager look takes over him, nearly bouncing on his feet. 

 

“Do you want to try my monster?” Lando asks, making Oscar choke on his own spit. 

 

“What?” he stutters out, wondering if he heard him right. Lando has the audacity to blush, seemingly taking in his own words. 

 

“I made a drink,” Lando says. “With Monster. A Monster Energy drink.” 

 

Oh. Oscar laughs a bit awkwardly. “I’m ok, but thank you.” 

 

“Alright,” Lando says. “You want a shirt or anything? We got plenty. Oh! Or a hat?” 

 

Lando doesn’t even wait for his answer, practically running off in the direction of the little merch truck they’ve set up. Oscar follows without a second thought. Lando slips in, dipping behind the counter and scouring through some boxes until he produces a bright, neon cap. A bold, black LN1 on the front. He looks at it for a moment and back at Oscar, before he digs into his pockets for a pen. 

 

Oscar watches as he uncaps it and signs the hat. A perfect swoop of his signature. A little 1 to cap it off at the bottom. 

 

“Perfect,” he says, as he leans across the counter and plops it right on Oscar’s head. 

 

“Oh,” Oscar says. “You don’t have to waste one of your hats on me.” 

 

“It’s not a waste,” Lando says earnestly. “You’re part of the LN team, Oscar.” 

 

“Actually, I’m part of McLaren,” Oscar says flatly. “I just work with whoever’s in your seat.” 

 

Lando clicks his tongue. “Your lack of fun and freedom never fails to surprise me.” 

 

Oscar reaches up and adjusts the hat, so it sits right on his head. “I’m joking. Of course, I’m on your team, Lando.” 

 

That lights Lando’s face right up. The sight of it so endearing, Oscar has to take a few calming breaths. 

 

Lando leans across the counter. “So, you’ll keep the hat?” 

 

“Sure,” Oscar says, aiming for light. “Might make me a fortune some day. Champion signed and everything.”  

 

“You better frame that fucking hat,” Lando says, pointing his pen at Oscar. He makes his way around the counter to come and stand back in front of Oscar. “It’s gonna be worth even more when I win on Sunday.”

 

“If you win on Sunday, I want something more than just a hat,” Oscar says. 

 

That gets Lando’s attention. “Oh? And what does my engineer want?” 

 

What does Oscar want? 

 

Fucking everything. 

 

Everything Lando will give him. 

 

His eyes drift down to Lando’s lip. Something he finds himself doing a lot lately. Like, since he’s had a taste, he can’t get it out of his mind. 

 

It’s not exactly appropriate to kiss Lando right here. In front of all of his friends and family and team. 

 

Isn’t really appropriate to kiss Lando at all. 

 

Obviously, that didn’t stop him the first time. 

 

Does give him a bit of pause as he’s still unsure where they align. 

 

He grits his teeth and looks away from Lando’s mouth. Looks at those, searching green eyes. 

 

“Why don’t you surprise me?” Oscar suggests. 

 

Lando grins, loving the challenge. “Ok. I’ll make it something good.” 

 

“Great,” Oscar says. He looks around, a sea of neon. “Give me your tour then? Some of us actually have to work today.” 

 

Lando gives him a playful scoff before his mouth opens in an endless monologue, pointing out every detail and person of interest. And Oscar hangs onto every word. 

 

----

 

Lando’s win at Silverstone is nothing short of amazing. 

 

After several safety cars, Oscar coaches him through the pit stops and the rain. Lando drives a clean race, keeping the car on track and not letting either Max or Daniel scare him off.

 

He’s not even smug when Oscar informs him of Daniel’s penalty. 

 

Watching Lando achieve another dream of his. It’s gorgeous. 

 

Daniel is fuming at his side from parc ferme to the podium, but Oscar can’t even care when Lando smiles up into the sun and looks younger and freer than ever. It’s like the clouds that haunted them all day, part just for this moment. For Lando to take the top step and be the homegrown hero. 

 

Though Oscar has little time to bask in it with him, as Lando’s quickly whisked away after debrief, stolen away by his friends and family. And Zak fucking Brown, taking Lando hostage for another fanstage. Oscar watches clips of it later on Instagram. He and Daniel are becoming fucking awkward, even with all their PR training. 

 

At least, Oscar can admire the line of Lando’s throat as he does his shoey in stride in perfect 4K. 

 

Luckily, the good energy carries into the last two races. Their understanding and teamwork are off the charts. He feels like they’re more in sync than ever. 

 

Lando is clearly disappointed to lose out on the win in Spa. Something Oscar’s a bit miffed about as well, confessing to Lando it’s his favorite track in a spare moment after their debrief. 

 

“Why didn’t you say earlier?” Lando asks. “I could have taken you on a hot lap.” 

 

“You’re a good F1 driver,” Oscar says, watching a spark of pride light up Lando’s face. “But I’ve seen videos of you on the road. I don’t trust you at all in an actual car.” 

 

Lando whines about it adorably until Jon comes and collects him for an ice bath.

 

In Hungary, Lando has a terrible start. Dropping places like it’s nothing as Daniel fights for a win. 

 

There’s not much he can do at the beginning, struggling to manage his tyres and get past Russell. But Oscar has a bit of a breakthrough once he passes Fernando.

 

He watches Lando pace in the first stint. It’s good. Better than good, even as everyone else’s tyres change. Even as Tom and the rest of the pit wall discuss the best way to undercut Leclerc. 

 

They lock Daniel onto a two-stop strategy, pitting early. His ability to manage the tyres far worse than Lando's. 

 

“Thoughts on doing a one stop?” Oscar asks after a while, not even bothering to code his message. Their main competitors will have already chosen their strategy. But Lando’s drifting in P4. And Oscar’s not chained to the same strategy as Tom. Freedom in being the prey vs the predator. 

 

“Yeah, why not?” Lando responds. Far more calm and collected than expected. Unless you’re Oscar. Oscar whose seen the change. Who knows, they can make this work. 

 

And they do. 

 

When Lando crosses the line in P1, he can’t stop himself from pounding his fist on the desk in excitement, rattling even Andrea. Shouting into his headset to Lando’s delight and giving even Zak a run for his money. 

 

He catches Lando after the podium, still drenched in champagne. 

 

“You were fucking perfect for me today,” Oscar says, voice low and heated. Lando’s eyes widen, conscious of the endless sea of people milling about them. 

 

“Oscar,” Lando whines, high and desperate. A bit panicked, like he can’t believe Oscar would do this to him in front of other people. Like he hasn’t begged for it on the radio.“I have to go to the press conference.” 

 

“Then go,” Oscar says, simply. “Go be good and answer your questions. Winner.” 

 

He leaves Lando practically panting against the wall. 

 

He’d feel more satisfied about it if it wasn’t the last time he got a moment of Lando’s time before the summer break. 

 

Nine points between him and Daniel. 

 

It feels good. Oscar feels like they’re on the verge of taking the lead back. Like Lando only needs one more win to seal this momentum. 

 

For once in his life, Oscar hates his time off. He’s hungry. He wants to keep going. Wants to keep guiding Lando to more and more victories. Keep watching Lando drive like a champion. 

 

And of course, he kinda misses Lando. 

 

His flight back to Australia is long, and embarrassingly, he spends most of it watching Lando’s race highlights. 

 

His mum and sisters embrace him warmly at the airport, thrumming with excitement about all the things they’re gonna do. 

 

And Oscar loves them. But he aches to talk to Lando. To not have their last moment for over a month be some stolen moment in a paddock hallway. 

 

Like he can hear his thoughts, it seems like Lando feels the same. 

 

He starts texting Oscar a week into break. 

 

A selfie of him and Max on a golf course, both squinting into the sun.

Oscar finds himself laughing, making his sister look at him oddly over her book while they’re lounging around on a lazy afternoon. 

 

“Oh, and who are you texting?” she asks, peering at him over the cover, more than interested. 

 

“Ah, it’s just Lando,” Oscar says. 

 

“Norris?” she asks. “That’s kinda weird. Isn’t he like your boss?” 

 

“What?” he says, fighting the redness spreading to his cheeks. “He’s not my boss. If anything, I’m his boss.” 

 

She laughs. “I don’t think you’ve ever been the boss of anyone.” 

 

“Um, I’m the eldest brother,” Oscar argues. “I’m the boss of all of you. Well, except mum and dad.” 

 

She rolls her eyes. “Oscar, you were hardly here enough to be the boss of us.” 

 

He knows she doesn’t mean it as any sort of insult. It’s just a simple fact, isn’t it? That Oscar left and didn’t come back. He tries to ignore the twinge of guilt that twists up in his chest. 

 

It’s not something he hasn’t tried to say sorry for. Hasn’t tried to fix. 

 

It’s just that no amount of designer bags or paddock passes can make up for time. 

 

The guilt must be written all over his face. 

 

“I didn’t mean it like that,” his sister says after a moment, opening her book back up. “Just curious why you’re sexting your boss.” 

 

“Are you high?” Oscar asks, making her laugh. “I’m not sexting my fucking boss. He’s not my boss.” 

 

“Then why are you blushing so much just at the mention of him?” she says, a sly smile playing on her lips. 

 

Oscar huffs, slinking into his seat and holding his phone in front of his face so he doesn’t have to look at her anymore. 

 

He’s got another text from Lando. Speak of the devil.

Better than being in the fucking snow. 

Who wants to celebrate in the cold like that?

And just like that, they keep texting all through the break. Oscar doesn’t even notice it’s been a few weeks of constant updates from Lando, until he’s due to fly back to London in a few days. 

 

Lando, who’s been galavanting all around Europe, it seems. Golfing with his mates, hanging out on yachts, spending time with his family, and playing padel with a rotation of drivers on the grid.

 

Oscar, in turn, has kept Lando updated. He’s sent Lando pictures of the spa he took his mum and sisters to. The pub he went to with some of his mates. The view of his collection of old RC cars from his childhood bed. 

 

That had been a request from Lando after Oscar had woken up to a collection of Lando’s texts from the day and confessed he was still lying in bed.

Probably pretty nice if their dinner in Miami was anything to go by. Even if the idea of Lando whipping out his black card and treating him to another expensive, dimly lit meal makes his body run a little hot.

Oscar expects more updates on what he did with his friends or some sponsorship work he gets wrapped up in. 

 

Nothing outside of what he’s been used to getting for the last few weeks.

[image]

 

The picture is. 

 

Well, it sets Oscar’s blood on fire. 

 

He swallows thickly, taking in the image that Lando’s just sent him. He’s standing in the mirror of some home gym. He’s smiling at the camera, a goofy kind of endearing grin that Oscar might just find sweet if it wasn’t for what was going on below the neck. 

 

Lando seemed to forgo a shirt while he worked out. And it was just… miles of sweaty, tan skin. Oscar took in the strong shoulders that flared down into nice, defined pecs. A small patch of hair between them that Oscar had the strongest urge to trace with his hands. Below that, his body tapered into that tiny, grippable waist and sharp V lines. Swathed in stiff hotel sheets and his softest sweats. He rubs his socked feet together. Some Dutch dating show that Lando doesn’t understand playing on the TV. Mindless noise. Anything to drown out the noise buzzing around his own brain. 

 

He’d avoided everyone. Done his media and sulked through a debrief before slinking away, back to his hotel. 

 

There had been apologies from everyone. Andrea. Zak. His mechanics. Even another one from Oscar. 

 

But it didn’t matter. Apologies, didn’t fix a mechanical DNF. 

 

Didn’t bring back 34 points. 

 

He sighs and shifts the comforter over his head, blocking out the TV and the constantly lit-up screen of his phone. Max had texted. His parents had called. 

 

He can’t look at his phone anymore. He’d already tortured himself watching the highlights. Seeing how close he was to Daniel. Watching as he pulled over and saw his name tumbling down the timetables. Heard how devastated both he and Oscar sounded over the radio. 

 

How poorly Daniel hid his satisfaction in the press conference and interviews. Clearly pleased that Lando was so far back. Looking like he’d just secured the title. Even Max seemed more sympathetic about Lando’s DNF, and he strived for nothing but wins, friendship or not. 

 

And maybe this was the win. Maybe this is what was gonna give Daniel the title. An oil leak that handed Daniel the championship on a silver platter 

 

Lando sighs. His throat feels tight. His eyes water again. 

 

He feels weak and stupid. It’s not like it’s even his fault. And maybe that’s what’s worse. That it’s so far out of his control. 

 

He shuts his eyes tight. 

 

There’s a trio of knocks on his door. 

 

He sighs and decides to ignore it. It’s probably Jon or Mark checking to make sure Lando hasn’t flung himself off the balcony. 

 

More knocking. 

 

“Lando?” 

 

It’s Oscar. 

 

Lando sighs. He’s not sure he wants to see him right now. It’s not like it’s Oscar’s fault. Not completely. Though he is Lando’s race engineer. Part of him thinks Oscar should have seen this. Should have known that there was something wrong with the car. That this could happen. 

 

The rational part of Lando knows that it doesn’t work that way. That the car was checked again and again. That Oscar would never let him go out if he didn’t think the car was alright. 

 

Not to mention they haven’t talked. Not like Oscar promised. Most of it due to the busy nature of it being the first race weekend since the break. Some of it due to Lando being a bit scared. 

 

Their phone call had left Lando euphoric. Left him feeling wanted and desired, and so in tune with Oscar, it was insane. Made him want Oscar even more than he thought was possible. Turned his little crush into something else. Something, he wasn’t sure he wanted to name. 

 

They’d exchanged a few more texts since that night. Tamer things. Updates about their weekends or days. Nothing like Lando wants or craves. 

 

Like he needs from Oscar. To know where they stand. To feel like this wasn't a one-time ordeal. 

 

That Oscar's feelings wouldn't fade away if they actually addressed them.

 

Or that Lando's own feelings wouldn't burn brighter afterward, into something like wildfire. Something that might scare Oscar off. 

 

Oscar knocks again. “I know you’re in there.” 

 

Lando shoves the comforter down and sits up. The last thing he needs is for Oscar to see him mope around. But he knows Oscar’s as stubborn as he is and won’t give up easily. Might as well beat him at his own game.

 

He shuffles over to the door, where Oscar is still annoyingly knocking. He rips it open. Oscar’s fist hangs in the air. 

 

“What do you want, Oscar?” Lando demands. 

 

Oscar’s face is lit with concern, eyes wild and searching Lando’s for any distress. 

 

“I just wanted to see if you were ok,” he says. Lando shrugs, looking to the side. “Can I come in?” 

 

Lando sighs. He doesn’t really want company. He also doesn’t want Oscar to leave. 

 

He steps aside and lets Oscar in. 

 

He shuffles back to his bed, sliding under the sheets and looking at Oscar. He pats the space next to him, and Oscar sits gingerly on the edge of the bed. 

 

“I’m fine,” Lando says. “I’m just upset.” 

 

“I think that’s to be expected,” Oscar says. He sighs. “I’m sorry too. I know I’ve said it a million times, but I mean it.” 

 

“Not really your fault, is it?” Lando says. 

 

“It’s not not my fault,” Oscar says. “I’m the one who leads the engineers and the mechanics on our side. I should have seen this coming. Or I should have had the mechanics do another check this morning.” 

 

“No, Oscar,” Lando says, shaking his head. He reaches out and touches Oscar’s arm, pulling him in closer by the sleeve of his sweatshirt. “Stop.” 

 

Oscar sighs and shuffles a little closer, letting his body sit fully on the bed. “It’s not your fault either.” 

 

“I know,” Lando says. “Just unlucky. That’s life.”

 

“Right,” Oscar says. He bites his lip for a moment. “It’s a setback. But we still have a lot of races left. We can come back. You’ve been behind before.” 

 

“Yeah, I guess,” Lando says, his hands picking at a loose thread on his own sweatshirt. “I don’t know. Hard not to feel like it’s over already.” 

 

“No, Lando,” Oscar says. “You can’t think that way. I know this sucks. I mean, I’m pretty fucking upset myself. But we’ve come a long way. We can do this.” 

 

Lando looks up at him. Oscar’s offering him a small, soft smile. Something meant to be reassuring. It makes Lando feel undeserving. Of Oscar’s time and attention. 

 

Of this car and this team. 

 

“I don’t know if I can,” he says. “I don’t know if I’m good enough.” 

 

Oscar’s smile falters and turns into a full frown. “What makes you say that?” 

 

Lando sighs.

 

“I’m scared,” he confesses, feeling like the truth and weight of emotions is clawing at his chest. Begging to come out.

 

“Of what, darling?” Oscar asks. 

 

“Last year was different,” Lando starts. “There were things in my favor. Max made a lot of mistakes. Their car too. It couldn’t keep up with us. And the DNFs helped too.

 

Oscar stays quiet as he listens. 

 

Sometimes,” Lando says. “I feel like I won, not because of me, but because of circumstance. Like things just worked in my favor.”

 

“So?” Oscar asks. “Championships have been won on less. It’s about who’s got the most points at the end. It’s not like you didn’t have DNFs or penalties last year, either. Was Miami not a real win because it wouldn’t have happened without the safety car?” 

 

“Of course it was,” Lando says. “But one race is not a championship.” 

 

“It’s not,” Oscar agrees. “But you did enough to win, Lando. Yes, things out of your control put Max behind, but you still won. You still drove great races and fought till the end. You still have to put yourself in the position to be a champion in order to win it.”  

 

“Yeah, but can I do it again?” Lando asks, feeling tired and unsure. “Can I do it the way I want to?” 

 

“And how do you want to?” Oscar says. 

 

“I want to do it without mind games or orders or everyone saying I was lucky,” Lando says. “I want to do it honestly. I don’t want to fight with Daniel off track. Or have to rise to Max’s bait. I just want to get on track and drive. And I know that sometimes that will mean I won’t have perfect weekends, and I moan about it to the media, and they'll make a big story about it. That Daniel or Max can use it against me. Can try to get into my head. The media, too. But at least I can be honest. I can be me.” 

 

“You can do that, Lando,” Oscar says. 

 

“It feels like I can’t,” Lando says, slumping further down the bed. “Like you were right. When we first started working together. That I’m too emotional. I let the comfort with the car get to me, and it put me behind. I let all that shit I felt at the beginning of the season get to me, and I drove worse for it.” 

 

Oscar takes a deep breath. He shifts just a bit closer to Lando. His hand hesitates for a moment before he reaches forward and grasps Lando’s in his. He intertwines their fingers. 

 

The touch is so warm and comforting, it shocks Lando for a moment. He looks down at their hands, his big enough to dwarf Oscar’s smaller ones. Lando’s summer tan contrasting perfectly with Oscar’s paler complexion.

 

“You’re not that person anymore,” Oscar says. “It’s ok to be upset right now. It’s ok to be scared. But Monaco. Austria. Those were the real you, Lando. The driver I know you can be. Racing is just a sport built on circumstances. It’s talent as much as luck. I can’t promise we’re gonna have an easy ride to the title, but I know we at least have the talent. You have the talent. You’re going to win this.” 

 

“How can you be so sure?” Lando asks, voice small and hushed. 

 

“Because I’m sure of you,” Oscar says, clear and unyielding. “I know you have what it takes to do this. And I know I’m gonna try my best to help you.”

 

Lando looks up at him. Those deep brown eyes are looking at him like he’s a prize. Like something to be cherished. It makes Lando feel strange. Not necessarily in a bad way. 

 

Just like something he’s never felt before. Not really. Not fully. 

 

“Ok,” Lando agrees. “We keep trying.” 

 

Oscar nods. “Good.” 

 

Lando regards him for a moment, taking in the soft flush on his cheeks. The pleased look in his eyes. The soft smile he gives Lando. 

 

He leans up and presses his lips to Oscar’s cheek. Quick and fleeting, just long enough to feel the warmth under his mouth. 

 

Oscar gasps as his other hand comes to touch the place where Lando’s lips were. If possible, he flushes even further. 

 

Silly, Lando thinks. How impossibly red Oscar is when they’ve done a lot worse than a little peck on the cheek. Not like he could see Oscar, though.

 

The image of Oscar being bright red and flushed as he talked Lando to orgasm is more than a little arousing. 

 

He tapers the feeling down for the moment.

 

“You’re not a robot, you know,” Lando says. “I was wrong in Bahrain. You’re not bad at this emotion thing.” 

 

Oscar laughs and shrugs. “I know. It’s just how I talk.” 

 

“I mean your voice, too,” Lando says. “Everyone around us is just too busy to actually listen. But when I take the time and just listen to you, then I can hear it. Like, your voice goes drier when you’re proud of me. Or it gets low when you’re angry. And your accent gets thicker when you’re excited. Or um.”

 

“What?”

 

“Like, it goes really thick when you’re turned on,” Lando says, flushing on his own now.  

 

“Oh Jesus,” Oscar says, pinching his nose. “That’s embarrassing.”

 

“No, Oscar, it’s not,” Lando says, laughing so high it’s almost a giggle. “It’s hot, baby.”

 

“Yeah?’ Oscar asks, slumping down further into bed, getting physically closer. His hand tightens around Lando’s. His voice a bit deeper. Accent thick. 

 

“Yeah,” Lando says and curls his hand further into Oscar’s. 

 

He likes it a lot, actually. Oscar’s voice. 

 

Likes Oscar a lot. 

 

Maybe more than like. He sighs and buries his face into the pillow. 

 

“You ok?” Oscar asks. “I thought we were on the ups.”

 

Yeah, I’m ok,” Lando says. “But, would you…stay? With me. Just for tonight.” 

 

“Yeah, I will, Lan,” Oscar says. “Anything you need.” 

 

Lando needs a lot. Needs as much of Oscar as he can get. 

 

 He sits up, untangling their hands and reaching over to turn off the bedside lamp. Oscar shifts and follows suit. He plops his phone on the nightstand and settles back into the bed. He crawls under the sheets. 

 

Lando watches as he settles, eyes adjusting a bit in the dark before he settles on his own side.  

 

“Thank you for staying,” Lando whispers into the dark.

 

“Of course,” Oscar says. He takes a deep breath. “You know it’s not over until it’s over? This isn’t the end.”

 

Lando sighs. Breathes in the scent of Oscar’s soft detergent and minty body wash and chocolate deodorant. He smells like a mint TimTam. Lando wants the scent of him embedded in all of his sheets and clothes from now on. 

 

“I know,” Lando says. “I still believe in myself. In us.”

 

“Good,” Oscar says. He looks at Lando with a soft smile. He reaches over to caress Lando’s cheek. A short, fleeting thing that Lando craves more of instantly before Oscar settles more comfortably in the bed. “Let’s sleep then. You need it. Deserve a good night of rest.” 

 

Landos nods and shifts around until he’s comfortable. Cheek pressed into the pillow, facing Oscar. He’s silent as he watches Oscar fall asleep. Oscar must be just as tired. His breathing evens out quickly, and his body goes lax with the sleep in a few moments. 

 

The urge to cuddle in closer overwhelms Lando. He wants to get close, press their bodies together. Feel Oscar’s arm, heavy and secure, around him. But there’s a touch of nerves. For all the flirting and the dirty talk. They’ve hardly touched except for one kiss and a few fleeting caresses. 

 

Instead, he settles for reaching his hand out and pinching the loose fabric of Oscar’s sweatshirt sleeve between his fingers. The fabric is soft and worn, Lando rubs it between his fingers. Touching, not touching. Like a tether to this man. 

 

“Shit,” Lando says, hushed into the darkness. “I really like you, Oscar.”

 

If Oscar’s awake or hears, he doesn’t make a move. Remains calm and asleep next to Lando, unaware of the effect he has on him. 

 

Lando breathes out slowly. Like doesn’t even seem like an accurate word. This feels like something more. Something all-encompassing. Something that makes Lando a bit afraid. 

 

He’s not sure he can voice it right now. Not even to himself. 

 

Instead, he shuffles closer gently and closes his eyes. He falls asleep with the scent of Oscar’s detergent and the rise and fall of his breathing lulling him to sleep. 

 

----

 

Singapore comes up on them quickly. 

 

And time to talk about them seems to escape them again. No matter how much Oscar craves to speak about it. Not just their phone call or the night he spent in Lando’s bed. But just them and whatever great shift is coming their way. 

 

With the point setback, the both of them are busier than ever. Lando spends days before the race, working more with Jon, spending more time in the sim or taking moments alone to collect his thoughts and keep a calm, relaxed mindset. This often meant a long nap before sessions, which Oscar didn’t dare to interrupt. 

 

And Oscar is finding himself in more meetings than ever. Working with the mechanics and the engineers to make sure another Zandvoort didn’t happen. And he’s dedicated himself to studying Daniel’s data. To making sure that Lando had the best chance of fighting, even if Lando himself was trying not to give into the media’s hunger for an interteam rivalry.  

 

So the chances to talk are far and few between. Especially with the run of races that follow the break. 

 

Monza is a good race. For Lando, at least, a good second place. Though it’s overshadowed by the swap. 

 

It’s a poor strategy on their part. Oscar wants to pit first. Insists on it. But Andrea and Tom insist that Daniel needs to pit first and avoid an undercut. Oscar doesn’t care much about Charles Leclerc, but he always seems to be at the scene of the crime when it comes to Daniel’s pit stops. 

 

But a slow pit stop for Lando means Daniel comes out ahead. 

 

Lando stays silent on the radio, but Oscar can practically feel the rage from his onboard. Letting go of his pitstop priority and then having to fight for second again. It’s bullshit. Oscar debates back and forth on calling it out. He knows Lando deserves the place. He knows the bullshit that will follow if they swap. 

 

Andrea makes the call for him. Demanding they swap. 

 

Oscar watches with object horror as Daniel gives back the position. As boos follow Lando onto the podium. 

 

Lando gets second place, but Daniel wins the crowd that day. 

 

In Baku, Oscar sees an opportunity. 

 

Daniel crashes in Q2 and starts P14. Lando can’t do much better than P7 due to another crash and a yellow flag, but it's something. Better when Daniel’s unable to make up more than a place or two on the start. Lando doesn’t move much either, but it’s still 6 points to Daniel’s nothing. 

 

Another chance to chip away at the lead. 

 

By Singapore, Lando seems to have gotten some of his fire back. Some of that underlying confidence that makes him a champion. Let’s him believe. 

 

He’s also sick as a dog. Coughing and sneezing in meetings and briefs like no other. Taking as much cold medicine as Jon and the FIA will allow to get himself into the car. 

 

It’s hot. Even when the sun goes down, the humidity is endless. Even Oscar, who is used to hot, humid Australian days, is sweating through his shirt before the race even starts. 

 

“If it’s too much, tell me,” Oscar says. He’s standing with Lando on the grid before he gets in, twirling the grid tape around in his hands. “I’d rather you quit than pass out behind the wheel.” 

 

“I’ll be fine, Oscar,” Lando says, voice low and scratchy. “I have to do this.” 

 

“Your health is more important,” Oscar says. Can’t even really believe he is saying this. Last year, Daniel was like a machine to him. His health was his own matter. Oscar only cared if he got in the car and how well he did. But Lando. 

 

He wants Lando to take care of himself. Wants to take care of him. 

 

“I’ll be ok,” Lando insists. He laughs, voice gone rough and scratchy from the cold. “I’m not like when Mark Webber was throwing up in his helmet.” 

 

“Alright,” Oscar says. His hands itch to press against Lando’s forehead or flushed cheeks, to feel how warm and sick he might be. “I’m in your ear. Let me know if it changes.” 

 

“I know,” Lando says. He offers Oscar a weak smile. “Thank you for caring.” 

 

Oscar hums. “Course. Be good for me and overtake Daniel.” 

 

“Always,” Lando says, looking a bit more energized. 

 

And he is good. Going for the gap Daniel leaves and pushing his way up to third. It’s more aggressive than Oscar’s used to. At least, from Lando. 

 

It makes his mouth water. Makes him feel a bit hotter than just the Singapore night. 

 

He’s about to turn on the radio. Let Lando know exactly how good the move is when he hears Daniel speak. 

 

“Mate, what the fuck?” Daniel says. “He can’t just crash into me like that. Can’t do whatever he wants cause he’s the fucking champion.” 

 

Tom sighs next to Oscar. “Ok, Daniel. We’ll check.” 

 

He turns in his seat and looks down the pit wall. Oscar looks back. He raises his eyebrow. 

 

“We should switch,” Tom says. “There was contact.” 

 

“What?” Oscar says. He turns in his seat and runs the onboard back. Watches the broadcast replay. “They barely bumped wheels. I’m not giving the place back.”

 

“Daniel gave the place back in Monza,” Tom argues. “How is this different?” 

 

“That was the team’s fault,” Oscar says. “And we didn’t ask for it. This is just racing. He hit Max, too. Should we just let him win?” 

 

“Oscar, c’mon,” Tom says. “You know Lando’s at fault.” 

 

“He’s racing,” Oscar reiterates. “I’m not giving the place back. If Daniel wants it, then he can fight for it.” 

 

“Mate, this is Singapore,” Tom says. “There is no fighting here.” 

 

“Then he shouldn’t have left a gap,” Oscar says. He watches as Lando continues on, working towards getting close to Max. Tom is right. This isn’t the circuit for overtakes. Not impossible, but difficult. To give back the place would keep Lando off the podium. 

 

Tom looks past Andrea to where Will and Andrea are looking at them. Will sighs. 

 

“It’s under investigation,” he says. “Let’s leave it to the stewards.” 

 

“On lap one?” Tom asks. “They’ll never say it was anything but a racing incident. Andrea, please?” 

 

Andrea hums. He looks to Zak, who puts his hands up, deferring to their team principal. 

 

“We leave it to the stewards,” Andrea says after a moment. “I don’t want to risk switching with Antonelli so close to Daniel and Lando chasing after Verstappen. If there’s no damage to either car, then it’s a racing incident to me.” 

 

Tom looks at them, but nods in acceptance eventually. He relays the message to Daniel once they get the no further investigation from race control. 

 

“That’s not fucking fair,” Daniel spits. “This is bullshit. Fucking golden boy just gets to do as he pleases.” 

 

Tom looks embarrassed. “We’ll talk about it after. Just focus on your own race.” 

 

Oscar looks back at his screen. Watches Lando’s pace. He tries again and again to get past Max. 

 

“You ok?” Oscar asks after his pitstop. 

 

“Yeah, I’m ok,” Lando says, voice a bit more breathless than usual. “If I stay here, how are we on the Constructor’s?” 

 

Oscar’s not even thinking about that. It’s more than sealed with how Lando and Daniel are now, but really, all he cares about is making sure Lando makes it to the end. 

 

“Yeah, but just worry about finishing, alright?” Oscar says. “You’re doing a really good job.” 

 

“Oscar,” Lando scolds lightly. “I’m hot enough as it is.” 

 

“Shut up,” Oscar says, hoping that this doesn’t get broadcast. Will laughs next to him, and Oscar has the decency to be a bit mortified. 

 

When Lando crosses the line in P3, he feels less like celebrating for the team and more for Lando. 

 

The point gap closes again. Oscar feels the same hope that took him over before the summer break flowing through him. 

 

Lando stumbles into the garage after the podium and the media, wet with champagne and sweat, but glowing. So beautiful that Oscar has to fight the urge to cross the room and kiss him in full view of the whole team. 

 

Instead, he smiles at Lando. Meets his eyes from across the room and feels Lando’s joy and relief of closing the Constructors’ in his body. He lets their hands brush briefly when Lando comes to stand next to him. Takes in Lando’s soft, shy smile as something just for him. 

 

The moment is almost perfect, even as sedated as it is. 

 

Until Daniel comes in. He rushes into the garage, pushing past mechanics and engineers, making their plastic cups of champagne splash over. 

 

He rushes up to Lando and shoves him. Not hard, but enough to make Lando stumble back into Oscar. Make his wheezy breath catch. 

 

“What the fuck?” Lando asks. He rights himself, but grips tightly onto Oscar’s arm as he catches his breath. 

 

“You crashed into me, you little prick,” Daniel says. He’s seething clear as day. Something Oscar’s been on the other side of more than a few times. “You could have put me into the wall.” 

 

“It was a racing incident,” Oscar says. “You left a gap, and he went for it. There wasn’t even damage.” 

 

Daniel lets out a low, humorless laugh. “Not surprised you’re both working well together. Two incompetent children. You’re only doing well because Zak and Andrea are obsessed with you both for some reason.” 

 

“I’m not gonna apologize for doing what anyone would do,” Lando says, voice still sick, but firm. “That’s racing. Sometimes there’s a bit of contact on the first lap. It’s just how it is.” 

 

“Fuck you,” Daniel seethes. “You’re just like him deep down. Pulling dirty moves and thinking you can barge your way through cause you’re the fucking champion.” 

 

“Hey,” Will says, as he and Andrea make their way through the crowd watching. “Let’s not do this here.” 

 

Daniel ignores them. He glares down at Lando. “Except you could never be Max. You’re too soft. Too weak. You’ll break yourself before you could ever break me.” 

 

“Daniel,” Lando says, shaking his head. “Just go.” 

 

“No,” Daniel says. “Be a fucking man and face me.”

 

“Daniel, stop,” Andrea says. Daniel turns to look at him. 

 

“You said there would be repercussions after Canada if that happened again,” Daniel says. “So where are they? He crashed into me again.” 

 

“Wow, running to daddy,” Oscar says, dryly. “They barely touched.” 

 

Andrea’s face is set in a hard line. “We’ll discuss later. Not here in the garage.” 

 

“Oh, please,” Daniel says. “I know what the fuck that means. Jesus, this is insane. I should call up Christian and-.” 

 

“Daniel.” 

 

A loud, booming voice echoes in the garage. Oscar watches as Zak walks in. His face, normally full of American jovialness, is set in a hard, angry expression. Daniel deflates. 

 

He puts his hands up. “Fine. We can talk about it in private.” 

 

Lando swallows loudly next to Oscar. Oscar’s hand finds itself on Lando’s lower back, rubbing a smoothing circle through his fireproofs. They watch as Daniel stalks off, his trainer and manager following. The quiet of the team flows back into a cacophony of noise. Zak and Andrea head off to hospitality. 

 

“Are you alright?” Oscar asks, moving to face Lando. He looks a bit ill still. Sweat still coating his face and neck. He nods. 

 

“Yeah,” he says. “I’m just tired.” 

 

Oscar gets what he means. 

 

----

 

They still haven’t had a chance to talk by the time they get to Austin. 

 

Which is unfortunate as it’s hot and Oscar’s back hurts. 

 

It’s not often his back twinges so badly that he can’t get out of bed. But when it happens, it’s hard. He has a prescription for when the pain gets bad, but he tries to avoid taking it as much as possible. Most of the time, Oscar just feels a bit sore or achy after a long day. But the long haul of flights from Singapore to London to Austin is hard after an already long season of traveling. 

 

He’d been more than disappointed in his body when he’d had to reject Lando’s offer to go golfing on Wednesday. 

 

Can I get you anything?” Lando had asked, standing in the doorway of Oscar’s hotel room. “Or I could stay with you?” 

 

“No,” Oscar said, wincing. “I just need to rest. Don’t let me stop you from having fun.” 

 

“Yeah, but,” Lando said, shifting on his feet. “We haven’t talked.”

 

“I know,” Oscar said, regretfully. “Soon. I’ll be better by the race. Maybe we can meet after?” 

 

“I’m supposed to take a flight with Carlos to Tulum,” Lando said, looking annoyed. “We’re having a mini vacation before the race. He thinks I need to chill out.” 

 

“He’s right,” Oscar said. “We’ll talk in Mexico then?”

 

Lando huffed in annoyance. Not at Oscar, but the situation. “That’s so far away.” 

 

Oscar hummed. “Sometimes, I run before the sessions on Saturday. You could join me? I was gonna run the track.” 

 

“It’s always exercise with you,” Lando said, rolling his eyes. “Alright. As long as it won’t make your back any worse.” 

 

“I’ll rest it as much as I can today and tomorrow,” Oscar promised. “We can talk on the run.” 

 

“Alright,” Lando agreed, pouting a bit. 

 

“Don’t pout at me,” Oscar said. “Go golfing. I’ll see you at the track.” 

 

And Oscar had sent him off with the promise. 

 

Now, he’s stretching by the start finish line barriers, trying to ease the tension in his back. The rest had been good and needed. But he’s eager to move again. 

 

“Osc,” Lando greets him cheerfully. He’s smiling brightly, wearing a set of black workout clothes that accent his tan skin. 

 

“You need sunscreen,” Oscar says in lieu of greeting, making Lando roll his eyes. 

 

“Ok, mum,” Lando says. “I wear it. Sometimes.” 

 

“I wear it every day,” Oscar says.

 

“Yeah, I can tell,” Lando says, making Oscar tsk at him. 

 

“Skin cancer isn’t a joke, Lando,” Oscar says, directly.  

 

“Ok, thank you for the lesson, Mr. Piastri,” Lando says. “I’ll get some.” 

 

“Alright,” Oscar says, quirking his lips up into a smile. “Sorry, I like the tan.” 

 

“Yeah?” Lando asks, voice light and full of mischief. “Wanna see how far it goes?” 

 

“No,” Oscar lies. “Start running.” 

 

Lando laughs, but falls in line with Oscar. And away they go. 

 

It’s still pretty early, not yet as hot as it will get, but they start sweating immediately under the rising sun. Worse on the incline to turn one. 

 

They’re panting for breath by the time they head towards turn two. 

 

“So,” Lando pants. “Are we gonna talk about it?” 

 

“Yeah,” Oscar says. “Bout time.”
