“I think George means we’re happy to hear whatever is bothering you,” Alex supplies. “Even if it feels stupid to you.” 

 

George rolls his eyes, but nods. 

 

“It’s just,” Lando starts. “I’m not even sure Oscar really wants me. Or maybe he wants me now when we’re, like, in the midst of the team stuff and the season. But when we’re not at work, will he still want me?  Or like, what if I lose?”

 

Lando bites his lip for a moment. He ignored how Alex looks at him so gently, like he might break. How George’s face is passive, but his eyes are intense. 

 

“I think I really like him,” Lando says.  “But will he still want me if I’m not a winner? Or everything he thinks I am? I don’t want to get hurt. And I don’t want to disappoint him or make him think less of me. Especially, if we’re going to keep working together.”

 

Both men look to be in thought for a moment after Lando speaks. He sighs and sips at his coffee. Cold and creamy, with a hint of real vanilla. Oscar had slipped it into his room before Lando arrived.

 

“Well, I don’t think he’ll be disappointed in you,” Alex says. “But you should tell him anyway. You won’t know until you know, right? And if you feel strongly, then it’s worth trying.” 

 

“Even if I might get hurt?” Lando asks. He feels stupid. Like a teen girl. Probably like all the situationships he’s had in the past few years. He has half a mind to open his phone and DM them all with a strict apology if they felt even half of this. 

 

“Yeah,” Alex says. “Better to know than just sit in the dark, right?”

 

“Alex is right,” George says. He shrugs. “But I wouldn’t worry too much about it anyway.”

 

“Think that’s not possible,” Lando says. All the work he’s done for himself. For his career. For his mental health. And yet, the worrying still seems to remain present in his life. 

 

“But Oscar gets it, right?” George says. “He’s a part of this world. And this world is a part of you. Of all of us. He’s already seen you, Lando. I’m guessing he’s seen you at your lowest already. Seen you ‘lose’ so to speak. And he still wants you. So, I wouldn’t worry too much.” 

 

Alex hums in agreement. 

 

George isn’t wrong. Lando thinks of Zandvoort. Of even last weekend. When he’d seen Lando in sadness and anger. When Lando had directed those feelings towards him. And he’d still wanted him. Still wanted to take care of him. 

 

Lando thinks of the beginning of the season. When they’d both been blinded by their own pride and assumptions. How they’d fought and bickered. How they hurled insults like they were nothing. How Lando thought he knew Oscar when he’d barely scratched the surface.

 

Oscar still wants him, despite all that. 

 

He smiles to himself. 

 

“Alright,” Lando says. “I suppose you’re right.” 

 

“Suppose,” George scoffs. “I am right.”

 

“It’s pretty obvious that Oscar’s just as down bad as you are, Lando,” Alex says. He reaches over and pats Lando’s arm. He offers him a bright, blinding smile. “Like you’d have to be blind to not notice how he looks at you.” 

 

“How does he look at me?” Lando asks. 

 

“Like he’s got those cartoon fucking hearts in his eyes,” George says. “Fucking Sailor Moon or some shit.” 

 

Oh. Lando’s never really noticed. Well, not never. But hadn't been able to put words into the warm, fond way Oscar looked at him sometimes. Like Lando was his world. Or just plain his. 

 

“Kinda embarrassing, actually,” Alex says. “I don’t even look at my girlfriend that way. Makes me feel like I have to step up my game.” 

 

“Whatever,” Lando says, waving his hand. Secret delight sits warm and light in his chest. He sighs, biting back his own smile. 

 

“Yeah, it’s disgusting,” George says. He pauses for a moment, words clearly sitting on his tongue. He fixes Lando with a sincere expression. “Also, for what it’s worth, I think Oscar would be fucking brain dead to not want you back, Lando.”

 

“Oh?” Lando asks. 

 

“Yeah,” George says, trying to be nonchalant. “You’re a good person and very much deserving of someone who loves you fully.” 

 

Lando looks at George. A light flush on his cheeks. He looks embarrassed, to Lando’s delight. Alex coughs out a laugh, a wild, delirious grin on his face.

 

“Aw, George,” Alex says. “That’s so sweet. Wow, do you love us? Why don’t you say such nice things to me?” 

 

Both he and Lando laugh, making George scoff as he looks away and adjusts his watch. 

 

“You don’t deserve them,” George says. “Actually, I hope you both burn in hell.” 

 

“Carmen’s so lucky if that’s how you compliment someone,” Alex says. George makes to swat at him before a media rep comes to grab them. 

 

Lando feels loads lighter than he did this morning. 

 

----

 

Oscar feels a bit dizzy looking at his notes. He dims the screen of his computer before it makes him go cross-eyed. He and Will have spent far too long talking strategy. 

 

Stupid mandatory two-stop, he thinks. Lando has many talents, but his tyre management is unmatched. A natural advantage, only stopped by the team’s own strategy. And now taken away by the FIA when he could win it all. 

 

Not that Oscar’s completely optimistic. He knows Lando isn’t. Taking every race one by one. But Oscar’s job is the big picture. Thinking of not just the current race, but the ones ahead. Thinking about the setup and the different tracks. How to wield Lando’s skill to these. 

 

There’s a never say never quality about racing. It’s not over until it’s over. 

 

It’s not impossible for Lando to win. Not impossible for Lando to get hit or have another failure, despite his best efforts. 

 

Or for Max to slide back in. To claim that coveted 4th championship. 

 

Or for Daniel to work his way back. To see both his rivals taken out and claim the victory for himself. 

 

The possibilities are never-ending. 

 

He sighs loudly, drawing Will’s attention back from his own computer. 

 

“It’ll be alright, Oscar,” Will says, brightly. “Lando’s more than capable. He knows what he needs to do.” 

 

“Yeah,” Oscar says. “But do I know what I need to do?” 

 

Will hums.

 

“The problem with being an engineer, isn’t it?” Will says. “We plan and plan and think everything through. But at the end of the day, the race will happen as it happens.”

 

“It’s frustrating,” Oscar admits. 

 

“Sure,” Will agrees. “But that’s what works with Lando. He knows shit happens. But he’s in the car. He knows how it goes. And frankly, he’s gotten much better at just driving and not overthinking it all.”

 

“He has,” Oscar says. “I’m proud of him.”   

 

Will smiles at him warmly. “Me too. And I’m proud of you as well, Oscar.” 

 

“You are?” Oscar asks, taken aback. He doesn’t really think he’s done much to make anyone proud of him. 

 

“Yes,” Will says simply. “There was a lot of debate on who would take on Lando once I got promoted. Frankly, some of the higher-ups wanted to bring in someone from the outside. You know, there are always ears open for a new opportunity. Even more so when it’s the reigning world champ. But I wanted you to take it. I knew you’d do well here.” 

 

“But Lando and I fought for a good three months,” Oscar says. “I mean, we clashed on almost everything.” 

 

“Sure,” Will laughs. “But that was part of it. Lando and I were too comfortable. We’ve been together too long. It was a dream to win a championship with him. But he’s destined for more. And he needed someone to challenge him. To make him work harder. Better. I just don’t think I could do that anymore. Let alone push back to the team.” 

 

Oscar supposes that makes sense. He thinks about Lando with a title to his name and comfort settling deep in his bones. Would Lando still be here fighting for another title without the challenge? It was one thing to go against Max, but your teammate? Someone in an equal car. 

 

And a team like this? That worried so much about fairness that they usually ended up disappointing both sides equally. In another universe, was Daniel still leading the championship while Lando faded into the background?

 

Beyond that. 

 

Did Oscar challenge him? Change him? Make him a better driver? 

 

It’s a heavy thought. He hopes it’s true.  Wants it to be true with every fiber of his being. 

 

“I don’t know if I’ve done that much,” Oscar confesses. 

 

“You have,” Will says. “And the same goes for you. I know you have so much potential, Oscar. You’re going to be a race director one day. Team principal, too. But you needed a push. Working on the other side was pushing down your abilities. You’re smart, and you’re great at strategy, but being a second driver’s engineer doesn’t suit you. And Lando, for all his annoying qualities, pushed you to be more outspoken. To think in different ways.”

 

Well, that’s true. Oscar thinks of who he was at the start of the season. Nose stuck in spreadsheets and dismissing everything Lando had to say. It’s almost madness to think where they are now. Where Oscar is now. Pushing back against Andrea of all people. Fighting to get Lando on the front. Working to strategize to the best of Lando’s abilities, not just treating him like data on a screen. 

 

“I guess I didn’t see it that way,” Oscar says. He sighs and leans back in his seat. 

 

“You two make a good team,” Will says. A smirk dances across his lips. “Better than I could have imagined. Landoscar and all that.” 

 

Oscar blushes. “I-, we’re, uh, we’re friends.” 

 

“Friends,” Will says, laughing. “Sure. Sure. If that’s what we wanna call it.” 

 

“Well, we haven’t really called it anything,” Oscar says, not sure why he’s saying this. Telling his boss this. But Will is one of the few people who really knows Lando. That have been in his ear and know how he works.  

 

“No?” Will says. “I’m surprised. You two are quite obvious.”

 

“Oh god,” Oscar says. “Does everyone know about Lando and me?” 

 

Will laughs. “Not everyone. I’m sure Zak and Andrea are too busy to even notice. But I can tell. So can Jon. It’s easy to tell that Lando’s smitten like a schoolboy. But you’re harder to notice.” 

 

“Oh,” Oscar says. “Lando’s smitten?” 

 

“Obnoxiously so,” Will says, rolling his eyes. “Just in the way he speaks to you. Certainly, not how he talks to me or Jon or Mark.”

 

Oscar flushes further. He thinks of the way Lando can be so loud. So expressive and buoyant when they’re in front of cameras or crowds of people. But then he thinks of the way Lando is when they’re alone. How he looks at Oscar so warm and sure. How he mumbles pet names and sweet phrases to Oscar and Oscar alone. How he asks for reassurance and praise in such soft, dulcet tones. 

 

“I guess we haven’t been very good at hiding it,” Oscar says. 

 

“That’s alright,” Will says. “It’s not like you’re kissing on the podium. And Lando knows what to say when there’s more conservative eyes and ears around. Here, especially. But, my advice? Just talk to him. If you want more. I highly doubt he feels any different than you.” 

 

“We’ve never been that good at talking,” Oscar says. 

 

“You get used to it,” Will says, shrugging. “I think I fumbled through every conversation for the first year I dated my wife. But it’s worth it.” 

 

Yeah, Oscar thinks it might be. 

 

“Right,” Oscar says. “This was a strange conversation to have with my boss. But I appreciate it.” 

 

“Of course,” Will says. “I won’t hold you any longer. I know you’ve got a race to try and win. And I’ve got two garges to try and keep happy.” 

 

“Course, thanks,” Oscar says, gathering his things and leaving. His mind rushes. The sun is starting to set over the track, but Oscar feels more awake than ever. 

 

----

 

The sprint is forgettable. Lando qualifies and finishes in third. Every point counts, but these points don’t scare Oscar. 

 

Instead, he focuses on qualifying. Daniel has been marginally better this weekend. Not great, but better. Lando keeps up as best he can. Coming close to Daniel in the sprint. And Oscar has a newfound gratitude for George Russell coming in second and cutting Verstappen off from some points. 

 

But qualifying is where it really matters. 

 

Four poles in a row is a bit ambitious. 

 

But Oscar knows he needs a bit of ambition now. 

 

All Lando has to do is finish in front of both Daniel and Verstappen. 

 

Then that’s it. He’s champion. For the second time. 

 

It sounds so easy. And yet, Oscar’s stomach twists in nerves. He’s been unable to sleep all weekend. Tossing and turning and waking up when the sun starts to rise. He’s barred Lando from his room for both their sake. Knows that he needs the rest and not to be kept up all night from Oscar’s restlessness. 

 

Not even the pout he’d gotten in return had swayed him. 

 

He watches as Lando starts his first flying lap of Q3. 

 

Lando has been second in Q2. Good, but not as good as Daniel. 

 

He’s hoping that Last Lap Lando decides it’s a good time to make an appearance. 

 

Oscar holds his breath as he watches Lando. He watches his dot on the map. Lets his eyes roam over each section’s timing eagerly. 

 

He’s doing good. Better than good. Provisional pole good. 

 

“Really good job, Lando,” Oscar says, as Daniel is unable to beat the time on his first run. “Come back in, and then we can do a second lap.” 

 

“Yeah, felt good,” Lando says, preening a little, even over the radio. “Coming in.” 

 

Oscar watches as Lando trundles back into the pits. As his car is lifted back into the garage. Oscar looks over his shoulder. The screens block his view of Lando’s head, but the car is enough. Enough to reassure that Lando is here. He’s present. And he’s ready to fight for this win. 

 

When Lando goes back out for his second lap, Oscar watches the screens closely. Lando will set them up, but Oscar can do his job too. He watches as the other cars make their way around the track. He relays some data to him from the first run as Lando starts his warm-up. 

 

Truthfully, Oscar somewhat hates Lando’s preference to go out first. He knows he likes the safety of having his lap in. Knows he is confident in his ability to put in strong laps. Laps that can’t be matched. But having to watch Daniel get the better track evolution is painful. He sighs in his chair. 

 

Lando finishes his warm-up and starts his flyer. Oscar waits as Lando gears up, attacks the first corner well, and gets into the second. 

 

Oscar curses as he sees Lando go wide. 

 

“Shit,” Lando breathes out over the radio almost immediately. “I fucked up. I have to do another warm-up.” 

 

“Yeah,” Oscar says. “It’s ok. We know where to push on that corner. You have enough fuel to go again.” 

 

“Right,” Lando says, taking a deep breath. “Ok, I’ll get it.” 

 

Daniel starts his lap. It’s good, so far. Better than Lando’s previous one. Enough to put him on pole. 

 

But Oscar knows Lando can match him. Can pull out a better time. 

 

Daniel finishes his lap well and moves into p1. 

 

Oscar bites his lip, watching as Lando eases to the end of his warm-up. 

 

He sees Lando start to gear up. “Ok, good to go again. You’ll just make the flag, so no hesitating.” 

 

Oscar watches as the clock ticks down. It’ll be close. 

 

The timing of their laps puts them close on track. Daniel nearing the end of his cool down as Lando gets ready to push. Oscar bites his lip. A slipstream would be amazing right now. Even if Daniel doesn’t want to give it. 

 

“Daniel ahead,” Oscar says. “Let’s try and get a tow.” 

 

“Got it,” Lando says. 

 

He speeds up. 

 

There’s only seconds left, but Lando should make it. 

 

Has to make it. 

 

He watches as one McLaren approaches another. As Lando makes to get a bit of tow. 

 

As Daniel should move to the side, out of the way, as he does his cooldown lap. 

 

But, he doesn’t. 

 

He moves, slow, but sure. Right in Lando’s incoming direction. 

 

Lando swerves, moving around him smoothly, but forcing his own car to slow slightly. 

 

“Fucking hell,” Oscar curses to himself. He watches as Lando crosses the line. The clock long since ticked down to zero. Checkered flag waving rapidly in the wind. Daniel on pole.

 

“What the fuck?” Lando says over the radio. “He impeded me. I couldn’t get a lap in.” 

 

Oscar looks to his side. Will and Andrea look at the screens. They replay the moment. Tom sighs beside him. 

 

“I’m checking,” Oscar grits out, anger sparking bright in his body. He mutes the radio and looks at Tom. “What the hell, mate?” 

 

Tom looks as confused as Oscar. He looks back at his screen, watches the movement again. He rubs at his forehead. “It was a mistake.” 

 

He says it, but even he doesn’t sound like he believes it. 

 

“Your driver just cost mine pole,” Oscar says. 

 

“Lando could have gotten a lap in,” Tom argues. “Daniel was just confused. He didn’t mean to get in the way.” 

 

“Nah, mate,” Oscar says. He looks at their bosses. “That’s not right. That’s not fair.” 

 

“Oscar,” Andrea says. “What do you want us to do? We can’t give a grid position back.” 

 

“I’m not asking for that,” Oscar says. “But c’mon, we can all admit Daniel did that on purpose.” 

 

“Daniel wouldn’t impede his teammate on purpose,” Tom says. “Look, they all make mistakes and stupid decisions like that. It’s racing.”

 

“That’s bullshit,” Oscar says. “He’s trying to fuck Lando’s race.” 

 

Tom looks at Oscar for a long time. Looks at Oscar like he’s young. Like he’s hot-headed and doesn’t know better. Like, he almost pities him for caring so much. 

 

“Oscar,” he says. “How is this any different from Singapore? I’m sure Lando didn’t mean to hit Daniel.” 

 

Christ. If Oscar never thinks about Singapore or Monza or Silverstone or Austin, he’d be a free, happy man. He’s tired of the what-ifs. Both Lando and Daniel made mistakes. The team made mistakes. 

 

But this was different. This wasn’t racing incidents. Or taking advantage of penalties. Or bullshit team orders that made no one happy. 

 

This was on purpose. 

 

This wasn’t racing. This was playing dirty. 

 

Oscar seethes. Anger sinking it’s claws into his brain and body. Settling warm and hot in his bones. 

 

“These are not the mistakes they’ve both made,” Oscar says. “I’m not playing this back and forth. This was intentional.” 

 

“Oscar,” Andrea says behind him, strong and firm. “It’s impossible to know if Daniel moved on purpose. Lando wasn’t going to match his time. We will not argue about this. We have a front row lockout, and that’s what matters.” 

 

Oscar’s at a loss for words. 

 

Everything matters when the points are this close, and the races are so few. 

 

His anger simmers under the surface. Will sighs, but doesn’t argue with Andrea. Tom looks at them expectantly. Oscar pulls off his headset and tosses it onto the desk. 

 

“If we lose this championship,” Oscar says. “It’s because this need for fairness will let a selfish winner in.” 

 

He stalks off before he can face the disappointment they have in him. 

 

And before he can even fathom the same one he has in them. 

 

----

 

Mandatory two stops make him wanna die. 

 

Lando’s already sweating by the time they’re lined up on the grid, waiting for the rest of the cars to finish their formation lap. 

 

He could make the tyres last. He knows it. And yet, he’s locked into this bullshit. Has to overtake Daniel or else he’s trapped in the worst pitstop priority. 

 

He could be on pole. He could be in the best position to win today. To be earning his second title tonight. 

 

Instead, he’s sandwiched between Daniel and Max. What a terrible fucking place to be. 

 

He’s tried to focus on the race. Not the championship. It’s hard. He knows Oscar was upset about yesterday. He’d tried to remain positive, but there’d been an underlying current of frustration as they’d debriefed about the last lap. Oscar’s fist stayed curled tight, and his voice was stiff as he watched Daniel move in their replay.

 

Lando sighs. It’s bullshit. He knows it. But Daniel’s made it clear he’s done playing the team game. He’s in it to win a championship. Even if that means playing loose with the rules and the fragile respect the team wants them to have. 

 

In some ways, Lando can’t blame him. It’s hard not to want to do everything you have to when the title is so close. Sitting within arm’s reach. 

 

But Lando can’t think about Daniel. Not really. Maybe Oscar can. Sitting on the pitwall, so close to Tom and all their shared data. 

 

And maybe a few years ago, if they were in this situation, when he was so young and still trying to find his place, he would be worried more about Daniel. About the frosty reception and discomfort that followed them since last year. 

 

Instead, Lando knows he has to focus on himself. Championships aren’t made trying to ruin someone else’s race. They’re made trying to win your own. 

 

And he’s gonna try to give himself the best race possible. 

 

“Ok, last cars coming to the grid now,” Oscar says. 

 

“Copy,” Lando replies. He looks at Daniel ahead. He looks at Max behind in the mirrors. 

 

“You’ve got this, Lando,” Oscar says. 

 

Lando takes a deep breath and thinks about Oscar. Oscar, who deserves a championship. Deserves a win tonight. 

 

The lights go out. 

 

Lando’s ready for a fight. 

----

 

Oscar holds his breath as the lights go out. 

 

Daniel gets a good start, Lando close on his heels. 

 

He watches as Lando draws closer and closer. 

 

He needs to pass. Has to or Daniel will keep the pit priority. 

 

He’s about to get on the radio, give Lando more data. More ways he can try taking the corner. 

 

Then he watches as Hulkenberg hits Gasly. 

 

They’re gonna bring out the safety car. 

 

On lap 7. 

 

The perfect pit window. 

 

They’d only have to pit once if they came in. Lando would be behind Daniel, but he has 25 laps to work his way back up easily on better, fresher tyres. 

 

He looks over at Andrea and Will. They’re talking. 

 

“We should pit,” Oscar says urgently. 

 

“Agreed,” Tom says. “Daniel can keep the lead if we’re quick.”

 

“No,” Andrea says, shaking his head. “It’ll be too messy and chaotic. We’re better staying out.” 

 

“What?” Tom asks. “We’ll have to make a second stop that everyone else gets for free. Oscar’s right, we should be pit.”

 

For once, Oscar’s grateful to have Tom here with him.

 

Both Andrea and Will look uncertain. 

 

“We can’t be the only team that won’t pit,” Will insists. “Other teams won’t want to get the order mixed up in the chaos.”

 

“That’s idiotic,” Oscar says, automatically. The safety car is slowing everyone down, but they’ll have to make a decision soon enough. Andrea frowns at him. “Sorry, but we’d be brain-dead to not take the free stop.”

 

Andrea bites his lip. “It wouldn’t be fair to the second car. Lando would tumble down the order. And Daniel could easily lose the position at the front in the chaos. We’d be putting ourselves in danger of losing to Verstappen.” 

 

Tom scoffs next to Oscar.

 

“I don’t care about being second car,” Oscar says, urgent as Daniel leads the pack closer and closer to them. Other pit crews are getting tyres ready. Their own pit crews wait, uncertain. “Lando can fight his way back up. We’d be fools not to take the free stop.” 

 

“We can fight better if we hold position,” Andrea says. He looks at Tom and Oscar.

 

Oscar looks at the tracker. They’re coming closer and closer. 

 

“Osc,” Lando’s voice crackles over the radio. “We should pit under the safety car, no?” 

 

“I’m checking,” Oscar says, looking at his bosses. 

 

“Checking what?” Lando asks. Oscar ignores him for the moment. He looks at Andrea. 

 

“We don’t pit,” he says. “We’ll have more options if we stay out.”

 

Oscar and Tom look at each other. 

 

“Relay the order to your drivers, please,” Andrea says firmly. 

 

Tom sighs and relays the order to Daniel. Oscar does the same. 

 

Stupid. 

 

They’re so stupid. 

 

Anxiety twists up in Oscar’s gut, mixing with anger and disbelief. Filling him so full, he might choke on it. 

 

“Why would we do that?” Lando demands over the radio. “Everyone else is gonna get a free stop. I can manage the tryes for a one stop.” 

 

Oscar watches the broadcast. They’re still recovering Hulkenberg's car.

 

Oscar’s stomach clenches with indecision. They could still do the pit stop. It could set Lando back. But it would be better than doing it later. Oscar looks over Lando’s times. He’s good, but his pace isn’t outstanding compared to Verstappen or Daniel. There’s no way either of them can get a big enough gap to make the second stop negligible. 

 

“Do you think you could work your way back up?” Oscar asks. “If the pit stop puts us in the middle of the pack?” 

 

Lando’s silent for a moment. Oscar watches his dot on the map. He’s more than halfway around. 

 

“Yeah,” Lando says, easily. “I could do it.” 

 

“Ok,” Oscar says. He looks at Andrea and Will. He takes a deep breath. “RC.” 

 

“You sure?” Lando asks. God, Oscar wishes he could see Lando’s face right now. Know if he gets what Oscar’s suggesting. Know if Lando agrees with his decision wholeheartedly. 

 

“I’m sure,” Oscar says. “Please, Lando. RC.” 

 

There’s a beat. 

 

“RC,” Lando agrees. 

 

Oscar looks over his bosses. “I’m sorry, but I have to do this.” 

 

Andrea and Will look at him. They don’t argue, but they both wear weary looks of disappointment and frustration. 

 

They look resigned. 

 

Tom looks at him slack-jawed as Daniel drives past the pit entrance. 

 

Oscar puts in the order to the pit crew. 

 

“Ok, Lando,” Oscar says. “Box this lap. Box this lap.” 

 

Lando’s coming in even before Oscar’s done speaking. 

 

Daniel continues around the track as Lando and the rest of the grid come into the pits. 

 

Oscar hopes the lashing he gets from the team is nothing compared to the championship he’s trying to win Lando tonight. 

 

----

 

It’s not the best second place Lando’s ever gotten, but it feels like relief crossing the line. 

 

“Ok, Lando,” Oscar says. “That’s P2. Really good job, mate.” 

 

Lando’s nose wrinkles under his helmet. Mate. God, he hates the word from Oscar’s mouth sometimes. Can’t not hate it when he knows what it’s like to have Oscar call him by much sweeter names. 

 

Baby. 

 

Darling. 

 

Lando yearns for them, even as he parks his car in parc ferme. Oscar’s waiting for him, behind the fence, chatting with some of his mechanics. 

 

He looks good. Wind swept hair. Chisled jaw. Cute, little bunny smile as he watches Lando get out of the car. 

 

He looks in good spirits. Hope he is, though Lando didn’t manage to wrap it up tonight. Knows Oscar’s probably in for a fight with the team during debrief. Even if Lando secured them a podium. Didn’t make them look like complete fools tonight. 

 

He knows everyone online is gonna say they favored him. That they purposely fucked over Daniel, even as he secured his own P3 after a good drive. 

 

Lando doesn’t give a fuck. He just cares that he’s that much closer to the title. That 18 points is a hefty lead to have over Max. 

 

 That he and Oscar are so in sync. 

 

That their silly little codeword actually worked for them. 

 

Lando rushes through the weighing and tears off his helmet and balaclava. He tugs off his gloves. 

 

He strides over to Oscar, a smiling blossoming on his lips. 

 

“Hi,” he says. Oscar smiles back at him. Something warm and fond. It makes Lando feel giddy. His mechanics laugh at them. They’re so fuckng obvious, and Lando doesn’t care. 

 

He’d kiss Oscar on broadcast right now if they weren’t being filmed for the whole world. 

 

“Congrats,” Oscar says. He reaches out and squeezes Lando’s hand. Something akin to a handshake, but secretly makes Lando’s skin tingle. “Sorry, it goes down to the final race again.” 

 

“Whatever,” Lando says, rolling his eyes playfully. “I can do one more race.” 

 

“Yeah,” Oscar says, smiling. “I know you can.” 

 

“Thank you for pushing,” Lando says quietly. “I know you’re gonna get shit for it. But it was the right call.” 

 

“Fuck 'em,” Oscar says. “My job’s to help get the best race for you. For the team. And I did. Sometimes you gotta fight a little.” 

 

“Oscar Piastri,” Lando says, shaking his head. “Look at you, all bold and defying orders. What’s happened to my nerdy, goody two-shoes race engineer?” 

 

“You,” Oscar says. Lando ignores how a simple word lights him up. He’s hot and flushed already after two hours racing in the warm Lusail night. Oscar doesn’t help. He flushes deeper and rubs at the back of his neck. 

 

“You’re gonna get me all riled up, Piastri,” Lando says. 

 

“I probably shouldn’t tell you how good you are, then?” 

 

“Jesus,” Lando says. He wouldn’t be surprised if his tongue is lolled out like a dumb puppy dog. 

 

Oscar chortles. “Go get interviewed before you do something stupid.” 

 

Lando feels like he’s on cloud nine. Like he’s got a celebration ahead of him. 

 

He tears away from Oscar’s smile. From the way his eyes look at Lando like that.

 

Heart eyes or whatever Alex said. 

 

Lando’s not gonna watch the broadcast back or go on social media tonight. 

 

Cause whatever look Oscar is giving him, Lando must be returning a thousand times over. 

 

----

 

Oscar waits in Lando’s driver’s room. 

 

He sits on the couch and taps his fingers along his water bottle. 

 

He’d gotten a decent lashing from Zak and Andrea for not following orders. But it’d been hard to make Oscar feel too bad when they’d rewatched, as every other team came into the pits. They would have looked unbearably stupid keeping both cars out and not splitting the strategies. Plus, Daniel was able to build a decent gap in the end. Nothing to recover his lead, but enough for a podium. 

 

And now it was a two-horse race again.

 

Oscar wasn’t optimistic that they still wouldn’t try to do some equality bullshit in Abu Dhabi. But, at least Oscar only had one rival to worry about. Even if that was Max fucking Verstappen. 

 

Daniel could try dirty tricks or whatever if he wanted. Oscar wasn’t afraid anymore. 

 

Wasn’t worried about him. Or trying to play the team game. 

 

The team game was now to make Lando a World Champion again. 

 

He sighs. He does feel a bit bad. Like a child having a tantrum. 

 

It’s just…not typical of Oscar to display so much emotion. To push back like this. To take a stance. He’s spent so long trying to prove himself that he didn't realize that maybe pushing back was proving himself. 

 

Or maybe having motivation as good as Lando was what he’d needed. 

 

“Oh, what’s this in my room?” 

 

Oscar looks up to see Lando sliding into the room. His race suit is rolled down and tied around his hips. His slight, hourglass waist on full display. The beginning curve of his ass just peeking out the top. Oscar’s hands twitch. 

 

“Hello,” Oscar says. He stands and finds them immediately closing the distance. 

 

Lando exhales and slumps into his body as soon as Oscar pulls him into a hug. 

 

“Too much media,” Lando mumbles into Oscar’s shoulder. “I’m seconds away from passing out.” 

 

Oscar laughs. “Are you flying out tonight?” 

 

“Yeah,” Lando nods. “I’m supposed to go golfing with Zak. And there’s some sponsorship bullshit on Tuesday.” 

 

“Shame,” Oscar says, rubbing Lando’s back. “I was hoping to keep the championship leader for the night.” 

 

Lando squirms in his hold until he can lean back and look up at Oscar. His large, warm hands soothing down Oscar’s biceps.  

 

“Oscah,” Lando bemoans. “You can’t say stuff like that. You’re cockblocking me again. I’m gonna have a stiffy on the jet.” 

 

“You’re ridiculous,” Oscar scoffs. “You and your penis and your private planes.” 

 

“You could come with me?” Lando suggests. “There’s room.” 

 

“I have a lovely ticket on a commercial flight,” Oscar says. “It’s better for the environment.” 

 

“If you cared about the environment, you wouldn’t work in this sport,” Lando argues. “Did they chew you out badly? Is that why you’re trying to give me blue balls?” 

 

“No,” Oscar says. “I have actual work to do. Besides, we’re already obvious enough. Let’s not make it worse by flying around and shacking up together.” 

 

“Aw, are you ashamed of me, Osc?” Lando asks. 

 

Oscar shakes his head. “Of course not. I’m proud of you. I know you wanna help the team, but I appreciate you listening to me. Doing something for yourself.” 

 

“Ah, my dick,” Lando moans, loud and exaggerated. “Oscar, stop, or I’m gonna come.” 

 

“Someone’s gonna come in here,” Oscar warns, rolling his eyes. “I’m serious. I know it’s probably not easy to defy orders. But you stood your ground. You pushed back, and we got something for it.” 

 

“It wasn't easy,” Lando admits. “But I’m working on the balance. I do wanna help the team. I can’t help it. I owe McLaren my whole career. But I guess there’s a balance. A good driver can help their team and do things for themself.” 

 

“They can,” Oscar agrees. “We’ll keep working on it.” 

 

“Together?” Lando asks. 

 

“Together,” Oscar says. 

 

Lando sighs. “I can’t wait for this fucking season to be over. I just want to relax and not think about racing for like a whole ass month. I just wanna spend time with you. Outside of all of this.” 

 

“I want that too,” Oscar says, soft and quiet like a confession. Maybe it is. Even though he and Lando have been mostly on the same page, there hasn’t been time for them to be together without something getting in their way. Without time limits. 

 

Just stolen moments. 

 

It’s not enough. Never has been.  Probably never will be. 

 

“When this is over,” Oscar says. “Can I take you out on a date? Like a real, actual date.” 

 

Lando’s eyes widen, and a pleased flush settles over his cheeks. 

 

“We’ve been on dates,” Lando says. ‘Kinda.” 

 

“Forced teambuilding is not a date,” Oscar says. “I mean a real, proper date. Like dinner and stuff.” 

 

“Hm, and stuff,” Lando murmurs. “Yeah, take me on a date.”

 

Lando leans up and presses his lips against Oscar’s. It’s fleeting. Not nearly enough of what either of them wants. 

 

“You’re mine after Abu Dhabi,” Oscar promises. 

 

“Stupid,” Lando says. “I already am.” 

 

----

 

Lando finds Daniel sitting in an empty, dark corner. After Oscar had left to go back to the hotel, Lando knocked on Daniel’s door, but got no response. 

 

Maybe it’s stupid. Or cruel. 

 

But Lando doesn’t think he can just leave. Jet off to a two-man fight and not talk to Daniel. 

 

They used to be friends. Some days, Daniel had been the only bright spot in the paddock for the past few years. Especially once Carlos found himself settled at Ferrai and growing closer to Charles. 

 

And Lando was just someone who enjoyed having a good relationship with the people he worked with. He wanted to come to work and get along with everyone. Start the day with a chat. 

 

It’s just that Lando’s championship put a divide between them. Cemented everyone’s opinion that Daniel was washed. That he couldn’t beat his younger teammate, even in a good car. It was Max and Redbull all over again. 

 

Lando had been foolish enough to believe they were different. Their friendship was different. Even as much as Max had tried to get through to him. 

 

“It’s Daniel’s issue, not yours,” he’d told him on a shared flight to Spain. “He comes around. He has emotions just like the rest of us. He just needs space to get over them.” 

 

There was no space on this team. Which was the problem. 

 

And Daniel had been closer to the title than ever. Everyone so sure he was gonna get it this time until Lando came in and worked his way back. 

 

Oscar would probably say that there’s no reason to feel guilty when he’s just been doing his job. To move on and not badger Daniel. 

 

Well, Oscar had avoided speaking to Daniel directly ever since their crash in Austin. 

 

It's just that Lando can't do that. Can't not feel guilty. Or try to speak to him one more time. 

 

So, he begs Daniel’s trainer to tell him where he is. 

 

“Hey, man,” he says as he approaches Daniel, slumped against a fence, still dressed in his fireproofs. “I’m sorry about today.”

 

Daniel looks up at him. His face carefully blank. None of the usual joy to be found. He looks exhausted. 

 

“Come to rub it in then?” Daniel asks, rubbing at the back of his neck. He sounds bitter. He sounds done. 

 

“No,” Lando insists. “I mean it. It’s been a good season, Daniel. You pushed me every step of the way.” 

 

“And it still wasn't enough,” he says.

 

Lando feels like he’s walking into a maze of a conversation. 

 

“You'll have other chances, Daniel,” Lando says. “You're meant to be a champion. I know it.”

 

Daniel sighs and looks away. Lando cringes internally. He’s sure Daniel’s tired of hearing that. How everyone thinks he’s a champion in the making. It’s been years now, and he still isn’t one. 

 

“What do you really want, Lando?” Daniel asks. He looks at Lando. His eyes are red rimmed with heavy bags underneath. 

 

What does he want? 

 

To console Daniel? To make him not feel like his dreams have been dashed tonight?

 

Selfishly, Lando just wants to know he’s ok. That they’re ok. That there’s still some remnant of their friendship around to salvage. 

 

“I want to go back to how we were,” Lando confesses. “We used to have fun. We used to be teammates. Friends, even. I don’t like being title rivals like this.”

 

“In this sport, we’ll always be teammates and rivals,” Daniel says. “It's how it is, Lando. You’re too old to be this ignorant. To be worried about friendships.”  

 

“We can put the season behind us,” Lando tries. “We can go back ot how we were. I mean, we don't even know if we'll be good with the new regs. We may be back to just fighting for points again.” 

 

Daniel sighs and stands up. 

 

“Exactly,” Daniel says. “We don't know, and this might have been my final chance.” 

 

“That's not true,” Lando says, even as he feels the same uncertainty. All the confusion and discussion for 2026 has been a lot. Scares him a little if he’s honest. 

 

“It is,” Daniel says. “And you're foolish to think we were ever gonna come out of this better than we were. This is racing, Lando. You can’t go head to head like we did. Have the team flight like we did and just go back to how we were. We are not the same midfield team anymore. I can't let you just beat me again and again and then go back to being best buddies again.”

 

The words aren’t surprising. But they still hurt. Still make Lando feel like a little boy. The one that Daniel undoubtedly regards him as. 

 

“What do you want me to do then?” Lando asks. “Not beat you? Let you win?”

 

“I don't expect you to do anything,” Daniel says resignedly. “This is just life. This is how Formula 1 works. We should just be grateful that this fight didn’t tear us apart more. That I can even look and talk to you. That I don't hate you so much that I want to hit you.” 

 

“You impeded me on purpose,” Lando says. A statement, not a question. 

 

Daniel shrugs. “I had to try until the end, didn’t I?” 

 

He speaks so clear and firm. No hint of regret. 

 

Lando swallows. He feels a heavy sadness sink into his stomach and settle cold and heavy in his bones. It's not like he expected Daniel would be celebrating with him. But they were out of the car. Out of the fight. 

 

There were bruises and mistakes on both sides. Lando can own up to it. He can race and leave it on the track. He can see Daniel as someone to race with and against. He can fight clean. 

 

But he understands now that that isn't the case with Daniel. 

 

He wracks his brain. Thinks if Carlos were in Daniel's place. 

 

Would he feel the same? Would he and Lando have drifted apart, friendship torn up by a title fight?

 

He thinks about Oscar. 

 

What if Oscar hadn’t gotten injured? If he was still racing. If he were Lando’s teammate. 

 

Would they be like this?

 

It’s hard for Lando to imagine. Hard for him to see a world where he doesn’t want to see Oscar shine. To have Oscar challenge him. To make him a better driver. To fight tooth and nail for what he cares about. 

 

Would Oscar hate him in this moment as Daniel does? 

 

Or would he and Oscar be able to stand side by side, knowing they made each other better drivers? 

 

He laughs to himself a bit. With the way they fought at the beginning of the season? They would have torn each other's throats out. He can’t even imagine the type of fights they might have gotten into. 

 

Still, he wonders if they would have found each other in that way, too? 

 

Would he still love Oscar if they were rivals? 

 

Would Oscar still want him if Lando were stealing wins from him?

 

Lando briefly envisions standing on a podium next to Oscar. He probably looks resplendent covered in champagne and confetti. 

 

Yeah, maybe they would find each other in every universe. 

 

He clears the thought from his mind. Luckily or unluckily, he’ll never know. 

 

“I'm sorry,” Lando says finally. “I think the world of you, Daniel. And you will keep winning. You’re meant to be a driver. And a winner.” 

 

It’s the truth. Lando’s truth. Daniel can hate him. Or lose respect for him. 

 

But Lando can’t stop how he feels. How much respect he can have for someone. Anyone willing to get into these cars and try their best until the end. 

 

Daniel sighs, shaking his head. “Thanks, Lando. At least, you won't have to worry about fighting me for wins now.” 

 

“What do you mean?” Lando asks. 

 

“Zak and I worked out an agreement,” Daniel says. “I can't be here anymore. I don’t belong here. Not like you do. I don’t think I ever have.”

 

“That’s not true,” Lando tries. 

 

“It is,” Daniel says. “I know where I belong. But I ran away and ended up in the same position here.”

 

“So you’re leaving?” Lando asks. “For good? Like retiring?” 

 

Daniel shakes his head. “They’re doing a trade. Me and Bortoleto. They wanna see Gabi back at McLaren. And I need to try something new. I hate racing sometimes. Especially here.” 

 

The news hits hard and unexpectedly. Lando loves Gabi, but he didn’t imagine him coming back to McLaren, though he was another driver development kid. He thought Daniel would stay. Knowing they can build championship cars. That he can lead a championship. 

 

There are so many questions in Lando’s head. 

 

“I don't wanna hate racing anymore,” Daniel says. “If that means being on a midfield team where I can just drive and not feel like I'm fighting for my life. For my worth every race, then that's what I need to do.” 

 

“I’m sorry,” Lando says again. “I’ve never wanted you to leave.” 

 

“I know,” Daniel sighs. “It's just not meant to be. Sides, I look shit in orange anyways.” 

 

He offers Lando a big, lopsided smile. He moves forward and claps Lando on the shoulder. 

 

“I’ll miss you,” Lando says honestly. “Maybe that’s stupid and childish, but it's true.” 

 

Daniel laughs, low and sincere. 

 

“I’ve already been missing us, Lando,” Daniel says. “In a few years, I can look at you again and not feel so disappointed in myself. Give me some time.”

 

“Ok,” Lando nods, feeling just a bit lost. 

 

“Aight,” Daniel says. “And I hope you’ll give it your all to Max next weekend.” 

 

“You’ll be cheering for me?” Lando asks.

 

“Fuck no,” Daniel says, laughing. “I’m gonna try and beat your ass one more time. Plus gotta cheer for the first husband, don't I? Max Verstappen 4 time champ for sure.” 

 

Lando laughs even as he rolls his eyes. 

 

For a moment, it feels like it's back to normal. 

 

Until a sad, glassy look reflects in Daniel's eyes. 

 

He sighs. 

 

“See ya on Thursday, mate,” Daniel says and walks away. 

 

A sort of relief and crushing sadness fills Lando’s chest. 

 

In truth, maybe Lando said goodbye to their friendship a long time ago. The minute he crossed the line in Abu Dhabi last year, probably. 

 

But it feels realer here. With rose water clinging to his curls and the sounds of Daniel’s steps fading. 

 

He sits there for a long time. Letting the sound of the race weekend fade. 

 

When he feels cold and numb enough, he stands and finds a car to take him back to the hotel. 

 

----

 

It’s not that Lando is guaranteed to win. 

 

But Oscar feels like their chances are good. 

 

Better than good. 

 

And while Lando might take things race by race. 

 

Oscar’s got a championship to secure. 

 

A champion to love. 

 

He takes a deep breath. 

 

A thought has been bubbling under the surface since Brazil. Itching at his brain and making him feel a little crazy. 

 

He wants to tell Lando he loves him.

 

No, he’s definitely going to. 

 

When he’s won. 

 

When it’s all said and done. 

 

Oscar’s gonna be honest. He’s gonna talk to Lando. He’s gonna stop this endless back and forth and just let them be them. 

 

He’s gonna love his champion to the ends of the earth. Going to make sure that he and Lando aren’t just tiptoeing around how they feel about each other. Not when they’re shifting far away from just flirting and attraction and getting into sex and real feelings. 

 

So, yeah. He’s gonna make Lando his for real. 

 

Even with the stupid little slit in his eyebrow that he’s shown up to the track with. 

 

“What is that?’ Oscar asks as they meet before qualifying. He points at the cut in Lando’s brow. 

 

Lando, who has the audacity to blush a bit. Faded against his tan skin, but there nonetheless. 

 

“I was trying to like shape them,” Lando says. “The razor slipped in my hand.”

 

“I think some people's whole jobs are to do brows and stuff,” Oscar says, laughing. 

 

“Ok, but it wasn’t like I needed a whole new shape,” Lando says. “Just a bit of a trim. Do you not like it?”

 

Truthfully? Oscar does kinda like it. It’s hot. The little slit in his brow. 

 

Makes him look like a fuckboy. Like a player. Like everything, Oscar is coming to find out he isn’t.

 

Like, he wants to play at danger when he really just wants to be Oscar’s good fucking boy. 

 

He rolls his eyes. “No. You look like a douchebag.” 

 

Lando scoffs. “Please. I might be unc or whatever, but even I know this looks good.” 

 

“What is unc?” Oscar asks. 

 

“Jesus, babe,” Lando says, rolling his eyes. “You’re unc.” 

 

“Thanks,” Oscar says blandly. Ignoring how the little pet name makes him feel giddy. “You’re unc too.” 

 

“No, Oscar,” Lando whines. “It’s bad.” 

 

“I’m assuming it means uncool or something like that,” Oscar says. 

 

“Actually, yeah,” Lando says, surprised. “How’d you know?” 

 

“I took an educated guess,” He reasons. 

 

“Hm,” Lando says. “Do you know what chopped is?” 

 

“Yeah, it’s whatever your brows are,” Oscar says, lifting one of his own. 

 

Lando makes a displeased noise.

 

“You’re so mean to me,” Lando says. “You like the slit.”

 

Yeah, Oscar does. 

 

“Sure,” Oscar says, hoping not to give away his interest in it at all. “Wanna talk tyre temps?” 

 

“Always,” Lando says, grinning at him. 

 

“Liar,” Oscar says. “Are you nervous?” 

 

They haven’t seen each other much. Since it’s the last race of the season, Lando’s been caught up with media and sponsorship obligations. He’s been trapped in interview after interview. And Oscar feels a certain responsibility for Lando. He needs food and sleep and rest. Focus. 

 

Not Oscar hovering around him and just yearning to be in his presence. Lando’s own wondering hands and Oscar’s unabashed need for him could only lead to trouble. Distraction. 

 

So they’ve only seen each other at the track, during pre-scheduled meetings. Oscar’s hardly had time to really talk to Lando outside the radio. He’s been busy as much as he’s been missing him. 

 

Lando hums to himself for a moment. He scratches at his chin, freshly shaven. He must have hit the goatee and the eyebrows all at once. 

 

“I don’t feel as nervous as I have before,” Lando says. “Like last year, I had to win. This year, I just wanna.” 

 

“That’s not a terrible way to think of it,” Oscar says. “You don’t even have to podium to win the title.”

 

“I know,” Lando says. “But I want to be up there. On the top step.” 

 

“You will be,” Oscar promises. He looks around. They’re in a meeting room, alone. But the walls are glass, and anyone could see them talking. Frankly, Oscar’s surprised no one’s come in and bothered either of them for something. 

 

Still, it looks like the upstairs level of hospitality is mostly empty at this time of the day. 

 

Oscar leans forward and cups Lando’s face. He traces his hand over Lando’s cheek, feels the smooth, tan skin there. He smiles at him. 

 

“You’re my perfect champion,” Oscar says. “And you’re gonna leave here a winner.” 

 

Those gorgeous jade eyes look at him. 

 

Oscar kisses him. Soft and sweet. Not nearly enough. 

 

But enough to say what he feels. That he believes in Lando. That everything they’ve worked for ends here. That Oscar loves him. 

 

Lando chases after him when they part. 

 

“More,” he demands. 

 

Oscar chuckles as he lets go of Lando. “That’s already more than I should be giving you while we’re at work.” 

 

Lando scoffs.” Oh, sure. He can blow me in my driver’s room, but a few kisses is where he draws the line. Absolute insanity, babe.” 

 

“I shouldn’t have done that either,” Oscar points out. Definitely should not have done that at work. How wildly irresponsible of him. Not that he’d have been able to stop himself from having Lando in that moment anyway. 

 

It should be studied what Lando winning does to Oscar. 

 

“Right, I’m just irresistible,” Lando says. He grins at Oscar. Bright and heart-shaped. 

 

“No, you’re annoying,” Oscar says. “Now, stop distracting me. We haven’t talked about pit strategy yet.” 

 

“Christ,” Lando says, scrunching his face up in faux annoyance. “What gets you harder, me or the fucking data?” 

 

Yeah, Oscar’s keeping him forever. 

 

----

 

In the end, it’s not all that climactic. 

 

The beginning bit is good. Lando has a good start for P2. He takes the lead right away. And he’s able to keep Max back enough to build pace. To completely tear away from the pack. 

 

After that, it’s a bit boring. 

 

So, Lando just thinks. 

 

His mind goes all over the place. 

 

He spends a good part of it worrying that his car is gonna break down again. That it would be just his luck for the engine to fail or the plank to wear too much again. 

 

But Oscar’s nearly silent on the radio. 

 

Except for when he’s come on after Lando’s start.

 

“Amazing start,” Oscar had said. “You’re doing so well, Lando.”

 

It took everything in him not to moan on the radio. Oscar’s evil. Knowing how good his praise is. How wild it drives Lando. 

 

How pent up he is.

 

Christ, he really might come in the car. 

 

He pulls his focus back onto the road. 

 

“Let’s keep up the pace,” Oscar says, after he completes his pit stop. “Build a gap and then we’ve got just 24 racing laps left.”

 

“Yeah, copy,” Lando says, keeping his eyes ahead. Max is far enough behind him that Lando lets his mind wander a bit. Let’s the weight of the season start to lift off his shoulders. 

 

He feels a bit verklempt suddenly. Maybe it’s the end of season exhaustion catching up to him. Or maybe it’s the ticking down of the laps. 

 

Or maybe it’s just Oscar in his ear. 

 

I did it, he thinks as the end of the race draws closer and closer. We did it.

 

The whole season flashes before his eyes. He thinks of sitting sour and tightly wound in Oscar’s office. Of all the moments they spent getting to know each other through arguments and disagreements. 

 

He thinks of how beautiful Oscar looked when he truly smiled at Lando the first. When he smiled because of Lando. 

 

He thinks of the anger he felt at the beginning of the season. How it consumed him. Lit him up quick like a match. Thinks of how Oscar calms him. Holds him like he’s something precious, and all those silly worries seem to slip away. 

 

He’s so different. Oscar’s so different. 

 

And they’re both better. For each other. Because of each other. 

 

Lando wants to press the radio button and just let his mouth open and talk and talk and talk for the rest of the race. Wants to tell Oscar how much he’s helped him. How he’s built this to be two-time world champion. 

 

How Lando wants to spend the rest of his career with Oscar in his ear. 

 

How much he yearns to be with him. 

 

How he misses him even when they’re in the same room. 

 

How much he loves him. 

 

Shit.

 

Lando’s foot presses down further. 

 

Fuck these tyres. Lando needs to get out of this car and kiss Oscar breathless. 

 

Tell him just how strongly he feels. 

 

But he should be good. 

 

He should be the driver Oscar knows him to be. 

 

“Ok, just two racing laps to go,” Oscar says. “You’ve got this.” 

 

He does. 

 

He’s gonna win this fucking race and this championship. 

 

----

 

It’s a completely different feeling from a year ago to watch Lando cross the line a winner. 

 

Before, he sat jaded and despondent on Daniel’s side. He’d watched with unabashed jealousy as Will and Lando’s side celebrated the win. The title. 

 

Then, he’d just wished he was on the winning side. That Daniel didn’t hate him and that he could be good enough at his job to make his driver a winner like Lando was. 

 

Now, being on Lando’s side, he felt something akin to pure, unadulterated joy. 

 

Not for himself. Not that he’d finally been on the winning side. Or that he was free from Daniel. 

 

No, just because he wants to see Lando win. Was just happy for him. Proud of him. 

 

Lando deserved his win. His second title. 

 

And Oscar felt lucky enough to just be a part of his story. 

 

He can’t even help the way the emotion surfaces in him. His voice cracks, wracked with emotion as he clicks on Lando’s radio. 

 

“That’s it, Lando,” Oscar says, face split in a cheek-numbing grin. “You’re a two-time world champion. Back to Back. Congrats, mate.” 

 

He’s sure Lando’s upset at being called mate, but Oscar hardly thinks it’s appropriate to call him baby over the radio. Even as much as he wants to.

 

Lando responds with a loud, jubilant roar.  

 

“Holy shit, Oscar,” Lando says. “Thank you. Thank you. Thank you.” 

 

Oscar laughs. He rubs his eyes. His hands come away wet with tears. 

 

“No, thank you,” Oscar says. “You were so good, Lando. Perfect fucking race.” 

 

“Oscar, stop,” Lando whines over the radio. 

 

Lando takes a moment. Oscar can hear him catching his breath as he drives. He watches through the onboard as Lando wipes at his eyes under his helmet. The sight of it is enough to set Oscar over the edge again. He chokes back his own emotion. 

 

“Congrats,” Lando says finally. “Thank you, Oscar. I-I love you…” 

 

There’s another bit of silence for a moment before Lando speaks again. Like he’s realized what he’s just said. “All. Ah, I love you all. Thank you to the team. All the boys and girls at the factory and here at the track. Congrats, everyone.” 

 

Oscar hides his smile into his shoulder. It’s stupid, but there’s something about the way Lando says it that Oscar just knows it's for him. 

 

Of course, Lando wants to be the one to say he loves him first. 

 

Annoying.
